The earlier fix only caught two of three occurrences of this pattern because this one used a different local variable name (`p` instead of `payload`) so the text didn't match. **This was very likely the actual cause of vendor assignment continuing to fail even after the Lookup fix.** Fixed now — verified no remaining references to `formatVendorForSave` anywhere in the file.

Added a new hidden `Vendor_Emails` field (comma-separated, not shown in this widget's own UI) that auto-populates whenever `Vendors1` is saved — resolved from `vendors_Report` via a new `email` entry in `CONFIG.vendorFieldCandidates`, using the existing `vendorInfo()`/`OPTIONS.vendors` cache (new `vendorEmailsFor()` helper). Wired into both places `Vendors1` actually gets saved: `buildPayload()` (the main wizard Save) and `saveFieldEditor()` (Final Closure's click-to-edit popup).

### 2026-08-03 (yet later) — Real bug fixed: Phone Number blank on reopen; still investigating Remarks

User confirmed the "RSID" column on `Agent_Ticket_Report` is just a display label for `Case_ID` — no code change needed, unrelated to `Invites.RSID`.

**Phone Number bug, found and fixed**: the Create step's own box reads/writes `Phone_Number1`, mirrored into the real required `Phone_Number` field only on save (via `extra()`). Any ticket where `Phone_Number1` itself was never set (created outside this widget) has a real `Phone_Number` but blank `Phone_Number1` — so reopening such a ticket showed an empty box despite the record clearly having a phone number, confirmed via a side-by-side native-record vs. widget comparison. Fixed: `openTicket()` now falls back to `Phone_Number` when `Phone_Number1` is empty.

**Remarks still blank on reopen, cause not yet found**: same side-by-side comparison showed a real Zoho record with actual timestamped Remarks entries (matching this widget's own append-only format, so the save clearly worked) but the widget showed "No remarks yet." on reopen. Both save and read use the same `Remarks` field name in code, so this isn't the same class of bug as Phone_Number — added a new targeted console log (`openTicket key fields — ... Remarks:`) to see on the next test whether the fetched record actually has `Remarks` populated or not, which will determine whether this is a field-naming issue (same class as RSID/Case_ID) or a display bug in the `t:"remarks"` rendering.

### 2026-08-03 (later still) — Real bug fixed: the whole agent dashboard showed 0 tickets in every bucket

User-reported live, with console proof: `apiGet` was correctly fetching all 3 real tickets from `Agent_Ticket_Report` (`code: 3000, records: 3`), but every one of the dashboard's four bucket sections still showed "No tickets right now." Root cause: `"RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)"` — the status every ticket sits at right after the agent invites vendors — was missing from **every single bucket's own `statuses` list** in `BUCKETS`. `bucketOfStatus()` returned `-1` for it, and `renderReportLists()`'s own `if(b>=0) cols[b].push(r)` silently dropped any ticket at that status from the dashboard entirely — invisible, not just miscategorized. Added it to the "Ready & Invited" bucket (its natural home, alongside `READY FOR ASSIGNMENT`/`RSP REJECT`). Also removed the stale `RSP COMPLETE` from that same bucket while touching it — already confirmed superseded by `RSP CLOSED` earlier this session.

### 2026-08-03 (later) — New "Invites" architecture: one record per invited vendor, created on Assignment save

Reverses the earlier 2026-07-31 decision to use `Create_Case` only. Per the user's confirmed answers: a separate `Invites` form (report `Invites_Report`) now gets one record per invited vendor per ticket — `RSID` (Lookup → this `Create_Case` record) and `Vendor` (Lookup → whoever's invited). New `syncInvites(recordId, vendorNames)` is called right after a successful Assignment-step save (`saveStep()`), reusing the same `OPTIONS.vendors` cache `Vendors1`'s own picker uses to resolve each vendor's real ID (`vendorIdByName()`). Duplicate-safe: fetches whichever vendors already have an `Invites` record for this ticket first (best-effort criteria match on `RSID`), only creates new ones for the rest — re-saving the Assignment step without changing `Vendors1` won't spam duplicate invites.

Both `RSID` and `Vendor` are sent as bare IDs, not wrapped objects — consistent with the Lookup write-shape revert earlier this session. See `QUESTIONS_TO_ASK.md` for what's confirmed vs. still assumed about this new mechanism (the criteria-string syntax used to check for existing invites in particular is best-effort, not independently verified).

### 2026-08-03 — Real bug fixed: Tab key jumping back to the first field; dashboard "Paid" flag tightened; Final Closure made collapsible

**Root cause found for the reported "pressing Tab jumps back to the top of the form" bug**: several field types (`recompute`/`parseTarget` fields like `Total_Service_Fee`/`LatLongPaste`, the `button` branch, plain `select`/radio/searchable-combo changes) fire a full `renderWizard()`/`renderClosure()` rebuild from their own native `change` handler. The browser's `change` event for a text-like input fires on blur — which, for a Tab keypress, happens *after* the browser has already moved focus to the next field. Destroying and rebuilding the whole fields DOM at that exact moment also destroys the element focus had just landed on, silently dropping focus to nothing — so the *next* Tab press has nothing to advance from and restarts at the very first focusable element on the page. Fixed with a new `preservingFocus()` wrapper (used by `rerenderCurrentView()`, which every one of these handlers now routes through instead of calling `renderWizard()` directly): it remembers the just-focused element's id before the rebuild and refocuses the equivalent freshly-built element right after, so Tab continues normally on the next press.

This was on top of the earlier (2026-07-31) roving-tabindex fix for the custom Yes/No-style radio pickers themselves (which had no `tabindex` at all before that, so Tab silently skipped over them) — both together should make Tab behave like a normal form now.

**Dashboard "Paid" flag** (`isPaidFlag`): removed the "no booking fee also counts as paid" shortcut per explicit request — it now ticks only when `Payment_Status` is literally `"PAID"`.

**Final Closure sections are now collapsible**, same chevron-toggle pattern the dashboard's report buckets already use (`closureGroupHtml`) — click a group's header (Customer & Vehicle, Service Fee & Booking, etc.) to collapse/expand it; state persists across re-renders.

### 2026-08-03 (later) — Final Closure sections now default to collapsed, one at a time

Per explicit follow-up ("collapsible section-wise, not full at a time"): every Final Closure section now starts **collapsed** when the screen opens, instead of all-expanded — the agent clicks into just the section(s) they actually need. Renamed the tracking set from `COLLAPSED_CLOSURE_GROUPS` to `EXPANDED_CLOSURE_GROUPS` and inverted the membership check (a group is expanded only if explicitly opted in) so a not-yet-seen group title (e.g. a different Service_Type branch's section name) still starts collapsed by default rather than needing to be pre-seeded into a "collapsed" set. The dashboard's own report buckets are unaffected — those still default to all-expanded, since only Final Closure was called out this time.

**Investigating the reported "fields show empty when reopening a saved ticket" issue**: couldn't reproduce or root-cause this without live access — added diagnostic `console.log` calls at `openTicket()` (logs the raw record exactly as Zoho returned it, both before and after this widget's own Lookup-unwrapping) and at `prevStep()`/`jumpTo()` (logs `state.record` at the moment of navigating). If a field that was definitely filled in and saved shows up missing in the very first log line, its `n:` name in `STAGES` doesn't match the real Zoho API name — see `FIELDS.md`'s own ❓/🟡-marked fields for the likeliest suspects, since those were never independently confirmed against a live record. If it's present in that log but still renders blank on screen, the bug is in this widget's own display code instead, and the console output will help pin down exactly which. Confirmed by re-reading the save code that the append-only Remarks field (added 2026-07-31) does correctly get included in the save payload on both save paths (`buildPayload`/`saveFieldEditor`) — no fix needed there, it was already working as intended.

### 2026-07-31 (yet later again) — UI pass: Create-step layout, common append-only Remarks, collapsible dashboard reports, radio Tab-order

Several UI/UX fixes requested together:

- **Create step layout**: `Client`/`Client_Tracking_Id` and `Vehicle`/`Vehicle_Registration_Number` now each share one row (`row:` grouping, same mechanism `Customer_Name`/`Phone_Number1`/`Email` already used) instead of falling into whatever column the grid happened to put them in. `Client_Tracking_Id` is now hidden whenever `Client` is the special "ON DEMAND" walk-in entry (case-insensitive match), added `Client` to `RERENDER` so this updates live the moment it's picked, not just after a save. `Alternate_Phone_Number` marked `full:true` so it no longer accidentally shares a row with `Client`. The `.fld-row` grid now sizes its own column count to however many fields actually land in it (`--row-cols`), so a 2-field row like `Client`/`Tracking ID` splits 50/50 instead of the fixed 3-column layout leaving an empty slot.
- **Common append-only Remarks**: every `Remarks` field (Create, Quote, Locations, Assignment, Final Closure — all five are the exact same `Create_Case.Remarks`) changed from a plain freely-editable textarea to a new `t:"remarks"` type: past entries show read-only, and the agent can only add a new timestamped line (`fmtDateTime(new Date())+" — "+text`) — existing text can't be edited or deleted through this widget. Implemented as one shared field type (`buildField`/`wireField`'s own `"remarks"` branches) so all five stay in sync automatically; `collectStep`/`collectSingleField` both skip it (same treatment as multiselect) since `state.record` is already kept current by the Add button, not scraped from a DOM value. Final Closure's own summary row shows just the last entry as a one-line preview. **Not yet attributed to a specific agent** — this widget doesn't track the logged-in agent's own display name anywhere (only `technicianTicket`/`driverTicket`/`vendorTicket` do that, for their own profiles), so entries are timestamp-only for now.
- **Collapsible dashboard report sections**: each of the four bucket sections (New & Open / Ready & Invited / Work In Progress / RSP Closed) can now be collapsed by clicking its header (chevron rotates, list hides) — state persists across re-renders (refresh, opening/closing a ticket) via a `COLLAPSED_BUCKETS` set, so a collapsed section stays collapsed until the agent re-opens it. All start expanded.
- **Radio-button Tab order**: `.opt` divs (the custom Yes/No-style pickers) previously had no `tabindex` at all, so Tab silently skipped over every radio field entirely. Now uses a proper "roving tabindex" (only the selected option — or the first, if none picked — is a Tab stop), with Left/Right/Up/Down arrow keys moving *and selecting* within the group and Space/Enter selecting the focused option — the same behavior native radio buttons have. Everything else in this widget (native `<input>`/`<select>`/`<textarea>`/`<button>` elements) was already correctly tabbable.

**Not independently verified**: whether every step now fits on one screen without scrolling depends on the actual device/viewport — I can't render this widget in a live browser to confirm pixel-perfect. `quote`/`locations`/`assignment` already benefited from the existing 2-column grid (non-`full` fields pair up automatically); no further explicit row-grouping was added to those beyond what's described above. Payment and Final Closure were intentionally left scrollable per the user's own instruction. If a specific step still scrolls more than expected, flag which one and I'll adjust its specific field layout.

### 2026-08-03 (later) — All Zoho forms deleted and rebuilt from scratch: this widget is now the build spec's own reference implementation

The user deleted every Zoho form/report and asked for a complete build spec instead — every form/field/type/dropdown-value, plus report List/Detail view guidance. That's now `../FIELDS.md` (Parts 1–2) and `../ACCESS.md`, both fully rewritten. This widget's own code changed to match:

- **`CONFIG.vendorFieldCandidates` (a 6-field-deep multi-candidate guess for `vendors_Report`'s phone/priority/status/lat/lon/email) collapsed into single confirmed field names** (`CONFIG.vendorPhoneField` etc.) now that `Vendors_Report` is being built fresh — `vendorInfo()`/`firstRawValue()` simplified accordingly (renamed to `rawValue()`, one field lookup instead of an array scan).
- **`vendors_Report` and the separate `My_Availability_Vendor` form (used by `vendorTicket`'s own login/toggle) are unified into one `Vendors_Report`** — no real reason for a vendor to have two records across two forms. `CONFIG.optionReports.vendors` updated to point at it.

- **`Invites` gained a real `Technician` Lookup field** (→ `Technicians_Report`), separate from `Vendor` — fixes a genuine bug in `vendorTicket`'s fleet hand-off (it was writing a `Technicians_Report` ID into the `Vendor` field, which targets `Vendors_Report` — a Zoho Lookup can't point at two forms, so it could never have resolved). This widget's own `syncInvites()` is unaffected (it only ever writes `Vendor`, for vendor invites) but the CONFIG comment was updated to document the new field.
- **`ticketKanbanGetRescue` was brought back in sync** on the dead-field removals (`Assignment_Sent`, `Service_Request`/`1`, `Action_field`/`2` — it had fallen behind several of this widget's own earlier fixes and was still deriving ticket status from fields no field widget ever wrote) plus two lingering type mismatches (`Odometer_Reading`, `Handover_to_Number`) — see its own README if it gets one, or `FIELDS.md`'s own notes.

### 2026-08-04 — Five real bugs/requests fixed: 24-hour Service_Time, Remarks author attribution, a Remarks-blank-on-reopen hardening, a new SCHEDULED status, and the Tab-jumps-to-top bug for Issue/Later

- **`Service_Time` widened to the full 24-hour day.** Was hardcoded to half-hour slots between 9:00 AM and 5:00 PM only — a customer scheduling an evening/overnight breakdown had no valid slot to pick at all. `SERVICE_TIMES` now generates all 48 half-hour slots across the full day (`fmt()` wraps the hour modulo 24 so the very last slot, `11:30 PM - 12:00 AM`, displays correctly). Same fix applied to `ticketKanbanGetRescue`'s own identical copy of this constant.
- **Remarks now show who added them.** `boot()` captures `LOGIN_EMAIL` from `ZOHO.CREATOR.UTIL.getInitParams()` (this widget never tracked its own logged-in identity before — unlike the vendor/technician/driver widgets, which fetch their own profile record; there's no "Agents" master report here to look up a display name from, so the login email is the stand-in). Every new Remarks entry is now stamped `"<timestamp> — <email>: <text>"` instead of just `"<timestamp> — <text>"`.
- **Real bug fixed (best-effort — see `QUESTIONS_TO_ASK.md` for the honest caveat): Remarks blank on reopen.** Root-caused to a known Zoho Creator quirk: bulk `getRecords()` responses can return long Multi-line field values truncated compared to a direct single-record fetch — matches the previously-unresolved report exactly (real entries visible in the native Zoho record, blank in this widget). Added `apiGetById()` and wired it into `openTicket()` and the dashboard's own "Close Case" button, so opening a ticket now re-fetches that one record fresh before displaying it, falling back to the bulk-list copy if the API is unavailable. Diagnostic logging updated to reflect the post-refetch values.
- **A ticket scheduled for later now writes `Status:"SCHEDULED"`** instead of the same `"SERVICE FEE QUOTE"` every ticket got regardless of `Time_of_service`. `SCHEDULED` was already wired into the dashboard's "Ready & Invited" bucket from an earlier round but never actually written by anything — `create`'s own status resolver now writes it when `Time_of_service==="LATER"`, and `quote`'s own resume list accepts it so the ticket still reopens at Quote next, same as before.
- **Real bug fixed: Tab jumping to the top of the form for the "Issue" and "Later" fields.** Two distinct causes: (1) the custom radio `.opt` divs (`Time_of_service`, `Preferred_Vendor`, `Refund_Due`, `B2B_GST_Invoice_Required`) had no `id` attribute, so `preservingFocus()` — which restores focus by looking up `document.activeElement.id` after a re-render — silently failed to find them again; picking "LATER" (which reveals `Schedule_Date`/`Service_Time`) lost focus outright, so the next Tab press restarted from the very first field. Every option now gets a stable, deterministic id. (2) `afterMultiselectChange()` (used by `Vehicle_Issue`, i.e. "Issue") called plain `renderWizard()` instead of `rerenderCurrentView()`, bypassing `preservingFocus()` entirely — fixed to go through the same wrapper every other RERENDER-triggering change already uses.

### 2026-08-04 (later) — Google Maps Distance Matrix removed from the Assignment-step vendor picker, per explicit request

A separate session had added a Google Maps Distance Matrix integration the same day (hardcoded `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`, `loadGoogleMaps()`/`googleDistanceMatrix()`/`refreshVendorDistances()`, a `VENDOR_DISTANCE_CACHE`) so the vendor picker's distance column would show real driving distance instead of straight-line, falling back to Haversine only while the request was in flight or unavailable. The user asked for this removed outright — vendor selection should come from `Vendors_Report` with the picker showing `Vendor_Name`, `Availability_Status`, phone, and distance, computed **only** from plain lat/long (no API key, no network call): the vendor's own `Latitude`/`Longitude` (on `Vendors_Report`) against this ticket's own breakdown `Latitude`/`Longitude` (on `Create_Case`, Decimal fields) via the existing `haversineKm()`.

Removed the entire Google Maps block (API key, script loader, Distance Matrix wrapper, the async refresh function and its cache) — `vendorDistanceKm(record, vInfo)` is now a plain, synchronous Haversine calculation with no other code path, `vendorsFor()` no longer kicks off a fire-and-forget network fetch, and `vendorPickerHint()`'s copy no longer references "driving distance". `vendorOptionHtml()` (name, priority, distance, phone, Online/Offline flag) was already correct and needed no changes.

**Not touched**: `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` also gained a similar Google Maps integration from that same session, for their own on-road ETA/distance calculations (a different feature — a technician's own travel to the breakdown site, not vendor selection). Flagging since it's the same embedded-API-key pattern, in case it should be reverted too — not touched here since it wasn't part of this request.

### 2026-08-04 (later still) — Real bugs fixed: Vendors_Report's actual field names confirmed live (vendor_name, Vendor_Priority, nested Address lat/lon, no Phone field yet)

Live screenshot showed the Assignment-step vendor picker displaying raw record IDs instead of names, with no priority/distance/phone either — only the Online/Offline flag rendered correctly. Added detailed diagnostic logging (`pull()`'s label-fallback warning, `vendorInfo()`'s incomplete-data warning) rather than re-guessing field names blindly (this session already got burned once doing that for `Vehicle_Issue`'s `VEHICLE_TYPE`) — the user then pasted their console output, which showed a real `Vendors_Report` record's exact keys and resolved this definitively:

- **`vendor_name` (lowercase)**, not `Vendor_Name` — the planned rename never landed on the real form, same surprise as `VEHICLE_TYPE`. `CONFIG.optionReports.vendors.label` now tries `vendor_name` first.
- **`Vendor_Priority`**, not `Priority` — `CONFIG.vendorPriorityField` corrected.

### 2026-08-07 — WhatsApp integration wired for real (whatsportal.io), replacing the flag-only placeholder buttons

Every "Send ... Link" button previously just flipped a one-way "done" flag with no actual message — noted as a `TODO(WhatsApp integration)` in several places since 2026-07-31. User provided real `whatsportal.io` API credentials and two example requests; added a shared `sendWhatsAppTemplate(phone, templateName, params)` helper (near `formatIndianPhone()`, whose formatting it reuses for the `to` number) and wired it into three buttons:

- **`quote`'s `Send_Link_For_The_Breakdown_Location`** ("Request Location", shown when `Booking_Fee` is not >0) and **`locations`'s own copy of the same field** — both send the `"location"` template, 1 parameter (`Customer_Name`, a reasonable guess not confirmed against the actual approved template text).
- **`quote`'s `Send_Payment_Link`** ("Send Payment Link & Request Location") — sends the `"booking_fees_link"` template, 2 parameters (`Booking_Fee` then `Total_Service_Fee`, in that order — also a guess; swap the order in `buildField`'s STAGES entry if the live message renders wrong).

All three fire-and-forget (not awaited by the button's own onclick, matching how every other best-effort side-effect write in this file behaves) and toast an error if the send fails, without blocking the existing "mark as done" behavior.

**Security note flagged to the user at the time**: the API key is a live, non-domain-restricted secret embedded directly in this widget's own client-side source — visible in plain text to anyone who opens dev tools on the page. Recommended asking `whatsportal.io` about domain-restricted keys, or eventually proxying this through a server-side function (e.g. Zoho Deluge) instead of calling it straight from the browser. Not fixed here — implemented exactly as the user explicitly asked, with the caveat surfaced rather than silently working around it.

### 2026-08-07 (later) — Real bug fixed: direct `fetch()` to whatsportal.io blocked by Zoho's own widget CSP; moved to a Deluge Custom Function

User-reported live console error confirmed the direct `fetch()` from the entry above never actually reaches `whatsportal.io` at all: **"Refused to connect because it violates the document's Content Security Policy."** This is a hard Zoho Creator widget platform restriction (the browser blocks the request before it leaves the page) — not fixable from this widget's own JS, no matter how the fetch call itself is written.

Fixed by moving the actual HTTP call server-side, the standard Zoho Creator workaround for this exact situation: a Deluge script (not subject to the widget's CSP) does the real `invokeurl` to whatsportal.io; the widget now calls it through the SDK instead of `fetch()` directly. The user created this as a typed **Custom Function** (`string sendWhatsAppMessage(string phone, string templateName, string param1, string param2)`), a different Zoho Creator feature from a "Custom API" REST endpoint — its Deluge body:

```
string sendWhatsAppMessage(string phone, string templateName, string param1, string param2)
{
	headers = Map();
	headers.put("Content-Type","application/json");
	headers.put("x-api-key","wp_live_UFs9sZS6Ab2ix99uKY1IhzkQ31dJ3bzm14h2Wi");
	headers.put("Authorization","Bearer wp_live_UFs9sZS6Ab2ix99uKY1IhzkQ31dJ3bzm14h2Wi");

	params_list = List();
	params_list.add(param1);
	if(param2 != null && param2.trim() != "")
	{
		params_list.add(param2);
	}

	template_map = Map();
	template_map.put("name",templateName);
	template_map.put("language","en_US");
	template_map.put("parameters",params_list);

	body_map = Map();
	body_map.put("type","template");
	body_map.put("to",phone);
	body_map.put("template",template_map);

	response = invokeurl
	[
		url :"https://api.whatsportal.io/v1/messages"
		type :POST
		parameters: body_map.toString()
		headers: headers
	];

	info response;
	return response.toString();
}
```

- **Also resolves the earlier security note**: the API key now lives only in this server-side Deluge script — removed entirely from this widget's own client-side source.

### 2026-08-07 (later still) — Real bug fixed: `invokeCustomApi()` needs an actual Custom API, not the Custom Function above

User-reported live console error: `"Invalid Configuration...!!!"`. The logged config confirmed `invokeCustomApi()` is real and does exist on this SDK — it auto-merged its own `version`/`workspace_name`/`envUrlFragment` defaults into what was logged, which only a genuine SDK method would do — but it invokes a **Custom API** (a REST endpoint with its own `api_name`), a different Zoho Creator feature from the typed Custom Function created in the entry above. The first attempt passed `function_name`/`params` (Custom Function terminology), which doesn't match what `invokeCustomApi()` actually validates against.

Fixed two ways together:
1. **User created a real Custom API named `sendWhatsAppMessage`** (POST, OAuth2/All users, Key-and-Value args, Standard response) via Zoho Creator's own wizard — bound directly to the existing Custom Function in its "Actions" step (Zoho's own wizard supports this binding natively; no manual wrapper Deluge script was actually needed, unlike this entry's own original plan).
2. **`sendWhatsAppTemplate()`'s own config switched to the real Custom API keys**: `api_name`/`http_method` instead of `function_name`, keeping `app_name`/`params` as before.

### 2026-08-10 — Real bug fixed: `invokeCustomApi()` (via `resolve()`) hits the wrong domain — `creatorapp.zoho.in` instead of `www.zohoapis.in`

User-reported live network tab: `POST https://creatorapp.zoho.in/creator/custom/getrescued/sendWhatsAppMessage` → **404**, body `{code: 2930, message: "Error Occurred..."}`. The Custom API's own detail page in Zoho Creator documents its real endpoint as `https://www.zohoapis.in/creator/custom/getrescued/sendWhatsAppMessage` — **identical path, different domain**. `creatorapp.zoho.in` is the Creator builder/app-hosting domain (where the widget itself is served from); `www.zohoapis.in` is the actual API domain Custom APIs are documented to live on.

`resolve()`'s own scan order (`[DATA, PUBLISH, META, UTIL, FILE]`) means it found `invokeCustomApi` on `ZOHO.CREATOR.DATA` and never tried any other namespace. Tried three things in order as a diagnostic round (`ZOHO.CREATOR.PUBLISH.invokeCustomApi` directly, the `DATA` version, and a direct `fetch()` to the documented `www.zohoapis.in` URL) — user's console output from testing this ruled out all three cleanly: `PUBLISH.invokeCustomApi` doesn't exist on this SDK at all, `DATA`'s version still 404'd exactly as before, and the direct `fetch()` was **blocked by CSP** — confirmed the widget's `connect-src` allowlist is just five specific domains (`*.zappsusercontent.in`, `*.zohostatic.in`, `*.sigmausercontent.com`, `*.qntrlusercontent.com`, `maps.googleapis.com`), and `www.zohoapis.in` isn't one of them. So no direct `fetch()` from this widget will ever reach ANY external domain not on that exact list — ruled out entirely as an approach, not just for `whatsportal.io`.

### 2026-08-10 (later) — Real bug fixed: wrong `invokeCustomApi()` config keys entirely — confirmed against Zoho's own official docs instead of guessing further

With raw `fetch()` ruled out and three guessed config shapes all failing, looked up [Zoho's own JS API v2 Custom API docs](https://www.zoho.com/creator/help/js-api/v2/custom-api.html) instead of guessing a fourth time. The real config shape was different from every previous attempt in two ways that likely explain all of the earlier failures:

- **There is no `app_name` key at all.** Every previous attempt sent one — it's simply not a real parameter, silently ignored.
- **The real key is `workspace_name`, not `app_name`** — and it's the Zoho account/workspace name (`"getrescued"`), not `CONFIG.appName` (`"get-rescue"`) — two different strings for two different things, easy to conflate.
- **The real payload key is `payload`, not `params`.** `params` isn't documented anywhere in Zoho's own spec — sending it instead of `payload` most likely means the SDK had no valid request body to work with on every prior attempt, which plausibly explains why it fell back to building a malformed/wrong-domain URL rather than a normal validation error.

`sendWhatsAppTemplate()` now sends `{api_name, workspace_name, http_method, content_type, payload}` per the official spec, and the earlier `PUBLISH`-namespace/raw-fetch diagnostic branches were removed — they were solving the wrong problem, kept only while the real config shape was still unknown.

**Confirmed working live** — user's next screenshot showed the Locations step's button flip to "Location Link Sent" with no error toast. First real end-to-end success across this whole multi-day WhatsApp integration effort.

### 2026-08-10 (later still) — Auto-send on arrival + stays resendable (user request)

Two related changes to `Send_Link_For_The_Breakdown_Location` (Locations step only — the Quote step's own copy of this field, and `Send_Payment_Link`, are unchanged):
- **`repeatable:true` added** — this button used to lock permanently into a green "Location Link Sent" state after one click (same one-time-lock pattern `Payment_Method`/other done-flag buttons use). User asked for it to "stay enabled" so a message can be resent (wrong number, missed notification, etc.). Same `repeatable` mechanism `Calculate_Distance`/`Send_ETA_To_Customer` already use elsewhere in this file — trades the persistent green "done" state for a one-off toast on each send instead (that's how every other repeatable button here already behaves, not a new tradeoff invented for this one).
- **Auto-fires once per ticket** — `renderWizard()` now sends the same WhatsApp template automatically the instant the agent reaches the Locations step, instead of waiting for a manual click, per "customer automatically receives location link" — guarded on the field's own flag so it only fires once (not on every re-render of this step, e.g. typing into the lat/long paste box) and updates that flag synchronously before the async send resolves, so a second re-render mid-flight can't double-fire it. The button stays there afterward purely for manual resends.

### 2026-08-11 — Real bug fixed: dashboard status buckets now start collapsed (user request)

`renderReportLists()`'s bucket sections ("New & Open", "Ready & Invited", "Work In Progress", "RSP Closed") used to default to all-expanded, tracked opt-out via `COLLAPSED_BUCKETS` (present in the set = collapsed). Flipped to `EXPANDED_BUCKETS`, opt-in (present = expanded) — same inversion `EXPANDED_CLOSURE_GROUPS` already uses for Final Closure's own sections — so every bucket now starts collapsed on load, and a click still expands/collapses just that one section as before.

### 2026-08-11 (later) — Real bug fixed: reverting `Acceptance_Mode` from "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" back to "Vendor Accepting via App" left stale seeded vendor data behind

User-described scenario: agent picks "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" (this seeds `Assigned_Vendor`/`Vendor_Email`/`Vendor_Email1`/`Vendor_Email2` via `seedAcceptanceVendorFields()`, see that function's own header comment), then later switches back to "Vendor Accepting via App" to actually wait for the vendor's own app. Nothing cleared the seeded fields on that reversal — they'd just sit there, looking exactly like a real acceptance even though the mode now says "waiting on the vendor," resurrecting the same "looks accepted but isn't" failure class this whole `Acceptance_Mode` feature was originally built to prevent (see this function's own comment on the forward-direction fix from 2026-08-07).

Fixed inside `seedAcceptanceVendorFields()` itself: tracks the last-seen `Acceptance_Mode` on the record as `_prevAcceptanceMode` (same bolted-on-property tracking style `calcQuote()`'s own `_rateBreakupSignature` already uses, never part of the real payload since it's not declared in any STAGES field list) — if the previous value was "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" and the current one isn't, clears all four seeded fields before doing anything else. Starts `undefined` on a freshly-loaded record, so this never misfires on initial load — only on an actual in-session switch away from "Agent Entering...".

**Live suspicion raised the same day**: user reports the `Acceptance_Mode` selection itself doesn't survive leaving and reopening a ticket. Turns out this field was never added to `FIELDS.md` — never independently confirmed as a real `Create_Case` field at all, unlike almost everything else in this file. Added targeted diagnostic logging to `saveStep()`/`openTicket()` (logs `Acceptance_Mode`'s exact value at both save and reload) and a `FIELDS.md` entry flagging the suspicion — waiting on the user's console output to confirm whether the field simply doesn't exist in Zoho (most likely, given Zoho silently drops unrecognized payload keys rather than erroring) before doing anything further here.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Acceptance outcome fields locked unless "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" (user request)

Every outcome field on the acceptance/acceptanceTow stage (`Vendor_Email1`/`Vendor_Email2`, `Vendor_Email`, `Technician_Email`/`Technician_Email1`, `Assigned_Technician`, `Assigned_Technician_Email`, `Assigned_Vendor`, `Image_Upload1`) used to stay editable regardless of `Acceptance_Mode` — meaning the agent could freely type/pick values even while the mode said "Vendor Accepting via App," undermining the exact "looks accepted but isn't" problem `Acceptance_Mode` was built to prevent in the first place (same failure class as both entries above). New `acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)` predicate (`s.Acceptance_Mode==="Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor"`) — every field above now sets `readonly:(s)=>!acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)`, locked whenever the mode is (still) "Vendor Accepting via App" or blank/unset (matching the field's own declared default), editable only once the agent explicitly switches modes. `Acceptance_Mode` itself is deliberately excluded from this gate — it must always stay interactive so the agent can actually flip it.

This required extending `isReadonly()` (added 2026-08-06 for `Client_Tracking_Id`, previously only read by the plain text/tel/email input branch in `buildField()`) to the `select`/searchable-select/file branches too — those still checked raw `f.readonly` directly, which would have always evaluated truthy for a function value regardless of what it returned. Radio/remarks/textarea/check branches still only support a static boolean, not yet needed dynamically anywhere in this file.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: `RSP_Completion_Time` save rejected with "Enter a valid date format"

User-reported live error: `{"RSP_Completion_Time":"Enter a valid date format for RSP Completion Time"}`, with the field showing `10/8/2026 17:50:00` — a numeric slash-separated date, not this app's own `fmtDateTime()` output shape (`10-Aug-2026 17:50:00`). Root cause: `RSP_Completion_Time` was `t:"text"` in all three of its STAGES entries (`wip`, `wipLoadingTow`, `wipUnloadingTow`'s Dropped action) — completely free-form, no format enforcement at all, so however it got into that shape (most likely a manual edit using a locale date notation, since this field is otherwise only ever machine-written via `fmtDateTime(new Date())` in the field-facing widgets), Zoho's own stricter validation rejected it outright on save.

Fixed by switching all three to `t:"datetime"` (a real `<input type="datetime-local">` picker), which also surfaced and fixed a real latent gap in `buildField()`'s generic input branch: it assigned a field's current value straight into the `<input>` unconverted, but `datetime-local` requires its own strict `"YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm"` shape — harmless for `Schedule_Date_Time` (added 2026-08-05, always freshly typed, so an already-saved value was never displayed there) but would have shown blank for any datetime field displaying a pre-existing value. New `toDatetimeLocalValue()` converts any parseable date into that exact shape before display.

**One caveat for the specific ticket that hit this**: since the malformed value was already free-text before this fix, re-parsing `"10/8/2026"` is genuinely ambiguous — JS's `Date` constructor always reads slash-separated dates as `MM/DD` (US convention) regardless of browser locale, so if the intended date was "10 August" (`DD/M`, common in India) rather than "October 8," the picker may show the wrong one for this one ticket after the fix. Worth the user double-checking that specific value once — going forward, every field-facing widget write uses `fmtDateTime()`'s own month-name format (unambiguous regardless of locale), and the picker itself prevents any new free-text ambiguity from ever being introduced again.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — UI polish: Expense Amount+Photo paired, acceptance-stage fields grouped into rows (user request)

Two related layout requests on the same screenshot (RSR `wip` stage): `Expense_Amount`/`Expense_Photo` now share a row (`row:"expense"`) instead of stacking separately, same shared-row mechanism `Customer_Name`/`Phone_Number1`/`Email` already use on the Create step.

Follow-up ("fields feel scattered/disorganized," confirmed via clarifying question) grouped the acceptance/acceptanceTow stage's now-unlockable fields (see the "Acceptance outcome fields locked..." entry above) into three 2-column rows instead of each stacking full-width alone: `vendorEmails` (`Vendor_Email1`/`Vendor_Email2` + `Vendor_Email`), `techEmails` (`Technician_Email`/`Technician_Email1` + `Assigned_Technician_Email` — both share the identical `vendorHasTechnicians` gate, so they only ever show/hide together), and `assignedWho` (`Assigned_Technician` + `Assigned_Vendor` — different gating, so this row degrades to a single full-width field if the technician side is hidden, per `renderFields()`'s own "column count matches however many fields actually land" behavior). Real gotcha hit while implementing this: `renderFields()`'s row-grouping only merges fields that are *adjacent* in the STAGES array sharing the same `row` tag — the original field order (grouped by "when was this field added" comments) had to be reordered to match the new visual grouping, since simply adding matching `row:` tags to non-adjacent fields silently does nothing.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Payment Gateway option + QR display + real WhatsApp send on the Payment step (user request)

Three related additions to the `payment` stage's `Final Payment Collection` screen:

- **"Payment Gateway" added to `Payment_Method_Final`'s options** (was Cash/UPI/Card/Net Banking only) — same option `vendorTicket`'s own payment screen already restored 2026-08-04; this agent-facing screen never had it added back.
- **New synthetic `t:"qrblock"` field type** (`_PaymentQR`, shown only `when: Payment_Method_Final==="Payment Gateway"`) — same decorative QR placeholder `vendorTicket`'s `renderPaymentScreen()` already uses (no real gateway/webhook wired up behind it on either side of the app — purely visual). Display-only, nothing stored — same class as `navlink`/`computed`, added to the same exclusion lists (`grp()`'s Final Closure filter, `collectStep()`).
- **`Send_Payment_Link1` converted from `t:"check"` to a real `t:"button"`** wired to `sendWhatsAppTemplate()`, reusing the `booking_fees_link` template (same as `quote`'s own `Send_Payment_Link`) with `Remaining_Fee`/`Total_Service_Fee` as its two params instead of `Booking_Fee`/`Total_Service_Fee` — no distinct "final/remaining payment" WhatsApp template was specified, so this reuses the confirmed-working one; swap the template name in this field's own `action` if a different approved template exists for final collection specifically. `repeatable:true`, same tradeoff as every other repeatable send button in this file (toast feedback instead of a permanent "done" lock, so the agent can resend).

Converting that button surfaced a real latent bug: `buildField()`'s own `t:"button"` branch never checked `f.readonly`/`isReadonly()` at all, unlike every other field type — harmless before now since button fields are already excluded from Final Closure's own summary entirely, but a button inside a `step.viewOnly` stage (or any future field-level readonly) would have silently stayed clickable regardless. Fixed alongside — disabled state and "done" styling are now tracked separately, so a readonly-but-never-clicked button shows its normal label without falsely claiming to be "done."

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — `SCHEDULED` tickets now auto-promote to `READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` once their 2-hour window closes (user request)

User asked whether `isServiceTimeFarFuture()`'s own 2-hour threshold (see that function's own comment) was "auto working for all tickets" — it wasn't. That check only ever ran inside the `locations` stage's status computation, which only runs when an agent actually opens and saves THAT ONE ticket — `bucketOfStatus()` (the dashboard's own bucketing) just reads whatever `Status` is already stored in Zoho, never re-evaluates anything. A `SCHEDULED` ticket left completely untouched stayed `SCHEDULED` on the dashboard forever, even long after its own scheduled time passed.

**Client-side fix (this repo)**: new `autoPromoteScheduledTickets(recs)` — filters the currently-fetched ticket list for `Status==="SCHEDULED"` tickets whose `isServiceTimeFarFuture()` has flipped to `false`, updates each via `updateTicket()`, and mutates the in-memory record so the dashboard reflects it immediately. Called from both `loadDashboard()` (every normal load/refresh) and the existing 30-second `pollDashboardForMechanicUpdates()` timer — so as long as *any* agent has the dashboard open, every `SCHEDULED` ticket gets checked and promoted within at most 30 seconds of crossing the threshold, and a promotion correctly surfaces through the existing mechanic-update-change notification for free (no separate handling needed — the poll's own before/after status comparison already covers it).

**Genuine limitation this doesn't solve**: if literally nobody has the dashboard open (e.g. overnight), nothing client-side can run at all — a browser tab has to exist for any of this JS to execute. Wrote (not yet installed — needs a human with Zoho Creator Studio access) a companion Deluge **Schedule** (`promoteScheduledTickets`, recommended every 15–30 min) that does the identical check server-side via `zoho.creator.getRecords`/`updateRecords`, independent of any widget being open. Requires a Zoho Creator **Connection** to be configured first (Settings → Connections) — both of those `zoho.creator.*` tasks need one even for same-app access — and the script's own `owner`/`appName`/`reportName`/`connectionName` placeholders confirmed against the real values before enabling it live. Script kept in the session scratchpad, not yet installed in Zoho.

**User asked for a Custom API version instead** (same day) — wrote a second variant using the SAME logic but the plain same-app `Create_Case[criteria]` native syntax, which needs no Connection at all (unlike the Schedule version's cross-app `zoho.creator.*` tasks) since a Custom API/Function lives in the same application as the form it's reading/updating. Important nuance flagged to the user: a Custom API only runs when something calls it — it doesn't run periodically on its own the way a Schedule does. Gave both pieces: the Custom API itself, and an optional one-line companion Schedule (just an `invokeurl` to the Custom API's own endpoint, same pattern `sendWhatsAppMessage`'s Custom API is invoked with from the widget) for whoever wants the Custom API to actually run unattended. Also flagged the simpler alternative: skip the Custom API entirely and put the same logic straight into a Schedule (the first variant above) if on-demand callability isn't actually needed. Both scripts kept in the session scratchpad, neither installed in Zoho yet.

Went through several more rounds getting a working Deluge function (`Scheduler()`, user's own chosen name) live:
- **Ternary operator (`? :`) rejected on save** — confirmed via Zoho's own docs that it's never been officially shipped in Deluge (only a long-standing community feature *request*) — rewritten with plain `if/else`, and the inline `{"Status":"..."}` map literal swapped for explicit `Map()`+`.put()` too, matching the exact form already proven working in `sendWhatsAppMessage`.
- **`"Please specify a valid date value in application date format"`** at runtime — the user's own Zoho Studio field screenshot confirmed `Computed_Service_Time` is a real Date-Time *type* field (not Single Line Text) with format `dd-MMM-yyyy hh:mm:ss A` (single capital `A`, not the `aa` originally guessed). Since a same-app `Form[criteria]` fetch likely already returns date-typed fields as comparable dates (not strings), `Scheduler()` now tries a direct comparison first and only falls back to `.toDateTime("dd-MMM-yyyy hh:mm:ss A")` if that throws — catching and reporting the raw value + Deluge's own error message on double failure instead of guessing a third format blind.
- **`"Promoted 0 of null"`** — `Create_Case[criteria]` returns `null` (not an empty list) when zero records match, and `null.size()` itself returns `null` rather than `0` or throwing. Guarded explicitly (`recordCount` computed via `if/else`, not the now-forbidden ternary) so the report string always shows a real number — a `0 of 0` result is the correct, expected outcome whenever no tickets are actually `SCHEDULED` at test time, not a bug.

**User then asked whether this could be done purely in the widget instead**, to sidestep Deluge entirely — confirmed yes, and that it already had been: `autoPromoteScheduledTickets()` (this same section, above) already covers the "at least one agent has the dashboard open" case with zero Deluge involved. Reiterated the one thing client-side code genuinely cannot do — run when no browser tab has the widget open at all — as the sole remaining reason to bother finishing the Deluge route.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Acceptance_Mode lock/unlock extended to the Reach step (user request)

`reach`/`reachTow`'s own fields (`Agent_ETA_Override`(`_TOW`), `Send_ETA_To_Customer`, `Image_Upload2`/`Image_Upload5`, `Cancel_Reason`/`Reject_Reason2`, `Odometer_reading_at_Reached_Location`, `Toll_Charges`) had no `Acceptance_Mode` gating at all, unlike the Accept step's own already-gated fields — user confirmed via screenshot this should extend to "not be able to edit or do anything" while "Vendor Accepting via App," same `readonly:(s)=>!acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)` gate as `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`. `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` themselves stay untouched — they're the Google-Maps-calculated originals (vendor-app-written), already living read-only-only in Final Closure's own Distance & ETA Metrics group since 2026-08-04, never editable on this step at all; `Agent_ETA_Override` is the one field the agent could actually type into, so it's the one that needed locking.

Required extending `isReadonly()` to the `radio` branch too (`Toll_Charges`) — same completeness gap as the `select`/file branches fixed earlier the same day, radio's own tabindex/`.locked` class were still reading raw `f.readonly` directly.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Distance/timestamp fields now editable per-stage in Agent-entering mode (user request)

`Distance_To_Breakdown`(`_TOW`), `Roundtrip_Distance`, `Office_To_Mechanic`, `Mechanic_To_Breakdown`, `Breakdown_To_Office`, `Service_Acceptance`(`_For_Tow`), `Service_Reject_Time`, `Cancellation_Time`, `Pickup_Time` — 11 fields that only ever lived read-only in Final Closure's synthetic Distance & ETA Metrics / Field Activity Log groups, normally computed/stamped automatically by the vendor/technician/driver's own app actions (confirmed each field's real write point live in `vendorTicket` before placing it). Nothing computes or stamps any of them when the agent is entering details manually instead — added as real editable inputs, `when:acceptanceIsAgentManual` (hidden entirely otherwise, not just locked), split across each field's own "natural" stage matched to its confirmed write point:

- `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`: `Distance_To_Breakdown`(`_TOW`), `Service_Acceptance`(`_For_Tow`), `Service_Reject_Time` — all written in `acceptService()`/its reject branch.

- `wipReachedTow`: TOW's own 4-point-total copies of the same four distance fields as `wip` above.

Timestamp fields use `t:"datetime"` (a real picker), not `t:"text"` — same fix as `RSP_Completion_Time`'s own entry above, avoiding the identical free-text date ambiguity bug.

**Real duplication bug fixed in the same pass**: since Final Closure shows every stage field automatically, these 11 fields would otherwise appear TWICE there in Agent-entering mode — once via the new per-stage row, once via the still-unconditional synthetic group row. Added a matching `!acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)` exclusion to each of these 11 fields' own entries in `DISTANCE_METRICS_FIELDS`/`FIELD_ACTIVITY_LOG_FIELDS`, so exactly one copy is visible at a time — the per-stage one in Agent-entering mode, the synthetic-group one otherwise (unchanged from how it already worked).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: picking a different Assigned Vendor didn't update Vendor_Email/Vendor_Email1/Vendor_Email2

User-reported live: changing the "Assigned Vendor" dropdown (Agent-entering mode) left "Vendor Email"/"Vendor Email (Assignment)" showing the *previous* vendor's email. Two compounding causes:

1. `Assigned_Vendor` wasn't in the `RERENDER` list — picking a new one didn't even trigger a re-render, so nothing downstream could react to the change until the agent navigated away and back.
2. `seedAcceptanceVendorFields()`'s own email-filling logic (`if(!r.Vendor_Email) r.Vendor_Email = email;`) only ever fills a *blank* field — once any value is there at all (from the first seed, or an earlier pick), it's permanently stuck; a manual re-pick never overwrites it.

Fixed both: `Assigned_Vendor` added to `RERENDER`, and a new `_prevAssignedVendor` tracker (same bolted-on-property style `_prevAcceptanceMode`/`_rateBreakupSignature` already use elsewhere in this file) — when `Assigned_Vendor` actually changes to something new, force-overwrites `Vendor_Email`/`Vendor_Email1`/`Vendor_Email2` to match the newly picked vendor's own email instead of leaving them on the old one.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: selecting "Payment Gateway" didn't show the QR block

Exact same root cause as the Assigned Vendor entry just above, different field: `Payment_Method_Final` also wasn't in `RERENDER` — selecting "Payment Gateway" updated the dropdown itself (native browser behavior needs no JS at all for that part) but never triggered the re-render `_PaymentQR`'s own `when:(s)=>s.Payment_Method_Final==="Payment Gateway"` needs to actually get re-evaluated and show the QR block. Added `Payment_Method_Final` to `RERENDER` — no other change needed, unlike Assigned_Vendor's own extra `_prevAssignedVendor` logic, since `_PaymentQR`'s visibility is driven entirely by a plain `when:` check with nothing else to seed/overwrite.

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: Assigned Vendor's email still stale after the RERENDER fix above

User-reported live: still stale even after `Assigned_Vendor` was added to `RERENDER`. Root cause was one level deeper — `seedAcceptanceVendorFields()`'s new "vendor changed" lookup matched `OPTIONS.vendors` by `o.id` only, but a **freshly picked** value from this `useId:true` select is the vendor's *label* ("Sagar Tomar 773"), not its id — `idsFor()` only converts label → id at save time (see `buildField`'s own select-branch comment on this exact shape, already fixed there for display purposes days earlier). Matching by id alone silently found nothing for a fresh pick, so the overwrite never ran and the old email stayed put — it would only have worked for an already-saved/reloaded record. Fixed with the same ID-first, label-fallback lookup already used elsewhere in this file for the identical ambiguity.
