User-reported live: clicking "Send ETA to Customer" (`reach`/`reachTow`'s `Send_ETA_To_Customer` button) only ever showed a toast — it never called `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` in the first place, unlike every other WhatsApp send button in this file (Locations' link-send x2, Quote's/Payment's payment-link-send). Wired both byte-identical occurrences (RSR `reach` and TOW `reachTow`) to actually call `sendWhatsAppTemplate(r.Phone_Number1, "location", [eta||"shortly"])`, reusing `expectedArrivalTime(r)` for the ETA text and reporting success/failure via toast exactly like the other send buttons.

**Flagged, not yet actioned**: no dedicated "ETA" WhatsApp template exists yet, so this reuses the `"location"` template (same one Locations' own link-send buttons use) — functionally sends now, but the template's own wording was written for sharing a location link, not announcing an arrival time. Swap in a purpose-built template name here once one's approved, same one-line change as any other `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` call in this file.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Reopening a ticket now resumes the exact step the agent was last on, not just whatever Status implies (user request)

**SUPERSEDED the same day** — see the next entry below ("...now backed by a real Zoho field") for the final version. Left in place per this file's own convention of not erasing the history of why something changed: the localStorage layer described here worked, but the user asked for it to instead match vendorTicket's own approach (`goToStatus()` there is always fired right after the matching Status write already succeeded — one persisted source of truth, no side channel), which needed a real field and replaced everything below.

User request: "when the agent is entering the details step by step, the system should save the agent's current progress... if the agent... goes back to the All Tickets page, and then opens the same ticket again, it should take them directly to the last step they were working on." Concretely: if they were on the Accept step, reopening should reopen straight onto Accept, not somewhere earlier.

`openTicket()` already resumed onto SOME step via `stageIndexForStatus()` — never truly "from the beginning" — but that function only ever reads the ticket's own persisted `Status` field, and a real gap exists between "Status" and "what the wizard is actually showing": several stages write the NEXT stage's status as part of THEIR OWN save and advance `state.stepIndex` onto the next stage in that same action — e.g. `assignment`'s save writes `Status:"RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)"` and moves the wizard onto `acceptance`, but nothing about merely being on the Accept screen touches Zoho until Accept's OWN save runs. Leave before that (back to All Tickets, nothing saved yet) and a Status-only resume lands back on Assign — one real step earlier than where the agent actually was, matching the report exactly.

Fixed with a small same-device localStorage layer (`gr_step_<recordId>` → the current step's own `key`, e.g. `"acceptance"`), deliberately NOT a new Zoho field — no schema change needed, and it only ever supplements Status, never overrides it outright:

- **`saveStepProgress()`** — wired into `renderWizard()` itself (the one function every `state.stepIndex` change already funnels through: `prevStep`/`jumpTo`/`jumpToAgentClosureStep`/`saveStep`'s own advance/`openTicket`'s initial resume), so every step change is captured for free without touching each of those call sites individually. Skipped for a brand-new unsaved ticket (nothing to key it by yet) and for a reopened-CLOSED ticket (nothing left to resume).
- **`resumeStepIndex()`** — used by `openTicket()` alongside the existing `stageIndexForStatus()` call. Only ever moves the resume point FORWARD from the Status-derived index, and only by exactly one step — the confirmed shape of the gap above. A remembered step further ahead than that (stale from a much older session, wrong branch after an RSR↔TOW change, etc.) is discarded in favor of the plain Status-derived index, so a ticket that's genuinely regressed (e.g. rejected and sent back for re-assignment, which moves the Status-derived index BACKWARDS) still resumes on the correct earlier step instead of a stale later one.
- **`clearStepProgress()`** — called once `saveClosure()` actually closes the ticket; nothing left to resume, so the marker is dropped rather than left stale in localStorage forever.

**Known limitation, by design**: this is per-browser/per-device, not a ticket-level field — a different agent (or the same agent on a different device/browser) opening the same ticket still resumes purely off Status, same as before this fix. A cross-device version of this would need a real new field on `Create_Case` to store the last-visited step, which needs a Zoho Studio schema change first — not done here; flag if that's actually wanted.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Step-resume now backed by a real Zoho field, matching vendorTicket's own approach (user request: "like we have set this type of thing in VendorTickets")

Same feature as the entry directly above, reworked at the user's request to match how `vendorTicket` already does this instead of the browser-only localStorage patch. The key observation: `vendorTicket`'s own `goToStatus()` (see that widget's own comment) is only ever called right after the matching `Status` write has ALREADY succeeded against Zoho — there's no gap between what's on screen and what's persisted, and no side channel needed, because every one of vendorTicket's screens maps 1:1 onto a `Status` value it writes itself. getRescueTicket's wizard can't fully copy that as-is — several of its steps share the SAME `Status` while the agent is mid-step (the `assignment`→`acceptance` gap described in the entry above is the confirmed example) — so `Status` alone still can't fully disambiguate. The fix: add a second real field that's ALWAYS kept in sync the same disciplined way, instead of leaning on the browser.

**New field**: `Agent_Wizard_Step` (Single Line Text, added to `FIELDS.md`'s Create_Case build spec — **needs to actually be created in Zoho Studio**, not yet done as of this entry) — stores the current step's own `key` (e.g. `"acceptance"`).

- **`persistWizardStep(recordId, stepKey)`** — a small best-effort `updateTicket()` call, `try`/`catch`-wrapped so a not-yet-created field (or any other save hiccup) fails silently and never blocks navigation or the ticket's own real data saves; this field is purely a convenience layer on top of everything else.
- Wired into `renderWizard()` — the one function every `state.stepIndex` change already funnels through (`prevStep`/`jumpTo`/`jumpToAgentClosureStep`/`saveStep`'s own advance/`openTicket`'s initial resume) — deduped against a new `state._persistedStepKey` so it only actually fires on a genuine step change, not on every one of renderWizard()'s other callers (an unrelated `RERENDER`-triggered field change, or the Agent Closure step's own 20s auto-poll — both call `rerenderCurrentView()`→`renderWizard()` too, and without the dedupe check this would have fired a Zoho write on every single one of those instead of just real navigation).
- **`resumeStepIndex(path, statusIndex, status, persistedStepKey)`** — reworked from the localStorage version's own "only trust it one step ahead" cap (calibrated to a browser-only marker that could go stale in ways this real field mostly can't) to: trust `Agent_Wizard_Step` whenever it resolves to a step the SAME OR FURTHER ALONG than `Status` implies (normal here — most steps don't touch `Status` until their own save runs), except when `Status` is `"RSP REJECT"` — the one confirmed case where something EXTERNAL (the vendor's own reject action) moves the ticket backward after the persisted step was recorded; `assignment`'s own STAGES comment already documents re-assignment as the deliberately-correct resume target there. The very next `renderWizard()` call after a reopen like that self-heals `Agent_Wizard_Step` to match, so a stale value never keeps reasserting itself.
- `openTicket()` seeds `state._persistedStepKey` from whatever's already stored in Zoho (skipping a pointless first write when nothing's actually changed) before its first `renderWizard()` call.

**Ships safely ahead of the field actually existing in Zoho**: every write is best-effort (see `persistWizardStep()` above), and every read just sees `undefined` on a record with no such field yet — behavior is identical to before this feature existed until `Agent_Wizard_Step` is actually added in Zoho Studio per `FIELDS.md`'s new row for it.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: editing the date/time on an already-scheduled ticket reset it back to "Service Fee Quote" (user-reported live, exact repro given)

User's exact repro: a "LATER" ticket saved for 16:30 already had Booking Fee paid and location captured, and had correctly reached `SCHEDULED` (shown in the "Ready & Invited" dashboard column). Going back to the Create step and changing the time to 15:00 made the ticket reset to `SERVICE FEE QUOTE` — even though nothing about the fee or location had changed, only the time. User's own stated rule: fee success + location success should always resolve to `READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` (time <2h away) or `SCHEDULED` (time ≥2h away), never backward to an earlier stage.

Root cause: `create`'s own `status()` (Step 1's save) was hardcoded to always write `"SERVICE FEE QUOTE"` — correct for a genuinely new ticket's first save, but the stepper never re-locks an already-reached step, so an agent going back to Step 1 later to fix `Schedule_Date_Time` (the only place that field lives) and clicking Save blindly re-ran that same hardcoded line, discarding everything the `quote`/`locations` steps had already legitimately settled.

Fixed — `create`'s `status()` now only hardcodes `SERVICE FEE QUOTE` for a genuinely new ticket (no `r.ID` yet, the original case). For an existing ticket:
- If its current `Status` is already past assignment (invited, accepted, in progress, closed — see the new `EARLY_STATUSES` allowlist) — leaves it completely alone. A Step 1 resave must never clobber real pipeline progress; this guards the case a full "always recompute" fix would have introduced.
- Otherwise, if Booking Fee is genuinely settled (`PAID`, or no fee due) **and** the location requirement is met (`locationReady()` — breakdown only for RSR, breakdown **and** drop for TOW) — re-applies the exact same `SCHEDULED`-vs-`READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` cascade the `locations` stage's own `status()` already uses, instead of resetting.
- Otherwise (fee or location genuinely not yet settled) — unchanged, still `SERVICE FEE QUOTE`, exactly as before.

Deliberately did NOT reuse `quoteStatus()` directly for the fee check — a blank `Booking_Fee` there is ambiguous (no fee needed vs. quote never even given), and stacking that ambiguity on top of a Step-1 resave path risked prematurely jumping tickets that had never even reached Quote. Gating on `locationReady()` too sidesteps this for free, since location can only be genuinely ready after the agent has already been through Quote at least once.

**Tested** (script kept in the session scratchpad, not part of the repo) by loading the real `<script>` body out of `widget.html` into a sandboxed Node VM and calling the actual shipped `create.status()` against 9 scenarios: the exact reported repro (moving the time closer → flips to `READY FOR ASSIGNMENT`), the reverse direction (pushing the time out past 2h → moves back to `SCHEDULED`), fee-still-pending, location-not-yet-captured, a brand-new ticket, an already-invited ticket (confirms it's left untouched), and both TOW sub-cases (drop location missing vs. both locations set). All 9 passed against the real code.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: reopening a SCHEDULED ticket landed on Locations (Step 3) instead of Assignment (Step 4) (user-reported live)

Root cause: `locations`'s own `statuses` list deliberately included `"SCHEDULED"` since 2026-08-06 — the original intent was "hold the ticket here so re-saving this step re-checks the 2-hour window" (see that entry's own comment; a job scheduled hours out was meant to not get a vendor invited immediately). User's own testing showed this landing page is wrong: clicking a `SCHEDULED` ticket should go straight to Assignment, same as an already-`READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` one.

Fixed: `"SCHEDULED"` moved out of `locations`'s resume list into `assignment`'s (which already resumes `READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` there) — `locations` still WRITES `"SCHEDULED"` exactly as before, only where reopening such a ticket lands changed. Verified against the real shipped `stageIndexForStatus()`/`buildPath()` (same Node-VM harness as the entry above): `"SCHEDULED"` now resolves to the `assignment` step; `"LOCATION PENDING"`/`"READY FOR ASSIGNMENT"` are unaffected.

**Worth knowing, not acted on**: `assignment` has no `canAdvance` gate of its own — nothing stops an agent from inviting a vendor on a still-`SCHEDULED` (>2h out) ticket the moment they land here now, which is exactly the "shouldn't get invited immediately" scenario the original design was trying to prevent. Left as-is since the ask was specifically about navigation, not about restricting the action — the auto-promotion feature (`autoPromoteScheduledTickets()`/the Deluge Scheduler, both from earlier the same day) still independently flips `SCHEDULED`→`READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` once the window closes regardless of this. Flag if a soft "this is still N hours out, assign anyway?" confirmation on Assignment's own Save is wanted too — straightforward to add on top of this (`confirmBox()` already exists and is used the same way in `cancelWizard()`), just not built here since it wasn't asked for.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: editing Schedule Date & Time from the Close Case screen didn't update the date/time shown on All Tickets (user-reported live)

Confirmed the exact mechanism. Once Booking Fee + Location are settled, `Schedule_Date_Time` becomes editable from the "Close Case" (Agent Closure) screen too — it's part of the same click-any-field-to-edit popup that screen's "Customer & Vehicle · Agent" summary group already exposes for every other `create`-stage field (see `closureFieldGroups()`). But the All Tickets dashboard row doesn't display `Schedule_Date_Time` at all — it displays `Computed_Service_Time` (`rowHtml()`'s own "Service Time" line), which is a *derived* field: normally re-computed via `create`'s own `extra()` hook (`computeServiceTime()`) every time the Create step itself is saved through the normal wizard (`buildPayload()` already runs `step.extra(r)` on every step save). The Close Case popup's own single-field save (`saveFieldEditor()`) never did that — it only ever wrote the one field actually being edited, so `Schedule_Date_Time` changed in Zoho but `Computed_Service_Time` — and so the dashboard — stayed exactly as it was.

Fixed generically rather than special-casing this one field: `closureFieldGroups()` now also records each summary field's own origin `STAGES` entry (`FIELD_ORIGIN_STAGE`, reset every render same as `CLOSURE_FIELD_INDEX` right next to it), and `saveFieldEditor()` re-runs that origin stage's own `extra()` hook (if it has one) and merges its output into the same save — exactly what a full step save already does. Confirmed both `extra()` hooks in this file are safe to re-run at an arbitrary later time this way: `create`'s (`Computed_Service_Time`/`Phone_Number`/`Lead_Source` are all pure functions of the current record) and `locations`'s (`Location_Received_Time` — already only-sets-once, guarded by its own `!r.Location_Received_Time` check). One side benefit for free: editing `Phone_Number1` via this same popup now also correctly re-mirrors into the real required `Phone_Number` field, which had the identical gap.

Deliberately did NOT also recompute `Status` (`SCHEDULED`/`READY FOR ASSIGNMENT`) through this same path — unlike `extra()`, blindly recomputing a stage's `status()` for every field edited via this popup would risk regressing a ticket that's since moved past that stage (e.g. tweaking an Assignment-group field here while the ticket's already `WORK IN PROGRESS` would wrongly write `Status:"RSP IRA..."` again) — the same class of risk the `EARLY_STATUSES` guard exists to prevent on the Create step's own resave path (see the entry above), not yet extended to this one. Practical effect: editing the date/time from Close Case now correctly refreshes what the dashboard *displays*, but doesn't itself flip `SCHEDULED`↔`READY FOR ASSIGNMENT` the way editing it via the actual Create step does — say if that's wanted too and it can be added with the same `EARLY_STATUSES`-guarded logic.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, same approach as the two entries above): simulated editing `Schedule_Date_Time` to `15:00` via the popup and captured the actual payload `saveFieldEditor()` sent — confirmed it now includes a freshly-recomputed `Computed_Service_Time` (previously would have been missing entirely) alongside `Schedule_Date_Time`/`Phone_Number`/`Lead_Source`. Also confirmed no regression: editing a field from a stage with no `extra()` hook (e.g. `quote`'s `Booking_Fee`) still sends only that one field, unchanged from before.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Verified: original vendor-calculated ETA is protected from customer-communication edits; locked a real gap found next to it (user request: "verify this use case")

User asked to confirm that once a vendor accepts and their app's own Google/Haversine ETA calculation is saved (`ETA`/`ETA_TOW`), it stays intact for reporting — and that later editing the ETA shown to the customer never touches that original value. Audited every read/write of `ETA`/`ETA_TOW`/`Agent_ETA_Override`(`_TOW`) across all six widgets to check.

**Confirmed already correct**: `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` are written *only* by the vendor/technician/driver's own `acceptService()`, at Accept time — no write happens anywhere else in `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` (not Reach, not Cancel, not WIP). The "Send ETA to Customer" button (`Send_ETA_To_Customer`'s `action`, `reach`/`reachTow`) never writes `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` either — it only reads them via `expectedArrivalTime()`, and the agent's own override for customer communication is a genuinely separate field, `Agent_ETA_Override`(`_TOW`) — confirmed no overlap with `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` in the same save payload. So the specific communication flow this request named was already correctly isolated.

**Real gap found and fixed**: `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` could still be directly hand-edited through two *other* paths, unrelated to customer communication —
- `getRescueTicket`'s Close Case screen (`DISTANCE_METRICS_FIELDS`'s click-to-edit popup) — `readonly:true` was explicitly removed from this whole group on 2026-08-07 (a separate, legitimate prior request: "ensure all app-generated information is available on the Close Case page for manual entry when mechanics are not using the app").
- `Ticket Kanban`'s Reach step — plain editable `t:"number"` input, no `readonly` at all, ever.

Fixed by asking which policy was wanted (flat lock vs. lock-once-real vs. leave as-is) — chose **lock once real, editable only if blank**: `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` now carry `readonly:(s)=>hasRealEta(s.ETA/_TOW)` in `getRescueTicket`, and the equivalent inline check in `Ticket Kanban`'s `reach` stage. `hasRealEta(v)` is just "does a value exist at all" (`v!=null && String(v).trim()!==""`) — deliberately does NOT treat `0` as blank, since a vendor already at the breakdown location is a genuine `ETA:0`, not a missing value. This preserves the 2026-08-07 manual-entry fallback for a ticket the vendor's app never touched (still editable while genuinely blank), while protecting any value that's actually the real Google/Haversine calculation from being silently overwritten later.

`Ticket Kanban` didn't have function-form `readonly` support at all before this (only ever checked `f.readonly` as a raw boolean) — ported the same `isReadonly(f)` helper `getRescueTicket` already has, one call site (`buildField()`'s plain-input branch) updated to use it.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, same approach as prior entries): confirmed blank/null/undefined `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` stay editable, a real value (including `0`) locks, string-vs-number value shape doesn't matter, and the two fields' locks are independent of each other.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`, `Ticket Kanban.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Ticket locking: only one agent can work on a given ticket at a time (user request)

User request: "if two agents try to open the same ticket, only one agent can work on it at a time... other agents will not be allowed to open or edit the same ticket while it is being worked on." Confirmed scope via clarifying question first: **agent-vs-agent only, inside `getRescueTicket`** — `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` are untouched; each of those already only shows a vendor their own assigned tickets, so cross-vendor conflicts are far rarer than two agents sharing one dashboard of every ticket, and none of those three widgets has a comparable "open the wizard" lifecycle to hook a lock into anyway.

**New fields on `Create_Case`** (not yet created — see `FIELDS.md`'s own note, same "ships safely ahead of the field existing" pattern as `Agent_Wizard_Step` earlier the same day): `Locked_By` (Single Line — the holding agent's login email) and `Locked_At` (Date-Time — refreshed as a heartbeat, not just stamped once).

- **`activeLockOn(rec)`** — null if genuinely unlocked, if the lock is stale (no heartbeat in `LOCK_STALE_MS`, 15 min — treated as an abandoned/crashed session, safe to silently take over), or if it's already held by the CURRENT agent (reopening their own ticket, e.g. after a refresh, must never block themselves). Otherwise returns who's holding it and how long ago, for the prompt below.
- **`openTicket()`** — checks `activeLockOn()` against the just-refetched record (not the dashboard's own possibly-stale bulk copy), deliberately BEFORE any `state.*` mutation so declining leaves the dashboard exactly as it was. If held by someone else: `confirmBox()` shows who and how long ago ("X opened this ticket N minutes ago and may still be working on it") with a **"Take Over Anyway"** escape hatch — declining returns `false` and aborts; taking over (or an unlocked/stale/self-held ticket) calls `acquireLock()` and starts a 60s heartbeat (`startLockHeartbeat()`) that keeps `Locked_At` fresh for as long as the wizard stays open. Skipped entirely for an already-CLOSED ticket opened read-only (`state.closureReadOnly`) — viewing a closed ticket isn't "working on it," so it was never worth locking out other agents from that.
- **`backToDash()`** — the one function every "leave the wizard" path already routes through (Cancel, Save-and-exit, Final Closure's own back button, a blocked step's own auto-exit) — now releases the lock there too, best-effort/not awaited so navigation never waits on it.
- **`openTicket()`'s own return value** (`true`/`false`) lets its one caller that chains a follow-up action (the dashboard row's "Close" quick-action, which jumps straight to Agent Closure right after) tell "actually opened" apart from "declined and bailed out" — without this, declining would still jump into Agent Closure against stale/unrelated state.
- **`window.addEventListener("pagehide", ...)`** — best-effort release on an outright tab close, for the common case. Neither `pagehide` nor `beforeunload` reliably let an in-flight async request finish before a page actually tears down, so `LOCK_STALE_MS` (not this listener) is what actually guarantees recovery if a tab is simply closed.
- **`agentDisplayNameFor(email)`** — generalized out of the existing `agentDisplayName()` (which only ever resolved the CURRENT agent's own name) so the lock prompt can show whichever OTHER agent is holding it too; `agentDisplayName()` itself now just delegates to this for `LOGIN_EMAIL`, so the two can't drift into different matching rules over time.

**Known limitation, by design**: this is a soft lock, not a truly atomic one — a pure client-side widget can't guarantee two agents clicking the exact same ticket within the same few-hundred-millisecond window won't both pass the check before either write lands. Same tradeoff most soft-locking UIs (Google Docs, Notion, etc.) accept for the realistic goal here ("stop the common case," not "guarantee it under concurrent load"); a genuinely atomic version would need a server-side Deluge function doing a real compare-and-set, not attempted here.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, same approach as prior entries) — 6 scenarios covering: declining a live lock (blocked, no write, dashboard state untouched), taking over a live lock (opens, lock rewritten under the new agent), an unlocked ticket (opens silently, no prompt), a ticket locked by the current agent already (no prompt, refreshes silently), a stale lock past 15 minutes (silently taken over, no prompt), and an already-CLOSED ticket (lock check skipped entirely). A separate run confirmed `backToDash()` actually clears `Locked_By` and stops the heartbeat, and that it's a no-op for a view-only ticket that never acquired one. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — "Missing Fields & Field Behavior" spec: Accept Location/ETA/Reach fields/Cancel-to-breakdown distance, all now real editable fields in Agent-entering mode; two real gaps closed in vendor-side GPS capture (user-provided spec)

User provided a detailed spec covering the Accept and Reach/Cancel screens' field behavior. Audited every named field first (dedicated pass across all four widgets) before changing anything — most of the editability logic already existed via `acceptanceIsAgentManual()` (see that function's own 2026-08-11 entries above); the gaps were narrower than the spec's own length suggested, but real:

**Confirmed already correct, no change needed**: Accept Time, Distance-to-Breakdown, Cancel Time all already had a per-stage Agent-entering-mode-only editable copy (earlier 2026-08-11 work), and are read-only in Vendor-App mode exactly as the spec's item 3 asks.

**Real gaps found and fixed:**

1. **Accept Location (`RSP_Start_Latitude`/`Longitude`) and ETA (`ETA`/`ETA_TOW`) had NO Agent-entering-mode editable copy anywhere in the wizard** — only reachable after the fact via the Final Closure summary's click-to-edit popup, not from the Accept step itself where the spec places them. Added both to `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`, `when:acceptanceIsAgentManual`. ETA's copy keeps its existing `hasRealEta()` lock on top (see that fix's own 2026-08-11 entry above) — editable while genuinely blank, still protected once a real vendor-calculated value exists.
2. **A clock-time "Expected Arrival" display (spec's own "ETA Example: 3:45 PM") existed only on the Reach step, never on Accept** — added the same `_ExpectedArrival` computed field (reusing `expectedArrivalTime()`, unchanged) to `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow` too.
3. **Real bug fixed: `Reach_Time` had NO `Acceptance_Mode` gate anywhere** — the one field in the entire Final Closure "Field Activity Log" group with no `when:` condition at all, so it stayed editable there regardless of mode, violating the spec's own item 3 ("read-only for the agent" while the vendor is using the app). Added a real per-stage editable copy to `reach`/`reachTow` (agent-manual only) and excluded the always-on Closure row in that mode to match every sibling field's own pattern.
4. **Cancel Location (`Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Lon`) and Cancel_Distance had no per-stage editable copy either** — same gap as Accept Location, fixed the same way: added to `reach`/`reachTow`, agent-manual only, with matching exclusions added to the Closure-only groups.
5. **Reach never captured any GPS location at all — a genuinely missing capability, not just a missing UI gate.** Confirmed via audit: Accept and Cancel both already grab a live `getPositionSafe()` fix at their own click point; Reach (`markReachedRsr()`/`markReachedTow()`/technicianTicket's/driverTicket's own equivalents) never did. Added it — new fields `Reach_Location_Lat`/`Reach_Location_Lon` (shared, not split RSR/TOW) — captured in `vendorTicket`, `technicianTicket`, and `driverTicket`'s own Reach handlers, and shown/editable here (agent-manual mode) on `reach`/`reachTow`.
6. **"Round Trip Distance ... based on the location captured when REACH is clicked" (spec item 2) doesn't match what `Roundtrip_Distance` already measures** (a pre-existing, unrelated Office→Breakdown→Office calc using a fixed office constant, not a live fix) — confirmed with the user this needed a genuinely NEW field rather than redefining an existing one already relied on elsewhere. Added `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown` (RSR) / `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown_TOW` (TOW) — a plain one-way distance from the Reach-click GPS fix to the ticket's static breakdown location, computed in the same three vendor-side widgets, shown/editable here alongside Reach Location.
7. **"Distance: Cancel Location → Rescue/Breakdown Location" (spec item 2) never existed under any name** — confirmed via audit that the existing `Cancel_Distance` is a *different* measurement (Accept location → Cancel location, already documented as deliberately distinct in `FIELDS.md`). Added a genuinely new field, `Distance_Cancel_To_Breakdown` (shared), computed in the same three widgets' Cancel handlers, shown/editable here.
8. **Adjacent gap closed while already touching this exact area**: `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`Longitude` is reused for TOW's own "Reached Drop Location" GPS fix (documented pre-existing behavior, not new), but had no agent-manual editable copy for THAT meaning either — only the Accept-time meaning got one (item 1 above). Added a labeled "Drop Location" copy to `wipReachedTow` too, and excluded the Closure-only row in agent-manual mode to match, so the drop-time value doesn't become uneditable in that mode.

**Eight fields (fewer distinct ones — several are shared) added to the `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`/`reach`/`reachTow`/`wipReachedTow` STAGES entries**, each `when:acceptanceIsAgentManual`, matching every prior field added this way this session. **Matching exclusions added** to `DISTANCE_METRICS_FIELDS`/`FIELD_ACTIVITY_LOG_FIELDS` for every field that now has a per-stage copy, to avoid the double-display bug this file has hit (and fixed) repeatedly.

**Five brand-new `Create_Case` fields — not yet created in Zoho Studio**: `Reach_Location_Lat`, `Reach_Location_Lon` (Text), `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown`, `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown_TOW`, `Distance_Cancel_To_Breakdown` (Number) — see `FIELDS.md`'s own build-spec rows. Same "ships safely ahead of the field existing" pattern as every other new field this session on the `getRescueTicket` side (reads see `undefined`, fields render blank until real data exists); **the vendor-side write side is a genuinely different risk** — see `vendorTicket/README.md`'s own matching entry for why (these are bundled into the same save as `Reach_Time`/`Status`, unlike this session's other isolated single-field writes).

**Known minor quirk introduced by item 8**: `RSP_Start_Latitude`'s field name now has two differently-labeled live copies in agent-manual mode (Accept Location vs. Drop Location, different stages) — `CLOSURE_FIELD_INDEX`'s "last one wins" resolution for the click-to-edit popup means clicking one row can pop up a modal titled with the OTHER copy's label. Cosmetic only; it still edits the correct underlying field either way.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, same approach as prior entries) — 60 checks across every new/changed field's `when`/`readonly` behavior in both Vendor-App and Agent-manual mode, RSR and TOW. All passed. The vendor-side GPS/distance additions (`vendorTicket` directly; `technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` mirrored via two parallel agents, each independently syntax-checked and packed) were code-reviewed against the exact intended pattern rather than re-run through the Node-VM harness (those files have heavier native-geolocation/camera dependencies the harness doesn't stub).

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`, `vendorTicket.zip`, `technicianTicket.zip`, `driverTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-11 (later still) — User confirmed: all 11 pending Create_Case fields now created in Zoho Studio

User confirmed creating every field this session had flagged as not-yet-existing in `Create_Case`. `FIELDS.md` updated across both its build-spec table and narrative sections — every "NOT YET CREATED"/"NEEDS TO BE CREATED" marker replaced with "🟡 Created in Zoho 2026-08-11 (user-confirmed) — not yet independently verified live," matching this file's own established confidence-marker convention (a field existing is not the same claim as its real behavior being confirmed against live data flowing through it).

Two real documentation gaps caught and fixed in the same pass, found via a dedicated field-sweep audit (not just the fields from this specific "field creation" confirmation):
- **`Booking_Fee_Transaction_ID`** — added to this widget back on 2026-08-07 with a clear "does NOT exist yet in Create_Case" code comment, but somehow never made it into `FIELDS.md` at all until now. ### 2026-08-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown` rejected live with "Enter a valid number" — wrong Zoho field type, not a code bug

User hit this live on the very first "Reached Location" tap after creating the new fields: `{"Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown":"Enter a valid number for Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown"}`.

Root cause: **my own field-creation spec said "Number" for this field — that was wrong.** Zoho Creator's "Number" field type is integer-only; the code has always sent a one-decimal-place km value (`Math.round(x*10)/10`, e.g. `2.3`), same shape every other distance field in this app already uses (`Distance_To_Breakdown`, `Cancel_Distance`, `Roundtrip_Distance`, etc.) — those all work fine live, which means they were actually built as **Decimal** in the real Zoho schema despite `FIELDS.md` having labeled them "Number" too (an existing, unverified documentation guess that happened to not matter until now, since no NEW field had ever been created from that literal label before this session). `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown` was the first brand-new field built to my literal spec, so it's the first time this label mismatch actually broke something live.

**Fix — Zoho-side only, zero code changes**: change `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown` from Number to **Decimal** in Zoho Studio. `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown_TOW` and `Distance_Cancel_To_Breakdown` share the exact same spec and computation shape and would hit the identical error the first time either is exercised — flagged to fix all three now rather than one at a time. `FIELDS.md` corrected to say Decimal for all three (was the source of the wrong "Number" instruction in the first place).

**User confirmed all three fields corrected to Decimal in Zoho Studio (2026-08-11).** `FIELDS.md` updated accordingly. Not yet independently re-tested live since the correction — the next real "Reached Location"/"RSP Cancel" tap on a live ticket is the actual confirmation this is fully resolved (I have no live Zoho access to verify this myself); if the same "Enter a valid number" error resurfaces after this, that would mean either the type change didn't save correctly or something else is going on, and is worth reporting back immediately with the exact error text.

No other new field from this session's list uses a fractional value the same way — `Booking_Fee_Transaction_ID`/`Agent_Wizard_Step`/`Locked_By` are text, `Locked_At` is a timestamp, `Agent_ETA_Override`(`_TOW`) is a whole-minutes integer (genuinely fine as Number), and `Reach_Location_Lat`/`Lon` are Text (not Decimal, deliberately — see that field's own note on the digit-limit error a real Decimal GPS field hits). So this specific type mistake is isolated to the three distance-in-km fields.

### 2026-08-12 — Real bug fixed: assigning a vendor then clicking "Cancel" wrongly showed "RSP ON THE WAY" on the vendor's own dashboard, despite no acceptance ever happening (user-reported live)

User's exact repro: agent creates a ticket, assigns it to a vendor, then clicks the wizard's own plain "Cancel" button — the vendor's own dashboard immediately showed the ticket as "ON THE WAY," even though the vendor had never opened or accepted anything.

**Root cause**: `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`'s own `status()` used to unconditionally return `"RSP ON THE WAY"` (or `"RSP REJECT"` if already that) any time this step was *saved at all* — correct back when this stage was `viewOnly` (nothing but the vendor's own genuine accept action could ever trigger a save here), but wrong ever since a 2026-08-07 change made every field on it editable/saveable by the agent too. The trigger: assigning a vendor auto-advances the wizard onto this Accept screen (the `assignment` stage has no `canAdvance` gate); `cancelWizard()`'s own best-effort save (persists whatever's on screen when the agent clicks the wizard's "Cancel" button) then saved THIS screen — and its status function asserted "accepted" regardless of whether anything had actually happened.

**Found one level deeper while fixing this**: `reach`/`reachTow` had the *identical* flaw (`status()` unconditionally forcing `"RSP ON THE WAY"` on any save) — and since `acceptance` has no `canAdvance` gate either, the wizard auto-advances onto the Reach screen too, regardless of whether a real acceptance happened. So fixing `acceptance` alone would have left the exact same bug reachable via one more click (Cancel/Save on Reach instead of Accept). Fixed both.

**The fix, same shape in both stages**: only ever resolve a *new* outcome while the ticket is genuinely still awaiting one (`acceptance`: currently `"RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)"` or `"RSP REJECT"`; `reach`: currently `"RSP ON THE WAY"` or `"RSP CANCELLED"`) — anything else (most importantly a ticket that was never actually accepted, or one already further along like `WORK IN PROGRESS`) is never touched by a stray resave of an old screen. While genuinely awaiting an outcome: **Agent-entering mode** resolves it from whichever real field the agent actually filled in (`Service_Acceptance`(`_For_Tow`)/`Service_Reject_Time` on Accept, `Cancellation_Time` on Reach) — mirroring `vendorTicket`'s own `acceptService()`/`rejectService()`/`confirmCancelCommon()` exactly, a real action, not just "this screen got saved." **Vendor-App mode** never decides an outcome from either screen at all anymore — the vendor's own app is the sole authority, so a resave just preserves whatever Status is already stored (including reaffirming a real `"RSP ON THE WAY"`/`"RSP CANCELLED"` the vendor's own app already set, which is the one case each screen should still say so).

**Real regression caught and avoided while writing the fix**: an early draft of `reach`'s own fallback would have defaulted to re-asserting `"RSP ON THE WAY"` whenever agent-manual mode was off and no cancellation was found — which would have silently reverted an *already-cancelled* ticket (the vendor's own app genuinely cancelled it) back to "RSP ON THE WAY" the next time this old screen got resaved in Vendor-App mode. Caught before shipping — the final version just preserves `r.Status` unchanged in that branch instead of asserting anything.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, same approach as prior entries) — 18 scenarios covering: the exact reported repro, the one-level-deeper Reach version of the same bug, every genuine agent-manual accept/reject/cancel transition, reaffirming an already-correct status in Vendor-App mode, and the regression guard above (already-cancelled ticket surviving a stray Vendor-App-mode resave) plus already-further-along tickets (`WORK IN PROGRESS`) never being touched at all. All passed, both RSR and TOW branches.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-16 — Real bug fixed: Vendors field wrongly showed "This vehicle has no issues configured"; vendor picker's actual distance issue diagnosed

User's exact repro: on the Assignment step, "Preferred Vendor? No" showed zero vendors to choose from even though a specific vendor was expected to be nearby, with the confusing hint "This vehicle has no issues configured" underneath — a message that has nothing to do with vendors at all. Switching to "Yes" did show that vendor, but with no km/distance figure next to their name, unlike every other vendor in the list.

**Root cause #1 (confirmed, fixed): wrong hint text.** `buildField()`'s multiselect rendering has ONE shared branch for "this field currently has zero options to pick from," used by every multiselect field in the app — but the message shown there was hardcoded directly to Vehicle_Issue's own wording ("This vehicle has no issues configured"), rather than being specific to whichever field actually rendered empty. Since `Vendors1` already had its own contextual hint (`vendorPickerHint()` — "Showing vendors within Xkm...") that was simply never consulted in the empty-options case, an empty vendor list fell straight through to the wrong, Vehicle_Issue-specific string instead.

Fixed by introducing a new field property, `emptyHint`, kept deliberately separate from the existing `hint`: `hint` still only ever shows alongside real options (unchanged behavior for every other field), while `emptyHint` is a NEW dedicated message field definitions can set specifically for "I have zero options." `Vehicle_Issue` now carries its own explicit `emptyHint:"This vehicle has no issues configured"` instead of that wording living inside shared rendering code. The empty-options branch now prefers a field's own `emptyHint`, falls back to its regular `hint` if no `emptyHint` is set (covers `Vendors1` for free — `vendorPickerHint()`'s own message is already contextually correct whether the list is empty or not), and only falls back to a neutral "No options available" if neither is set.

**`vendorPickerHint()` also improved** to explicitly say so when the "No" (radius-filtered) mode's own list genuinely comes back empty — "No vendors found within X km of the breakdown location. Switch 'Preferred Vendor?' to 'Yes' to see every vendor regardless of distance — check the console for each vendor's own computed distance..." — instead of the previous generic "Showing vendors within Xkm..." wording, which was actively misleading when the answer was actually zero.

**Root cause #2 (diagnosed, not a code bug — data-dependent): the missing distance figure.** Traced `vendorInfo()`/`vendorDistanceKm()`/`vendorsFor()` in full: a vendor with no computable distance (`vinfo.distanceKm === null`) shows in "Yes" mode with no km figure (exactly the screenshot) and is silently excluded from "No" mode's radius filter entirely (can't confirm they're within radius if their location isn't known) — this is the correct, intentional behavior of the existing code (`vendorInfo()` already had its own live diagnostic warning for exactly this case, confirmed still firing correctly). This means the reported vendor's own `Latitude`/`Longitude` fields on `Vendors_Report` are most likely blank, or under a different field name than `CONFIG.vendorLatField`/`vendorLonField` expect. Added a new diagnostic to `vendorsFor()` — logs the ticket's own parsed breakdown `Latitude`/`Longitude` plus every vendor's own name/lat/lon/computed distance in one place on every render of this step, so checking the console will show directly whether it's that one vendor's own record missing coordinates, or something about the ticket's own breakdown location, or a genuine "just actually far away" — asked the user to check console output and report back rather than guessing further without live data.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, same approach as prior entries) — 9 checks: a nearby vendor correctly included and a far one excluded in "No" mode, a vendor with no lat/lon at all correctly excluded from "No" mode but shown (with no distance) in "Yes" mode, the new "No vendors found within Xkm" message shown exactly when the list is genuinely empty (not the wrong Vehicle_Issue text), Vehicle_Issue's own empty message unchanged and still scoped to itself only. All passed.

### 2026-08-17 — Vendor distance now uses the vendor's LIVE location when online, and a real Google Maps driving-distance instead of pure straight-line (user request, follow-up to the 2026-08-16 entry above)

The 2026-08-16 diagnosis above ("Root cause #2 — data-dependent") turned out, per the user's own follow-up, to actually be a missing capability rather than a data problem: *"when vendor is offline it is supposed to show distance from the vendor's static location (as in vendor table); when vendor is online it is supposed to show the distance from the vendor's current location (latest location from app)"*. An exhaustive audit confirmed no live-location tracking existed anywhere in this codebase before today — `Vendors_Report` only ever had the static `Address` composite field; nothing anywhere wrote a "current" position. User confirmed scope (**Vendors + Technicians + Drivers**, not just Vendors) and mechanism (**heartbeat every 2–3 minutes, plus use the Google API for the actual distance calculation** — a reversal of this project's earlier "no Google Maps, no API key" decision for the vendor picker).

- **Two brand-new fields**, not yet created in Zoho Studio: `Current_Latitude`/`Current_Longitude` on `Vendors_Report` (and the mirrored pair on `Technicians_Report`, see `technicianTicket`'s/`driverTicket`'s own matching entries) — see `../FIELDS.md`. Same "ships safely ahead of the field existing" pattern as every other pending field this session: reads return blank, writes fail silently.
- **`vendorLatLon()` renamed to `vendorStaticLatLon()`** (no behavior change) and a new **`vendorLiveLatLon()`** added, reading the two new fields.
- **`vendorInfo()` rewritten** to branch on `Availability_Status`: an Online vendor with a live fix already landed is located by that fix; an Online vendor with no fix yet, or an Offline vendor, falls back to the static `Address` exactly as before (never shows "no location" for someone genuinely available right now). The return value now also carries `locationSource: "live"|"static"|null`, shown as "(live)" next to the km figure in the picker (`vendorOptionHtml()`).
- **Google Distance Matrix ported from `vendorTicket`** (`GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`, `loadGoogleMaps()`, `googleDistanceMatrix()` — identical mechanism, already proven working there; `plugin-manifest.json`'s CSP already allowlisted `https://maps.googleapis.com` for this widget, but had zero actual usage until now). Rather than rewrite `optionsListFor()`→`vendorsFor()`→`vendorDistanceKm()`'s synchronous render pipeline to be async, added a small batched-cache layer instead: `refreshVendorDistances()` fires ONE Google API call per render (one origin — the ticket's breakdown location — against every vendor with a resolvable lat/lon as destinations), caches the results in `VENDOR_DISTANCE_CACHE` keyed by vendor id, and calls the existing `rerenderCurrentView()` once real driving distances land. `vendorDistanceKm()` reads this cache first and falls back instantly to the existing Haversine straight-line estimate if nothing's cached yet (new breakdown location, API call still in flight, or the call failed) — "show something immediately, upgrade once real data lands," same pattern already used elsewhere in this widget.
- **`vendorPickerHint()`** wording adjusted to no longer claim "straight-line Haversine only," since driving distance is now used where available.

**Not yet verified**: the real Google API call and real device geolocation can't be exercised outside a live browser/device session (same caveat as every other GPS-dependent feature this session) — syntax-checked via `node -e` against the `<script>` block only. Recommend testing with a vendor toggled Online in `vendorTicket` (heartbeat needs a few minutes to land a first fix) and watching this widget's own console for `refreshVendorDistances`'s log lines once created in Zoho.

Packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-17 (later) — Real bug fixed: reopening a ticket the vendor accepted via their own app landed the agent back on Accept, not Reach (user-reported live)

User's exact repro: **Vendor Accepting via App** mode — vendor clicks Accept on their own app, moves forward there to "Reached Breakdown Location"; on the agent's own "All Tickets" list the case correctly shows Status "On The Way"; but clicking into that case took the agent to the **Accept** screen, not **Reach**, where it should land given the ticket is already past acceptance.

**Root cause, confirmed**: `stageIndexForStatus()` (the plain Status-derived half of the 2026-08-11 resume-to-exact-step feature — see that entry above) picks the FIRST stage in the wizard's own path whose `statuses` list contains the ticket's current Status. `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow` and `reach`/`reachTow` BOTH listed `"RSP ON THE WAY"` in their own `statuses` arrays — correct for `reach`/`reachTow` (that's genuinely the status meaning "accepted, now awaiting arrival"), but a leftover from when `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow` were still `viewOnly` (pre-2026-08-07): back then, landing on Accept to just *view* the already-accepted state was harmless. Since `acceptance` sits earlier in the wizard path than `reach`, it always won the match — regardless of whether the vendor accepted via their own app or the agent typed it in manually here in "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" mode. `resumeStepIndex()`'s own persisted-step override (`Agent_Wizard_Step`) didn't save this either: it only ever pulls the resume index UP via `Math.max()`, never down, and a stale/absent persisted step (the normal case for a ticket the agent hasn't opened since before the vendor's own accept came in) just passes the already-wrong status-derived index straight through.

**Fix**: `acceptance` and `acceptanceTow` no longer list `"RSP ON THE WAY"` in their own `statuses` — `reach`/`reachTow` are now the sole owner of that status for resume purposes, since once Status is genuinely "RSP ON THE WAY," Accept is done either way and Reach is always the correct current screen. Confirmed via full audit of every stage's `statuses` array (`../FIELDS.md`-style status list cross-check) that this was the *only* such overlap in the whole `STAGES` table — every other status value belongs to exactly one stage's `statuses` list. Doesn't touch the separate persisted-step resume mechanism at all: an agent genuinely mid-edit on the Accept screen (navigated there, hasn't saved yet) still resumes there via `Agent_Wizard_Step`, since that match is by stage key, not by `.statuses`.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 11 checks covering both RSR and TOW branches: reopening with Status "RSP ON THE WAY" and no persisted step now lands on Reach/ReachTow; a stale persisted step of `"assignment"` or even `"acceptance"` itself still correctly lands on Reach once Status has moved past it; `"RSP IRA..."`/`"RSP REJECT"` still correctly resolve to Assignment (unaffected); `"RSP CANCELLED"` still resolves to Reach (unaffected); and an agent genuinely mid-edit on Accept (Status still `"RSP IRA..."`, `Agent_Wizard_Step="acceptance"`) still correctly resumes on Accept. All passed.

Packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-17 (later still) — On-Demand Billing split, Agent Closure GST/Extra Payable/vendor-payout distance calcs, and a new Operations Manager role + Fleet Map (user-provided spec, fields created in Zoho 2026-08-17)

User shared a detailed requirement table (Booking Fee page, Closure page, Vendor table, Agent Closure page, Agent Closure button validations/actions, and a new "Operations Manager" user type) and asked for the exact fields to create in Zoho first, then to proceed with implementation once those were in place — 27 new fields + one new `Closure_Status` option, see `../FIELDS.md` for the full create-ready list and per-field notes. All additive — nothing existing was renamed, retyped, or removed; every new field/screen degrades safely (blank/no-op) if it somehow isn't there yet, same discipline as every other field this session.

**Booking Fee (Quote step)** — new `Client_Charge`/`Rescue_Charge` fields, a new row right below Booking Fee/Remaining Fee. Splits the total fee between what a corporate/contracted **Client** is billed vs. this company's own **Rescue Charge**, prep for the future Client Billing/Vendor Payment reconciliation work (same territory as the earlier accounting-integration estimate). Reuses the existing `Client==="ON DEMAND"` special value `Client_Tracking_Id` already keys off — both fields null/locked for that walk-in Client, live-recomputed otherwise (`Rescue_Charge = Total_Service_Fee − Client_Charge`), same pattern as `Remaining_Fee`.

**Closure_Status** gets a 4th option, `Cancelled - Billable` — supersedes a 2026-07-30 decision (see that entry above) that deliberately kept this to 3 options; a new requirement (billable cancellations need their own vendor-distance calc) makes the split matter now. `computeClosureTSF()`'s own fee-bearing check (renamed `closureCountsAsFeeBearing()`) now covers all three of Completed/Cancelled/Cancelled-Billable.

**Vendor Fleet Location / Vendor Round Trip Distance / Vendor Distance** — three new fields in the existing "Distance & ETA Metrics" group, computed ONCE at Agent Closure time (`runClosureDistanceCalculations()`, called from `saveClosure()`), not live-rendered like the rest of that group:
- `Vendor_Fleet_Location_Lat`/`Lon` — whichever of the assigned vendor's own (new, capped-at-5) `Base_Location_N_Lat`/`Lon` pairs is nearest (crow-flight) to the breakdown location.
- `Vendor_Round_Trip_Distance` (TOW only) — Google API multi-leg: fleet location → breakdown → drop → fleet location when Completed, or fleet location → breakdown → fleet location when Cancelled-Billable. Forced null for Repair.
- `Vendor_Distance` (Repair-completed only) — the one-way equivalent, fleet location → breakdown. Forced null for TOW.
- Backfill-only by design (never overwrites an already-populated value) — including a **backfill for the existing `Roundtrip_Distance` field** if it's still empty at Closure (Office ↔ Breakdown[↔ Drop] ↔ Office, "Office" being the same already-hardcoded `OFFICE_LAT`/`OFFICE_LON` that field always used).
- New `legDistanceKm()` helper sums a chain of Google Distance Matrix legs in one batched call, falling back to Haversine per-leg if Google's unavailable — same "show something, upgrade if possible" tolerance as the vendor-picker distance work from earlier today.

**Agent Closure page** — `Extra_Payable` (plain number), `GST_Name`/`GST_Address` (tied to the existing `GST_Number`, auto-fetch **deferred** — plain manually-typed fields for now, per user decision), `Google_Feedback_Requested` (checkbox — a real WhatsApp send is wired via the existing `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` mechanism, `sendGoogleFeedbackRequest()`; the template name and `GOOGLE_REVIEW_LINK` are both unconfirmed placeholders, same confidence level as every other WhatsApp template guess in this app).

**Agent Closure validations** — "Closure Status required" and "Total Service Fee = Booking + Remaining" were already structurally guaranteed by existing code; the one genuinely new check blocks the save if `Client_Charge > 0` and `Client_Charge + Rescue_Charge` no longer matches the Closure-time Total Service Fee (a Refund applied at Closure can shift the final TSF after Rescue_Charge was last computed at Quote) — surfaces a clear toast instead of silently re-deriving a financial number.

**Zoho Books / Airtable** — deliberate no-op stubs (`sendToZohoBooks()`/`sendToAirtable()`), called from `saveClosure()` so the call sites already exist; both need real credentials (Zoho Books org+OAuth, Airtable API key+base/table, plus a Deluge Custom API proxy for Airtable — this widget's CSP blocks a direct browser call to airtable.com) that don't exist yet, per explicit 2026-08-17 user decision ("fields/UI now, wiring later").

**Operations Manager role + Fleet Map** — new `User_Type` field on `Agent_Report` (`Agent`/`Operations Manager`), resolved once at boot (`isOperationsManager()`) via the same lookup `agentDisplayName()` already uses. A brand-new "Ops Map" nav button/view (hidden for every other agent) shows every Vendor/Technician/Driver on a Google Map, colored 🔴 on a case / 🟢 free / ⚫ offline, with an Own-Mechanic/Tie-Up filter (vendors only — `Technicians_Report` has no equivalent field) and a click-for-details popup (last ping/last online). "On a case" is computed live from the current ticket list each time the map loads, not a stored flag, to avoid drift. Reuses `OPTIONS.vendors`/`OPTIONS.technicians` (already loaded at boot) and `vendorInfo()`'s own online/live-vs-static location logic — no new report fetch for location data, one extra ticket-list fetch for the on-case computation.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, 34 checks): `nearestVendorBaseLocation()` picks the genuinely closer base and skips blank ones; `legDistanceKm()` sums Haversine-fallback legs correctly with no network; the full `runClosureDistanceCalculations()` flow across TOW-Completed/TOW-Cancelled-Billable/TOW-plain-Cancelled/Repair-Completed/Repair-Not-Converted, confirming each field is forced null, computed, or correctly left untouched per branch, plus the backfill-guard (an already-populated value is never overwritten) and the no-vendor/no-coordinates early-outs; `agentUserTypeFor()`/`isOperationsManager()` role resolution; and the full `renderWizard()` Quote-step render-prep for Client_Charge/Rescue_Charge (normal Client, not-yet-entered, ON DEMAND forcing both null, case/whitespace-insensitive matching). All passed.

**Not independently verified**: the real Google Distance Matrix responses, the Ops Map's actual Google Maps rendering/markers, and the WhatsApp Google-feedback send all need a live browser/device session and (for the WhatsApp send) the real approved template name — same caveat pattern as every other GPS/WhatsApp-dependent feature this session.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-18 — Real bug fixed: WIP-family screens (WIP, Loading, Reached Drop, Unloading, Payment) had no Acceptance_Mode lock at all — every field stayed editable regardless of "Vendor Accepting via App" (user-reported live)

User's exact repro: vendor accepts a case via their own app, clicks Reach, then Reach's own action writes `Status:"WORK IN PROGRESS"` — correctly landing the agent on the **WIP** step when they open that ticket from "All Tickets" (confirmed this part of the navigation is right, not a bug — `technicianTicket`'s `markReached()` really does write that status directly on Reach). The actual bug: once there, every field and photo on WIP was fully editable regardless of `Acceptance_Mode` — it should only be editable under "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor", locked under "Vendor Accepting via App", exactly like `acceptance`/`reach` already work.

**Root cause, confirmed**: the 2026-08-11 pass that added `readonly:(s)=>!acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)` to every outcome field on `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`/`reach`/`reachTow` never touched their own downstream siblings — `wip`, `wipLoadingTow`, `wipReachedTow`, `wipUnloadingTow`, and `payment`. Each of those stages' own "viewOnly REMOVED 2026-08-07" comment says "every remaining vendor/technician/driver-side stage is now editable here too," but that 2026-08-07 change only ever made fields editable — the later 2026-08-11 pass that added the Acceptance_Mode *lock* on top of that editability simply stopped one stage too early. Only each stage's own handful of brand-new distance fields (`Roundtrip_Distance`/`Office_To_Mechanic`/etc., added 2026-08-11) had any Acceptance_Mode awareness at all, via `when:acceptanceIsAgentManual` — hidden entirely in vendor-app mode, but every *original* field (photos, Issue Resolved, Expense Amount, Odometer Reading, Handover details, Payment Method, Receipt Photo, etc.) had no gate whatsoever.

**Fix**: added `readonly:(s)=>!acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)` to every agent-facing field across all five stages — `wip` (Issue_Resolved, Unresolved_Note, Image_Upload3/4, Expense_Amount, Expense_Photo, Rejection_Reason, RSP_Completion_Time), `wipLoadingTow` (Pre_service_Photo, Image_Upload_On_truck, VCRF, Image_Upload6, Rejection_Reason, RSP_Completion_Time), `wipReachedTow` (Odometer_Reading, Drop_Location_Photo, Toll_Charges1, Drop_Location_Arrival_Time), `wipUnloadingTow` (Unloaded_Images, VCRF_Image, Handover_Image, Handover_to_Name/Number/Designation, Navigate_To_Drop_Link, RSP_Completion_Time), and `payment` (Payment_Method_Final, Payment_received, QR_Code, Send_Payment_Link1, Upload_Photo_of_receipt). `payment`'s own `Total_Service_Fee`/`Payment_Status` stay unconditionally `readonly:true` exactly as before (display-only regardless of mode, correct already). Reported only on WIP, but fixed across the whole downstream family — leaving the other four would just relocate the identical complaint to the next screen down the path.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 62 checks: every field named above locks under "Vendor Accepting via App" and unlocks under "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" across all five stages; `payment`'s always-readonly display fields are confirmed unchanged; `wip`'s own pre-existing `when`-gated distance quartet (hidden, not just locked, in vendor-app mode) is confirmed untouched by this fix. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-18 (later) — Real bug fixed: the stepper/progress-bar visually un-greened already-passed steps when the agent navigated back to review an earlier one (user-reported live)

User's exact ask: "the tracking bar must always show GREEN till the last status that has been set. if we move back then the page may move but the status in the status bar will remain the same."

**Root cause, confirmed**: `renderWizard()`'s stepper (`done`/`✓` per step) and the progress-bar fill (`pbarFill`'s width) were both computed off `state.stepIndex` — wherever the agent's own view currently sits — instead of `state.maxStepIndex`, the furthest step this ticket has genuinely reached (seeded from the real Status/`Agent_Wizard_Step` on open, and only ever bumped upward on a real save — `jumpTo()`/`prevStep()` never touch it). Since `jumpTo(i)` only ever changes `stepIndex`, navigating back to review an earlier step immediately shrank the green fill and un-checked every step between the new position and the real frontier, even though the ticket's actual progress hadn't changed at all.

**Fix**: both now key off `state.maxStepIndex` for "done"/checkmark and fill-width, while `state.stepIndex` still drives which single step shows the "active" highlight. The two are independent and can combine on the same step — e.g. navigating back to review an already-passed step now correctly shows it as both `done` (green ✓) *and* `active` (highlighted, since it's what's on screen), rather than the old either/or. The step exactly at `maxStepIndex` itself — the ticket's real current, not-yet-completed status — correctly shows neither done nor active once the agent looks away from it (it's genuinely not finished yet, regardless of where they're currently browsing).

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, rendering the actual DOM output and inspecting it) — 14 checks: a fresh resume at the frontier renders identically to before this fix (no regression for the normal case); navigating back preserves every already-passed step's green checkmark and the fill-bar width; the frontier step itself correctly shows as neither done nor active once no longer being viewed; a step that's both before the frontier and currently being viewed correctly shows both `done` and `active` together; steps beyond `maxStepIndex` stay locked throughout. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-18 (later still) — Real bug fixed: Status could regress backward when an agent navigated back and re-saved an earlier step (user-reported live)

User's exact repro: ticket's Status has already genuinely progressed to "final payment" (`REMAINING FEE DUE`); agent navigates back 2 pages (now easy to do since yesterday's stepper fix keeps every already-reached step visibly green and clickable) to Reach and clicks Save there — Status must **not** revert to "on the way." Generalized per the user's own framing: "status can only move forward, status must never move backward."

**Root cause, confirmed via a full audit of every stage's own `status` value**: `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow`/`reach`/`reachTow` already had a regression guard (2026-08-12) and `create` had one too (`EARLY_STATUSES`, 2026-08-11), but eight more stages never got one at all — five had a **plain unconditional static string** (`assignment` → always `"RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)"`, `wip` → always `"WORK IN PROGRESS"`, `wipLoadingTow` → always `"LOADING"`, `wipReachedTow` → always `"TO DROP LOCATION"`, `wipUnloadingTow` → always `"UNLOADING"`, `payment` → always `"RSP CLOSED"`), and two more had an unconditional function (`quoteStatus()`, `locations`'s own status computation) that never checked what the ticket's *real* current Status already was before overwriting it. `assignment` was the most dangerous of these — any already-accepted, in-progress, or even paid ticket that got navigated back to Assignment and resaved would have been slammed all the way back to "awaiting a vendor's response," undoing everything that had genuinely happened since.

**Fix**: a new shared helper, `statusStillEarly(r, stageStatuses)`, generalizes the exact pattern `acceptance`/`reach`/`create` had each independently arrived at for their own equivalent gap — true only while the record's current Status is blank (brand new ticket, nothing to protect yet) or is itself one of the given stage's own resume `statuses`. Every one of the eight newly-vulnerable stages now checks this first and returns `r.Status` unchanged otherwise; `quoteStatus()` and `locations`'s status function got the same guard inline. `payment` is the one asymmetric case (its write value, `"RSP CLOSED"`, is a genuine forward transition from its own entry status `"REMAINING FEE DUE"`, not a copy of it) — the guard there only stops a stray resave once the ticket's already past Payment (`RSP CLOSED`/`CLOSED`) from reasserting anything. `agentClosure`/`saveClosure()` was deliberately **not** touched — closing a ticket early (Not Converted/Cancelled) via the standalone "Close Case" button is an intentional admin escape hatch, not a pipeline-order violation.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 30 checks: the user's own exact scenario; every one of the eight newly-guarded stages confirmed to both (a) compute completely normally on a genuine first pass (no regression in the intended/common case) and (b) refuse to regress Status once the ticket has moved further along, including all the way from Assignment/Quote/Locations up through Payment/RSP CLOSED/CLOSED; the legitimate `RSP REJECT` ↔ `RSP IRA` re-assignment cycle confirmed still works (not blocked by the new guard); and the pre-existing `acceptance`/`reach` protection confirmed unaffected by this change. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 — Re-confirmed the Final Payment Collection page's Acceptance_Mode lock (user request); found and fixed one field the 2026-08-18 pass missed

User asked to specifically re-confirm the same lock already applied to WIP/Loading/Reached Drop/Unloading/Payment on 2026-08-18 for the Final Payment Collection page. Re-read every field on `payment` directly against the live code rather than assuming the earlier fix was complete.

**Result: four of five outcome fields were already correctly locked** (`Payment_Method_Final`, `Payment_received`, `QR_Code`, `Send_Payment_Link1`, `Upload_Photo_of_receipt` — all gated `readonly:(s)=>!acceptanceIsAgentManual(s)` since 2026-08-18), and `Total_Service_Fee`/`Payment_Status` were correctly unconditionally read-only, as they should be (pure re-displays, never independently editable by anyone). **One real gap found**: `Remaining_Fee` had no gate at all — editable regardless of `Acceptance_Mode`, the only field on this page the 2026-08-18 pass missed. Navigation itself was also double-checked: `"REMAINING FEE DUE"` only appears in `payment`'s own `statuses` list, no overlap with any other stage, so landing here from "All Tickets" is correct.

**Fix**: `Remaining_Fee` is now unconditionally `readonly:true`, matching `Total_Service_Fee` right above it (both are pure re-displays of values computed at Quote — `Total_Service_Fee − Booking_Fee` — not vendor-outcome data anyone should retype, even in Agent-Entering mode, so it gets the stricter always-locked treatment rather than the mode-conditional gate its other siblings on this page use).

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 15 checks covering all 8 fields on the page: the 5 mode-gated fields confirmed locked/unlocked correctly in both modes, all 3 always-readonly fields (including the newly-fixed `Remaining_Fee`) confirmed unconditionally locked, and an exhaustive field-name check confirming no other ungated field exists on this stage. All passed.
