### 2026-08-19 (later) — Real bug fixed: a raw Zoho record ID showed up as a selectable "vendor" in the Assignment picker (user-reported live screenshot)

User's exact repro: "Preferred Vendor? Yes" listed a row showing a raw record ID (`4488810000000105082`) instead of a name, with an "UNKNOWN" status pill.

**Root cause, confirmed**: `pull()`'s own existing fallback — when none of `Vendors_Report`'s label fields (`vendor_name`/`Vendor_Name`/`Name`) resolve on a given record, it falls back to showing that record's raw Zoho ID as its "name" instead of silently vanishing (deliberate, so a genuine field-name mismatch stays visible somewhere — `vendorInfo()`'s own `console.warn` already fires for exactly this case). That fallback is useful as a diagnostic, but `vendorsFor()` was still offering that record as a normal, selectable option in the picker — an agent has no way to know who they'd actually be assigning the ticket to.

**Fix**: `vendorsFor()` now filters out any vendor whose `label` equals its own `id` (the raw-ID fallback signal) before building its own list, in both "Preferred Vendor: Yes" (show everyone) and "No" (radius-filtered) modes. The underlying diagnostic — `vendorInfo()`'s own console warning dumping the record's real keys/values — is untouched, so the bad/incomplete Zoho record is still traceable; only the agent-facing picker changes. Once that vendor's own `vendor_name`/`Vendor_Name`/`Name` field is actually filled in in Zoho, it'll resolve a real label and reappear normally — nothing about this fix is permanent for that specific record.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 5 checks: an unnamed vendor is excluded from the picker in both Preferred-Vendor modes, while genuinely named vendors remain shown and unaffected. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (later still) — Real bug fixed: Final Closure only ever showed the FIRST uploaded photo for a multi-file field, even with several attached (user-reported live)

User's exact repro: uploaded multiple photos for a "min 2, max 4" multi-file field (e.g. Pre-service Photo, `Image_Upload3` on `wip`) — Final Closure's own summary showed only one thumbnail.

**Root cause, confirmed**: this was a known, deliberate simplification from 2026-08-07's own fix (see `zohoFileDownloadUrl()`'s own header comment at the time), not a new regression — that fix made multi-file photo previews work AT ALL (they were completely broken before it, always `null`), but explicitly used only `rawValue[0]`, "matching the existing summary UI (one thumbnail slot per field row regardless of how many files are actually attached)." Both halves of that design — one URL builder call and one placeholder per row — always capped the summary at exactly one photo, no matter how many were genuinely uploaded.

**Fix**: `zohoFileDownloadUrl()` now takes an explicit `index` parameter (defaults to 0, so every pre-existing call site is unaffected); a new `fileCountFor()` helper reports how many files a field's raw value actually represents. `closureFieldRowHtml()` renders one `data-thumb-field`/`data-thumb-index` placeholder **per file** instead of a single fixed one, and `hydrateFilePreviewsIn()` reads each placeholder's own index and resolves its own real photo through `zohoFileDownloadUrl()` — every uploaded photo gets its own `<img>` now, not just the first. The legacy `apiDownloadFile()` fallback path (already documented as broken for real binary files, kept only as a last resort) is field-level only and can't be extended to address individual files — noted inline, not a new limitation.

**Not touched**: `buildField()`'s own file-input rendering (used by the live wizard step and the field-edit popup, `#fieldModalBody`) has the identical one-thumbnail-only limitation, but the user's report was specifically about the Final Closure summary — flagging this as a related, not-yet-fixed spot rather than changing an active upload screen without it being asked for.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 17 checks: `fileCountFor()` across arrays/single-values/the `true` placeholder/blank; `zohoFileDownloadUrl()` resolving three genuinely different URLs for three different indices (the actual bug) plus its own backward-compatible default and out-of-range safety; `closureFieldRowHtml()` rendering exactly 3 placeholders for 3 uploaded files, exactly 1 for a single file (no regression), 0 for no file, and correctly suppressing thumbnails entirely for a freshly-picked-but-not-yet-saved local file. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later) — Multiple Final Closure photos now lay out horizontally, not stacked vertically (user request)

Follow-up to the same-day multi-photo fix above — once every uploaded photo actually got its own thumbnail, they stacked straight down the page (the outer `.crow-photo` row is a flex column, so each new placeholder was just another sibling below the last). User asked for them side by side instead.

**Fix**: the per-file thumbnails are now wrapped in a new `.crow-thumbs` container (`display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:10px`) instead of sitting as loose direct children of the column-flex `.crow-photo` row — they flow left-to-right and wrap onto a new line once they run out of horizontal space, while the label/value line still sits below the whole group as its own row. A single-photo field renders identically to before (one item in a flex row lays out the same as one item in a column) — no visible change for the common case. `.crow-thumb.big`'s own CSS was deliberately left untouched, since that same class is also used by the live wizard's own single-photo field and the field-edit popup — this change only affects how the closure summary specifically groups multiple photos together.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — the existing multi-photo suite re-run plus one new check confirming the `.crow-thumbs` wrapper is actually present when multiple photos exist; all 18 checks passed, including the single-photo and zero-photo cases still rendering exactly as before.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later still) — Real bug fixed: tickets at "Final Payment" were completely invisible on the "All Tickets" dashboard (user-reported live)

User's exact report: a ticket at Final Payment Collection ("REMAINING FEE DUE") isn't linked to any of the dashboard's own groupings, so it never shows up on "All Tickets" — asked for it to display under "Work In Progress."

**Root cause, confirmed**: `BUCKETS` (the dashboard's own 4 report-list groupings, separate from each STAGES entry's own resume `statuses`) never listed `"REMAINING FEE DUE"` in any of its four `statuses` arrays — the exact same class of gap as the 2026-08-03 `"RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)"` fix documented right above it in this same file. `bucketOfStatus()` returned `-1` for it, and `renderReportLists()` silently drops any ticket whose bucket index is `-1` — invisible on the dashboard entirely, findable only via phone/Case-ID search.

**Fix**: `"REMAINING FEE DUE"` added to the "Work In Progress" bucket, per the user's own explicit ask — the job itself is done, but the ticket isn't finished (payment not yet collected), the same "still active" spirit as everything else already in that bucket.

**Also found and fixed during the same audit (not separately reported)**: `"CLOSED"` (`agentClosure`'s own final write, via `saveClosure()`) was ALSO missing from every bucket — meaning a ticket that had gone all the way through Agent Closure, the most finished state a ticket can be in, was even more invisible than the reported gap. Added to "RSP Closed" (its natural home — the bucket's own name/sub already describe "closed out" tickets, and the lesser-closed "RSP CLOSED" was already there).

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 26 checks: both newly-added statuses resolve to their intended bucket; every other status that was already correctly bucketed (all 19 of them, across all four buckets) still resolves to the exact same bucket as before this change — no regression; case-insensitivity, blank, and genuinely-unknown status handling all confirmed unaffected. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later again) — New: Distance to Breakdown auto-calculates the moment "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" is chosen (user request)

User's exact ask: when the agent picks "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor," `Distance_To_Breakdown`(`_TOW`) should auto-calculate via Google API — the assigned vendor's own **static** location (never live — an agent entering manually has no live GPS fix to trust in the first place) to the ticket's own entered breakdown location — triggered the moment that mode is chosen, and the field stays fully editable afterward.

**Implementation**: a new `calcAcceptanceDistanceToBreakdown()`, fire-and-forget async, hooked into the END of `seedAcceptanceVendorFields()` — the same function that already seeds `Assigned_Vendor`/`Vendor_Email*` the moment this mode is picked, and already runs on every render of `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow` (so it naturally re-fires once a vendor gets picked, if the agent chose the mode before picking one). Reuses `legDistanceKm()` (a 2-point chain = a single Google Distance Matrix call, Haversine fallback) — the same mechanism already proven for the Agent Closure vendor-payout distance calc — and `vendorStaticLatLon()` for the vendor's registered address, same function the Assignment-step picker already uses for an Offline vendor.

- **RSR** writes `Distance_To_Breakdown`; **TOW** writes `Distance_To_Breakdown_TOW` — branch picked the same way `DISTANCE_METRICS_FIELDS`' own matching pair already is.
- **Fires only while the field is genuinely blank**, both before starting and again when the result lands — "field remains editable" from the moment it's filled, exactly as asked: a value the agent already typed (before or during the calculation) is never overwritten, and once auto-filled, re-rendering never touches it again.
- Guarded by a fetch-key (`recordId|fieldName|vendorId|lat,lon`) so the same combo never re-fires while a request is already in flight.
- No vendor assigned yet, or no breakdown location captured yet → skips silently, retrying naturally on the next render once both exist.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 8 checks: RSR and TOW both auto-fill their own correct field (and leave the other branch's field untouched); "Vendor Accepting via App" mode never triggers the calc at all; an already-present value (manual or from an earlier run) is never overwritten; missing vendor and missing breakdown location both skip gracefully with no throw; and re-rendering after a value already landed leaves it untouched (idempotent). All passed.

**Not independently verified**: the real Google Distance Matrix response — same caveat as every other Google-API-dependent feature this session, needs a live browser session with a real breakdown location and assigned vendor to confirm end-to-end.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later again still) — New: Accept Time auto-stamps the moment "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" is chosen (user request)

User's exact ask: `Service_Acceptance`(`_For_Tow`) should auto-populate with the current timestamp the instant this mode is picked, and stay editable afterward.

**Implementation**: added right next to the Distance to Breakdown auto-calc in `seedAcceptanceVendorFields()` (same trigger, same function, same day) — `fmtDateTime(new Date())` stamped into `Service_Acceptance` (RSR) or `Service_Acceptance_For_Tow` (TOW) the moment Agent-manual mode is active, same "only while genuinely blank, never overwrite" discipline as every other field this function seeds. Auto-filling it here means simply choosing "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" and then saving is now, on its own, enough to advance Status to `"RSP ON THE WAY"` — mirroring how the vendor's own single Accept click in their app does both at once. Verified this end-to-end against the real `computeStatus()` path, not just the field seeding in isolation.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 10 checks: RSR and TOW both auto-stamp their own correct field with a genuine current timestamp, with no vendor/location needed first; "Vendor Accepting via App" mode never triggers it; an already-entered value (manual or from an earlier render) is never overwritten; a second render after the stamp already landed leaves it untouched; and an end-to-end check confirming the auto-stamp genuinely advances Status via `computeStatus()` for both branches. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later again still further) — "Four ETA fields" behavior discussed and fixed: ETA to Breakdown now auto-calculates too, which makes Expected Arrival Time resolve on both Accept and Reach for free

User asked to discuss and fix the interaction between four fields under "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor": ETA to Breakdown (Accept), Expected Arrival Time (Accept), Expected Arrival Time (Reach), Agent ETA Override (Reach).

**The behavior, worked through and confirmed**:
- **ETA to Breakdown (mins)** — was NOT auto-calculated (unlike Distance to Breakdown, fixed earlier the same day) — the agent had to type it in by hand, meaning "Expected Arrival Time" had nothing to compute from even after Distance/Accept Time started auto-filling.
- **Expected Arrival Time (Accept)** — a computed field (`expectedArrivalTime()`), derived from `Service_Acceptance` + `ETA` (or `Agent_ETA_Override` if set). Already correctly reactive — it just never had real inputs to work with.
- **Expected Arrival Time (Reach)** — the exact same computed function/inputs, just displayed a second time on a later screen. Identical behavior to Accept's own copy by construction.
- **Agent ETA Override (mins) (Reach)** — already correctly designed: a manual, always-editable-in-Agent-mode adjustment that `expectedArrivalTime()` already prefers over the plain `ETA` the moment it's set. This is the agent's own closer-to-actual-travel-time correction. Left untouched — nothing wrong with it.

**Fix**: extended the same Google Distance Matrix call `calcAcceptanceDistanceToBreakdown()` already makes for Distance to Breakdown to also capture the API's own `duration` value and auto-fill `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` — one call now fills both fields instead of just distance. Once that lands alongside the already-auto-stamped `Service_Acceptance`, **both copies of Expected Arrival Time resolve automatically with no separate code change** — they were always correctly wired, just starved of real inputs.

**One thing preserved on purpose, not silently changed**: `ETA`/`ETA_TOW` already had its own rule from 2026-08-11 — `readonly:(s)=>hasRealEta(s.ETA)` locks it **permanently** the instant it holds a real value ("the eta calculated as per google has to be saved for further reporting... has not to edit this originally calculated eta"), unlike `Distance_To_Breakdown`/`Service_Acceptance`, which stay editable forever. This fix respects that existing rule rather than overriding it — it only ever fills ETA while genuinely blank, same as everything else this function seeds; the field's own pre-existing readonly gate is what locks it afterward, exactly as already designed. Flagging this explicitly since it's an asymmetry across the "four fields," not a bug.

**Honest limitation, not swept under the rug**: straight-line (Haversine) distance has no way to estimate real driving *time* — if Google's own API call fails or is unavailable, `Distance_To_Breakdown` still falls back to Haversine (as before), but `ETA` simply stays blank for the agent to type in by hand, exactly as if this feature didn't exist for that one field. Only a genuinely successful Google Distance Matrix response can auto-fill ETA.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 13 checks: Distance auto-fills via Haversine fallback while ETA correctly stays blank when Google's unavailable (both branches); Expected Arrival Time resolves once Service_Acceptance + ETA both exist, identically on both its Accept and Reach copies; Agent ETA Override still correctly overrides plain ETA; ETA's own lock-once-real rule confirmed unchanged (editable while blank, locked once real); and re-rendering after ETA already has a real value leaves it untouched. All passed.

**Not independently verified**: a genuinely successful Google Distance Matrix response (with a real `duration` value) needs a live browser session — same caveat as every other Google-API-dependent feature this session.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later again still further again) — Real bug fixed: Remaining Fee could still silently drift after being paid online, on both Quote and Final Closure (user-reported live)

User's exact ask: "Final Payment page and Closure page -> after remaining fee payment is completed by online mode (Zoho Payments), the remaining fee cannot be edited."

**Root cause, confirmed**: `Remaining_Fee` itself was already unconditionally `readonly:true` everywhere (can't be typed into directly, on either the Quote or Payment screen) — but its recorded VALUE was still silently **recomputed** from `Total_Service_Fee`/`Booking_Fee` on every render of the Quote step, and `Total_Service_Fee` itself had **no lock at all**. An agent who navigated back to Quote (easy now thanks to the stepper's own "stays green" fix from a few days ago) and changed `Total_Service_Fee`, then saved, would silently regress the recorded Remaining Fee away from whatever amount the customer had already genuinely paid online — even though the field itself looked read-only the whole time.

**Fix**: a new `remainingFeePaidOnline(s)` helper — true only once the vendor/technician/driver's own `confirmPaymentReceived()` action has genuinely fired with the `"Payment Gateway"` method (confirmed live in `vendorTicket`: writes `Payment_Method_Final`/`Payment_received`/`Payment_Status`/`Status` all in ONE atomic payload, so the first two are always set together). Deliberately does **not** key off `Payment_Status` alone — that field is reused for both the Booking Fee (Step 2) and this Final Payment (Step 8), confirmed live 2026-08-03, so "PAID" alone can't tell the two apart; `Payment_Method_Final`/`Payment_received` only ever get written by this specific, later action.

- `Total_Service_Fee` (Quote step) now locks once `remainingFeePaidOnline()` is true.
- The Quote step's own Remaining_Fee recompute is now explicitly guarded too (belt-and-suspenders) — its recorded value can never drift even if some future change ever loosened `Booking_Fee`'s own separate lock.
- **Final Closure inherits this for free** — its own "Service Fee & Booking" group renders the exact same `quote`-stage field object, not a copy, so locking it once fixes both screens the user asked about with one change.
- `payment` stage's own `Total_Service_Fee`/`Remaining_Fee` were already unconditionally `readonly:true` (stricter than this fix, unaffected).

**Scoped exactly as asked, flagged for confirmation**: the lock is specific to `"Payment Gateway"` (the literal "online mode") — a Cash/UPI/Card/Net Banking final payment stays editable/correctable after the fact, same as before this fix. Ask if every payment method should lock the fee instead of just the online one.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 12 checks: the helper's own logic across Payment Gateway/Cash × Yes/No combinations; `Total_Service_Fee` locks only for the online-paid case and stays editable for both the normal case and a Cash-paid one; `payment` stage's own stricter always-locked fields confirmed unaffected; the shared-field-object relationship with Final Closure confirmed directly; and a full `renderWizard()` render confirming Remaining_Fee recomputes normally before payment but is completely protected from drifting afterward, even when its inputs would otherwise imply a different number. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later again still further again yet more) — New: Accept page's Save button now shows "Save" and exits to the dashboard in "Vendor Accepting via App" mode, "Save Acceptance" and advances to Reach in "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" mode (user request)

User's exact ask: "When Vendor is Accepting Via App, disable the 'save acceptance' and show 'save' then close the page, and when Agent Entering on behalf of vendor show save acceptance and work same like we already did."

**Worth knowing — this is a deliberate reversal of a 2026-08-07 bug fix, not a new idea**: back then, "Vendor Accepting via App exits to the dashboard instead of advancing to Reach" was itself the reported BUG (this stage had no `canAdvance` gate at all, so it should have advanced regardless of mode, and something else was investigated as the cause). The user has now explicitly asked for exactly that old "bug" behavior on purpose — there's genuinely nothing for the agent to do on Reach either while waiting on the vendor's own app (its own fields are locked the same way), so landing there instead of the dashboard was never actually useful in this mode.

**Fix**: `acceptance`/`acceptanceTow` now have:
- `save:(r)=>acceptanceIsAgentManual(r)?"Save Acceptance":"Save"` — the button label itself is now dynamic. `renderWizard()`'s own `step.save` handling was extended to support a function (same "string OR function" pattern `blockedMessage` already had) — every other step's own plain string label is completely unaffected.
- `canAdvance:(r)=>acceptanceIsAgentManual(r)` — reuses the exact same `blocked`/`backToDash()` mechanism `locations` already relies on for its own "nothing to do yet, save and exit" case. No new plumbing needed — "Vendor Accepting via App" now makes this step `blocked`, which `saveStep()` already handles by saving as-is and closing back to the dashboard; "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" keeps `canAdvance` true, so it behaves exactly as before (advances to Reach on save).
- `blockedMessage` — a friendly toast explaining the ticket is waiting on the vendor's/driver's own app, instead of the generic fallback.

Two now-outdated comments were corrected rather than left misleading: `reach`'s own 2026-08-12 comment (which said "`acceptance` has no canAdvance gate" — no longer true, though the underlying concern it describes is still valid for Agent-manual mode specifically, so that stage's own status-regression guard stays necessary) and `saveStep()`'s own 2026-08-07 diagnostic (which was investigating the exact behavior now being deliberately restored, framed at the time as an unexplained bug).

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 17 checks covering both RSR and TOW: the save label and `canAdvance`/`blocked` computation for both modes, a live `renderWizard()` render confirming the actual `#saveBtn` text updates correctly for both modes, and a no-regression check confirming a plain-string step (`create`) renders its own label exactly as before. All passed.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).


### 2026-08-19 (yet later again still further again yet more again) — Real bug fixed: assigning a vendor then saving Accept in the same session corrupted Status to a stage title instead of "RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)" (user-reported live, screenshot: a ticket showing "SERVICE ACCEPTANCE" as its own Status pill)

User's exact repro: assign a vendor, click **Save and Continue**, land on Accept with "Vendor Accepting via App" selected, click Save — the ticket's own Status should read `RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)`, but reopening the ticket showed something else. The search-result screenshot confirmed it: the Status pill literally read **"SERVICE ACCEPTANCE"** — not a real status value at all, the `acceptance` stage's own `title`.

**Root cause, confirmed by reproducing it against the real shipped code**: `buildPayload(step)` computes each save's own Status via `computeStatus(step, r) || step.title` — a "should never fire" fallback for a stage somehow missing its own `status` property. But `state.record`'s own `Status` field was **never updated locally after a successful save** — `saveStep()` computed the new status for the *outgoing payload* and sent it to Zoho, but never wrote it back into `state.record` itself. Assignment's own save correctly sent `Status: "RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)"` to Zoho — but the wizard then auto-advanced to Accept in the very same session, still holding a stale/blank local `r.Status`. Every one of `acceptance`'s own "only touch Status while genuinely still awaiting a decision" guards (built earlier this session, see the 2026-08-12/2026-08-18 entries above) correctly saw that blank value, correctly declined to invent a new status, and returned it unchanged — which was **also blank**, tripping the `|| step.title` fallback and writing `"Service Acceptance"` straight into Zoho as if it were a real status. Confirmed reproducing the exact reported value with a direct `buildPayload()` call against the live code before writing the fix.

**Fix — two layers**:
1. **Primary**: `saveStep()` now syncs `state.record[CONFIG.stageField] = payload[CONFIG.stageField]` immediately after a save is confirmed to have gone through. Every later stage's own status computation in the same session now always sees the true, just-persisted Status — exactly as if the ticket had been freshly reopened from Zoho, without actually needing to reopen it.
2. **Defense in depth**: `buildPayload()`'s own fallback chain is now `computeStatus(step, r) || r.Status || step.title` — even if some other, not-yet-discovered path ever leaves `computeStatus()` returning blank again, it now falls back to *preserving whatever Status already exists* (a safe no-op, matching every other status guard's own "if in doubt, preserve, don't invent" discipline built this session) before ever falling all the way to a human-readable title masquerading as a real status. `step.title` remains the absolute last resort, only reachable if both `computeStatus()` and `r.Status` are genuinely blank.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — reproduced the exact user-reported scenario end-to-end (Assignment save → same-session auto-advance to Accept → Accept save) and confirmed it now correctly yields `RSP IRA (INVITE RESPONSE AWAITED)`, not the corrupted title; confirmed the fallback hardening preserves an already-valid status; confirmed the absolute-last-resort path still works for the case both signals are genuinely blank; and confirmed the primary sync line is genuinely present in the shipped `saveStep()`. Re-ran every other test suite written earlier this same day (status-regression, Save Acceptance flow, Save button behavior, ETA/Expected-Arrival, Remaining-Fee lock, multi-photo closure, dashboard buckets — 100+ checks total across 7 files) to confirm this fix touches nothing else — all still pass (two assertions in the Save-Acceptance-flow suite were themselves updated, being stale relative to the same day's own later canAdvance change, not a regression).

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).
### 2026-08-19 (later) — Real bug fixed: Arrival Photo (and any other file field) could show a permanently broken image icon instead of the actual uploaded photo, even though "Already uploaded" correctly showed underneath it (user-reported live, screenshot)

User's exact repro: a vendor uploaded the Arrival Photo on their own phone (via `vendorTicket`); reopening the same ticket on the agent's own Reach screen showed the classic browser broken-image icon in the thumbnail slot, while the "Already uploaded" hint text right below it was correct.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the render path**: `hydrateFilePreviewsIn()` builds a direct download URL via `zohoFileDownloadUrl()` and, whenever one could be built at all, set `<img src>` to it and stopped — with no way to notice or recover if the browser's own request for that URL then actually failed (a 404/permission error, or any other live failure). A directUrl being *buildable* only ever proved `state.record`'s own raw value had a parseable filepath in it; it never proved the URL actually loads. Once that happened, the placeholder was gone for good — every future render kept re-setting the exact same failing `src`, forever.

**Fix**: the thumbnail `<img>` now carries its own `onerror` handler. If the direct URL fails to load, it logs a diagnostic (the field, index, URL, and the raw field value — so a repeat is actually diagnosable from the console) and falls through to the same readFile()-based last-resort path already used when no direct URL could be built at all (extracted into its own `fallbackToReadFilePreview()` so both call sites share one implementation instead of two copies). The happy path (URL builds and loads fine) is completely unchanged.

**Not fully root-caused**: *why* the direct URL failed to load for this specific photo isn't confirmed yet — the same app/report config is shared correctly across every widget (checked: all four widgets point at the same `get-rescue` app + `Agent_Ticket_Report`, so this isn't a cross-app URL mismatch), so it's more likely either a transient failure or a genuine gap in Zoho's own file-field response for that particular record at that particular moment. The new onerror diagnostic will pin this down exactly if/when it recurs — this fix's job is to make sure a failure like this is recoverable and diagnosable instead of a silent, permanent dead end.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness with a slightly richer fake DOM that actually tracks appended children) — 4 checks: the happy path still appends a real `<img>` with the correct direct URL and an `onerror` handler wired on it; manually firing that `onerror` (simulating exactly what the user saw live) logs the expected diagnostic and successfully falls through to the readFile()-based path, replacing the broken thumbnail with a real image instead of leaving it broken. Re-ran the multi-photo closure test to confirm no regression there — still passes.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).
### 2026-08-20 — UI change: "Reject Time", "Accept Location (Lat)", and "Accept Location (Lon)" removed from the Accept screen in Agent-manual mode (user request)

User's exact ask: when "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" is selected, these three fields should no longer appear on the page.

**Context**: all three were added 2026-08-11 so an agent standing in for the vendor could manually type in what the vendor's own app would otherwise auto-capture (Reject Time as a plain timestamp; Accept Location as a raw decimal lat/lon pair). Since then, `Distance to Breakdown`/`Accept Time` on this same stage were upgraded (2026-08-19) to auto-seed themselves the instant Agent-manual mode is picked — but Reject Time and Accept Location never got an equivalent auto-fill, so they stayed pure manual-entry burden: typing a rejection timestamp on what's fundamentally an ACCEPT screen, and hand-typing decimal GPS coordinates from a desk, neither of which had a realistic, reliable input to begin with.

**Fix**: removed the `{ n:"Service_Reject_Time", l:"Reject Time", ... }` and `{ n:"RSP_Start_Latitude"/"RSP_Start_Longitude", l:"Accept Location (Lat)"/"(Lon)", ... }` field entries from both `acceptance` (RSR) and `acceptanceTow` (TOW). `Distance to Breakdown`/`Accept Time`/`ETA`/`Expected Arrival Time` — none of which the user asked to remove — are untouched and still shown exactly as before.

**Deliberately left alone**: `acceptance`'s/`acceptanceTow`'s own `status()` function still has `if(r.Service_Reject_Time) return "RSP REJECT"` — this is now a safe no-op (nothing on this page writes the field anymore), but removing it wasn't asked for and it does no harm sitting there in case the field is ever set some other way. Also untouched: `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`RSP_Start_Longitude`'s own SEPARATE reuse on the "Reached Drop Location" stage (shown there as "Drop Location (Lat)/(Lon)") — same field names, different meaning, different stage, not part of this request.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 15 checks: all three fields confirmed gone from both stages' own field lists; every field the user did NOT ask to remove confirmed still present in both; the defensive `Service_Reject_Time` status check confirmed still working. Re-ran the ETA/four-fields, acceptance-distance-calc, and status-corruption-fix suites from earlier this session to confirm no regression — all still pass.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).
### 2026-08-20 (later) — UI change: "Reach Location (Lat)" and "Reach Location (Lon)" also removed from the Reach screen in Agent-manual mode (user request, same pattern as the Accept screen fix above)

Same follow-up ask, same day: hide these two on the Reach step too. Same reasoning as the Accept Location removal just above — hand-typing decimal GPS coordinates from a desk was never a realistic ask, and Reach_Time/Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown(_TOW) already cover what actually matters here.

**Fix**: removed `{ n:"Reach_Location_Lat"/"Reach_Location_Lon", l:"Reach Location (Lat)"/"(Lon)", ... }` from both `reach` (RSR) and `reachTow` (TOW). `Reach_Time`, `Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown`/`Distance_Reach_To_Breakdown_TOW`, and the Cancel-related fields on these same two stages are all untouched — none of those were asked for.

### 2026-08-20 (yet later) — New: "Ondemand Workflow for Invoicing" implemented — Step 1 of a two-stage plan (Zoho Creator record now, real Zoho Books push once credentials exist)

User provided a PDF spec ("Ondemand Workflow for Invoicing") describing a GST reverse-calculation + Zoho Books invoice flow, and asked for the fields needed on a new `On_Demand_Invoicing` form; after confirming those fields were created in Zoho Studio, this implements the Creator-side half of the pipeline — the half that needs no external credentials.

**Trigger, mapped onto real existing fields** (no new ticket-side gating needed): `saveClosure()`, right where `sendToZohoBooks()`/`sendToAirtable()` already fire (2026-08-17's own stub call sites) — fires when `Closure_Status==="Completed"` **and** there's actually something to invoice: the ticket's own final Total Service Fee for an `"ON DEMAND"` walk-in (no corporate account to bill), or `Rescue_Charge` — only when it's `>0` — for any other/billed `Client`. Uses `finalTSF` (the function's own already-computed, refund-adjusted figure), not a possibly-stale `r.Total_Service_Fee`, so the invoiced amount always matches what was actually just saved.

**New**: `onDemandInvoiceEligibility(r, finalTsf)`, `clientDisplayName(r)`, `computeGstBreakup(amount)` (exact PDF formulas: Service Charge = `amount×100/118`, CGST/SGST = `Service Charge×9%` each, Round Off = the residual — verified against BOTH of the PDF's own worked examples, including the ₹999 case where Round Off is `+0.01`, not `0.00`), and `maybeCreateOnDemandInvoice(r, finalTsf)` — creates one `On_Demand_Invoicing` record via `addRecords`, guarded against duplicates by reading that report and checking for an existing row with this ticket's own `RSID` first (same "read the report, filter client-side by Lookup id" pattern already used for Invites/Task_Rejections elsewhere in this app, rather than guessing Zoho's own Lookup criteria syntax).

**A real bug caught by the test harness before it ever shipped**: the first draft called `vehicleDisplayFor()`/`issueNamesFor()` for the Vehicle/Service fields — those are `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket`'s own helper names, not this file's. `getRescueTicket` has no such functions at all; its real equivalent is `lookupNamesFor(rawValue, optionsList)` (see the dashboard's own ticket-card call site). Fixed before packing — a plain syntax check wouldn't have caught this (both names are valid identifiers, just undefined at call time), only actually invoking the function in the Node-VM harness did.

**Not yet implemented, and deliberately left as-is** (same "fields/UI now, wiring later" decision as the 2026-08-17 Zoho Books stub): `Zoho_Books_Contact_ID`/`Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID`/`Invoice_Number`/`Invoice_PDF_URL`/`Sync_Error`/`Emailed_At` all stay blank — those need `sendToZohoBooks()` itself wired with real org ID/OAuth credentials. The PDF's own final step ("return Invoice Number to ticket") also isn't implemented — it needs a decision on which field on `Agent_Ticket_Report` itself should receive that mirror, not yet asked.

Re-ran the status-corruption, dashboard-buckets, and both same-day field-removal suites to confirm no regression — all still pass.

### 2026-08-21 — Real bug fixed: in Agent-manual mode, Status never advanced past "RSP ON THE WAY" through WIP/Loading/Reached Drop/Unloading — "All Tickets" kept showing "ON THE WAY" even after those pages were genuinely saved

User's exact report: "when AGENT accepts on behalf of vendor, when every page is submitted by the agent, the status bar is moving forward. but the status shown in the 'ALL Tickets' page is continuing to show as 'ON THE WAY'." Clicking into the ticket correctly opened it at the right step — because the wizard's own step navigation (`Agent_Wizard_Step`) is a completely separate field from the ticket's real `Status`, and only `Status` drives the "All Tickets" pill.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading `statusStillEarly()`'s own logic against each call site**: `statusStillEarly(r, stageStatuses)` only allows a stage to write its status when the ticket's CURRENT status already equals one of the given values (or is blank). `wip`'s own 2026-08-18 guard passed `["WORK IN PROGRESS"]` — its own OUTPUT status — instead of `"RSP ON THE WAY"`, the INCOMING status Accept actually sets. A ticket arriving from "RSP ON THE WAY" (the completely normal, expected case) could never satisfy that guard, so it silently fell through to `return r.Status`, preserving "RSP ON THE WAY" forever — on every genuine first save, not just a stray resave (which is what the guard was actually built to prevent). The exact same copy-paste mistake was made in `wipLoadingTow`, `wipReachedTow`, and `wipUnloadingTow` (TOW's own siblings). `payment`'s own 2026-08-18 guard, by contrast, got this right from the start — it checks `"REMAINING FEE DUE"` (its own INCOMING status) to decide whether to advance to `"RSP CLOSED"` (a different OUTPUT status) — the visible input/output mismatch there is likely exactly why that one was written correctly while the other four, where input and output "look similar," weren't.

**Why this only ever showed up in Agent-manual mode**: in Vendor-app mode, the vendor's own `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` widget writes "WORK IN PROGRESS" (etc.) directly, through a completely separate write path — by the time the agent views the ticket, Status has already arrived correct, and this broken guard never actually gets exercised. Agent-manual mode has no other writer for these transitions at all — this stage's own `status()` function was the ONLY thing that was ever supposed to advance it.

**Fix**: all four stages' guards now check the correct INCOMING status instead of their own output —
- `wip`: checks `"RSP ON THE WAY"` → advances to `"WORK IN PROGRESS"`
- `wipLoadingTow`: checks `"RSP ON THE WAY"` → advances to `"LOADING"`
- `wipReachedTow`: checks `"LOADING"` → advances to `"TO DROP LOCATION"`
- `wipUnloadingTow`: checks `"TO DROP LOCATION"` → advances to `"UNLOADING"`

Also added the same `acceptanceIsAgentManual(r)` gate `reach`/`reachTow` already use for their own agent-driven writes, to all four — in Vendor-app mode these stages' fields are read-only, and the vendor's own app is the real source of truth for these transitions; this guard means the agent merely opening/saving a Vendor-app-mode WIP-family page can never prematurely advance Status ahead of what the vendor has actually done. `payment` was not touched — its own guard was already correct, and it deliberately has no `acceptanceIsAgentManual` gate (closing a ticket is an agent action regardless of acceptance mode, not something the vendor's own app ever does).

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — 11 checks: each of the four stages correctly advances from its real incoming status in Agent-manual mode; a re-save while already at the target status stays put (no regression, no crash); a ticket already further along never gets reverted backward; Vendor-app mode is confirmed to never advance status via any of these four functions (matching the existing, correct behavior where the vendor's own app is the real writer); `payment`'s own unrelated, already-correct transition confirmed unchanged. Re-ran the status-no-regression, WIP-readonly-gate, status-corruption-fix, and dashboard-buckets suites from earlier this session to confirm no interaction — all still pass.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).
### 2026-08-24 — New: Ops Map "Repair, Tow" filter added — deliberately NOT the vendor-capability filter the spec literally describes, and here is exactly why

User asked to implement the remaining points from the 2026-08-17 field-planning spec image. Auditing every row against the real code first surfaced: the Ops Map already had the "Own Mechanic"/"Tie-Up" filter, but never got the "Repair, Tow" half the same spec row also asked for.

**Why this could not just be "add two more dropdown options"**: filtering vendors/technicians by Repair vs. Tow *capability* would need a field on Vendors_Report/Technicians_Report marking who can do which kind of job — and QUESTIONS_TO_ASK.md has an OPEN, still-unanswered item asking for exactly that field ("Vendor 'Mechanic vs Driver' categorization field"), dating back to the very first Action 4 reconciliation. Guessing a field name here would risk silently hiding a real vendor from the Ops Manager's map based on a made-up classification — exactly the kind of guess this project has deliberately avoided all session for anything that could misrepresent real operational data.

**What was implemented instead**: a Service Type filter that narrows down *only the already-on-a-case (red) markers* to the selected type, using the Service_Type of whichever ticket they're currently assigned to — real data already available, no new field needed. A FREE (green) or OFFLINE (grey) marker has no "current job" to filter by, so it always stays visible regardless of this filter's selection — hiding available capacity would defeat the whole point of an Ops map. Clicking an on-case marker's info window now also shows which service type they're currently doing.

**Fix**:
- `activeAssignmentIds()` now also returns each active vendor/technician's own current ticket's `Service_Type`, alongside the existing on-case id Sets.
- New `#opsMapServiceFilter` dropdown (All / Repair (RSR) / Tow) next to the existing Engagement filter.
- `renderOpsMapMarkers()` applies it: an on-case marker whose current job doesn't match the selected type is excluded; free/offline markers are never affected by this filter.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, mocking the Google Maps API surface — Marker/InfoWindow/LatLngBounds/SymbolPath) — confirmed `activeAssignmentIds()` correctly tracks each assignment's own Service_Type; confirmed selecting "Repair" correctly excludes a vendor currently on a Tow job while still showing a free vendor; confirmed no regression with the filter left on "All". Re-ran the dashboard-buckets, WIP-family status-advance, and On-Demand-Invoicing suites from earlier this session to confirm no interaction — all still pass.

**Everything else from the same spec image stays exactly where the last audit left it** — still needs from you: the real Google review link, Zoho Books/Airtable credentials, clarification on the GST auto-fetch mechanism, and the vendor payout formula. Nothing else was guessed at or implemented without that input.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).
### 2026-08-25 — New: "Send Payment Link" now creates a real Zoho Books invoice first and includes the actual payment link in the WhatsApp message (user request)

User's exact ask: call `onDemandInvoicing`, get the invoice URL it returns, include that URL in the WhatsApp payment message, then send via the existing `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` — same mechanism `sendWhatsAppMessage` already uses — without touching the existing invoicing or WhatsApp functionality.

**Important architectural distinction surfaced while implementing this**: the "On Add" Workflow built earlier this session for `On_Demand_Invoicing` (triggered from `saveClosure()`, fires in Zoho's own background) has no way to hand a value back to the widget that triggered it — Workflows are fire-and-forget from the caller's perspective. Getting a real invoice URL back synchronously, right when a button is clicked, needs a Zoho Creator **Custom API** instead (the exact same mechanism `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` already uses via `invokeCustomApi()` — see that function's own 2026-08-10 comment for the hard-won config shape: `api_name`/`workspace_name`/`http_method`/`payload`, confirmed against Zoho's own docs). `onDemandInvoicing()` calls a Custom API of that same name — if it doesn't already exist as one in Zoho Creator, it needs to be set up that way (backed by the same invoice-creation logic already built for the Workflow, but ending in a real `return invoice_url;` instead of a workflow's own record-save).

**Fix**:
- New `onDemandInvoicing(record)` — calls `invokeCustomApi()` with `api_name:"onDemandInvoicing"`, passing the ticket's own record ID. Tries several plausible response keys for the returned URL (`invoice_url`, `output`, `result`, `data`, nested `details.*`) and logs the full raw response either way, since the exact shape a Custom API's response takes through `invokeCustomApi()` hasn't been exercised anywhere else in this app yet.
- `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` gained an optional 3rd parameter (`param3`) — every existing call site only ever passed 2, so this sends `""` for all of them exactly as before; purely additive.
- `Send_Payment_Link`'s own action now calls `onDemandInvoicing(r)` first, then passes whatever URL comes back (or `""`) as `param3` into the existing `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` call for the `booking_fees_link` template. Deliberately best-effort: if invoicing fails or returns no URL, the payment-request WhatsApp message still goes out rather than blocking the agent from requesting payment/location at all — same non-blocking philosophy as Google Feedback/Airtable elsewhere in this file.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, stubbing `invokeCustomApi`) — 9 checks: `onDemandInvoicing()` calls the Custom API with the right config and correctly extracts a URL from the response; gracefully returns `""` (not a throw) when no recognizable URL field exists; `sendWhatsAppTemplate()`'s existing callers are confirmed unaffected (`param3` defaults to empty); a real invoice URL is confirmed to flow all the way from `onDemandInvoicing()` through to the actual WhatsApp payload as `param3`; the full button flow still sends the WhatsApp message even when invoicing comes back empty. Re-ran the Ops-Map-filter, On-Demand-Invoicing, and status-no-regression suites from earlier this session to confirm no interaction — all still pass.

**Still open**: whether "onDemandInvoicing" already exists as a Custom API in Zoho Creator, or needs to be created — and if creating it, whether it should invoice the Booking Fee specifically (the amount this exact button is requesting) or something else. Not guessed at; needs confirmation.

Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

**2026-08-25 (later) — added explicit logging** (user request: "also log the invoice url and message"): `Send_Payment_Link`'s own action now logs the real invoice URL and the exact WhatsApp params (Booking_Fee/Total_Service_Fee/Invoice_URL) being sent, right at this call site — `onDemandInvoicing()`/`sendWhatsAppTemplate()` already log their own request/response internally, but this puts the two things that actually matter for checking this specific flow in one place instead of piecing it together from each function's own generic logs. Re-tested — all 9 checks from the suite above still pass; packed again.

**2026-08-25 (later still) — restructured as a dedicated parameter, not a 3rd array element** (user request: "add ONE NEW parameter for the Invoice URL... keep all existing parameters and functionality unchanged"): `sendWhatsAppTemplate(phone, templateName, params)` gained a genuinely separate 4th argument, `invoiceUrl` — sent in the Custom API payload under its own clearly-named `invoiceUrl` key, instead of the generic `param3` the previous entry added. `params[0]`/`params[1]` (param1/param2) are completely untouched — every existing call site across this file (Locations' own "location" template, the Final Payment "Remaining_Fee" send, the Google Feedback send, etc.) simply never passes a 4th argument at all, so `invoiceUrl` defaults to `""` for every one of them, identical to their behavior before this change. `Send_Payment_Link`'s own action updated to match: `sendWhatsAppTemplate(r.Phone_Number1, "booking_fees_link", [Booking_Fee, Total_Service_Fee], invoiceUrl)`. Re-tested against the real shipped code — 11 checks, including an explicit check that param1/param2 land correctly and are unaffected by the new argument's presence or absence. Packed again.

**Real bug fixed 2026-08-25 (later yet again, user-reported live) — the invoice URL genuinely never reached WhatsApp, and now we know exactly why**: the user's own console log from a live production call gave the actual response shape for the first time — `{ code:"SUCCESS", message:"Invoice created successfully", invoice_id, invoice_number, total_amount, issue_type, distance, url }` — a **flat `url` field**, not any of the originally-guessed candidates (`invoice_url`/`output`/`result`/`data`/`details.*`). Every one of those guesses missed, so `onDemandInvoicing()` always returned `""` even though the invoice was being created successfully every time. Fixed: `url` is now checked first (and confirmed correct against this exact real response); the old guessed keys stay as harmless fallbacks afterward. Test's own mock response updated to the real confirmed shape rather than a plausible-looking guess. Re-tested — 14 checks, all pass. Packed again.

**Real bug fixed 2026-08-25 (yet again, user-reported live) — the dedicated `invoiceUrl` parameter was correct at the JS/payload level but still never showed up in the actual WhatsApp message, because the approved template has nowhere to put it**: the user pasted the exact rendered WhatsApp text — `"...please use the following link to pay the booking fee of Rs. 200/- : 1000. The remaining amount can be paid at service completion."` — no link anywhere, just the two numbers. Root cause: `booking_fees_link` is a pre-approved WhatsApp Business template with a **fixed 2 placeholders** ({{1}}/{{2}}, i.e. `param1`/`param2`). Anything sent under any other key — including the `invoiceUrl` key added in the two previous fixes — simply isn't part of that approved template and is silently dropped by WhatsApp regardless of what the widget sends. There was never a template-side slot for a 3rd/4th value to land in.

User's exact instruction: **"add link without sending param"** — i.e. stop sending the URL as any kind of separate/dedicated parameter, and instead fold its text directly into one of the two params that are actually proven to render.

**Fix**: `Send_Payment_Link`'s action now builds `param2` as `Total_Service_Fee + "\nPay now: " + invoiceUrl` when an invoice URL comes back (plain `Total_Service_Fee` alone, unchanged, when it doesn't), and calls `sendWhatsAppTemplate(r.Phone_Number1, "booking_fees_link", [Booking_Fee, param2])` — no 4th `invoiceUrl` argument from this call site anymore. `param1` (Booking_Fee) is untouched. `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` itself keeps its `invoiceUrl` 4th parameter exactly as-is (unused by this call site now, but not removed — no other call site was touched, and removing a parameter outright isn't the kind of minimal, backward-compatible change this project has committed to).

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — updated the "Full Send_Payment_Link button flow" checks: `param2` now contains the real invoice URL text and still starts with the real `Total_Service_Fee` value; confirmed no separate `invoiceUrl` payload key carries anything anymore; `Booking_Fee`/`Send_Link_For_The_Breakdown_Location` behavior unchanged. Updated the "invoicing fails" case to assert `param2` falls back to exactly the plain fee value (`"1000"`, no appended link text) when no URL comes back, instead of the now-stale assertion about an unused `invoiceUrl` key. Full suite: 14 checks, all pass. Syntax-checked and packed again (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

**Real bug fixed 2026-08-25 (yet again, user-reported live) — the WhatsApp message's `param2` ended up carrying the ENTIRE raw invoice JSON blob instead of just the link**: live log showed `param2` as `'1000\nPay now: {"code": "SUCCESS", "message": "Invo…url":"...3988332000000104003?organization_id=60078911104"}'` — the whole response object, stringified, instead of the URL alone. Root cause: this Zoho account's `invokeCustomApi()` wraps a Custom API's real output inside a `result` key that comes back as a **JSON-encoded string**, e.g. `{code:3000, result:"{\"...\",\"url\":\"...\"}"}` — confirmed by the exact same `{code, result}` wrapper shape showing up in the `sendWhatsAppMessage` response logged in the same incident (`{result:'{"error":{...}}', code:3000}`). Because the real `url` field lives one level deeper, inside that string, `onDemandInvoicing()`'s old key-search never found `res.url` at the top level and fell through to the `res.result` fallback — which matched, since `result` existed — and returned the *entire raw string* as if it were the URL.

**Fix**: `onDemandInvoicing()` now checks whether `res.result` is a string and, if so, `JSON.parse()`s it first before searching for the url field inside the parsed object — a harmless no-op when `result` isn't a string (or doesn't parse), so responses shaped like the earlier fix's flat mock (`{url:"..."}` with no `result` wrapper at all) still work exactly as before.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — added a new check with a mock shaped exactly like the real wrapped response (`{code:3000, result: JSON.stringify({...,url:"..."})}`), confirming the real nested URL is extracted correctly and not the raw blob. Full suite: 15 checks, all pass. Syntax-checked and packed again (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

**2026-08-25 (later still) — `invoiceUrl` removed from the outgoing WhatsApp payload entirely** (user request: "send msg without invoiceUrl"): `sendWhatsAppTemplate()`'s payload to the `sendWhatsAppMessage` Custom API no longer includes an `invoiceUrl` key at all — not even as `""` — regardless of whether a caller passes a 4th argument. This key had been dead weight since the earlier same-day fix that started folding the link into `param2` instead; the approved template can't render it, and the underlying Custom API has no use for it either. The `invoiceUrl` function parameter itself is left in place (unused, harmless) so the function's own signature doesn't need to change and nothing calling it breaks.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — updated the dedicated-4th-argument checks to confirm the `invoiceUrl` key is now absent from the payload object entirely (`"invoiceUrl" in payload` is `false`), even when a 4th argument is explicitly passed; confirmed the same for `Send_Payment_Link`'s own call site. `param1`/`param2` behavior is unchanged in every case. Full suite: 17 checks, all pass. Syntax-checked and packed again (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

**Real bug fixed 2026-08-25 (later still, user-reported live) — WhatsApp rejected the send outright, even with a clean-looking `param2`**: live log showed `param2` as `'1000\nPay now: https://books.zoho.in/invoices/...'` (looked completely reasonable), yet the API call itself came back `{code:3000, result:'{"error":{"code":"message_rejected","message":"WhatsApp rejected the message: (#132018) There's an issue with the parameters in your template",...}}'}`. Root cause: WhatsApp Business template parameters **cannot contain newline characters** (nor tabs, nor 4+ consecutive spaces) — this is a hard restriction on the Meta/WhatsApp side, not something specific to this template. The `"\n"` joining the fee and the payment link in `param2` was exactly what WhatsApp's API was rejecting the whole message over, regardless of how reasonable the text looked in a console log.

**Fix**: `Send_Payment_Link`'s action now joins `Total_Service_Fee` and the invoice link with a plain `" - "` instead of `"\n"` — e.g. `1000 - Pay now: https://books.zoho.in/invoices/...` — same information, same fallback to just the plain fee value when no URL comes back, just without the character WhatsApp's API silently rejects the whole message over.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness) — added an explicit check that `param2` contains no `"\n"` character; re-ran the full suite (18 checks) — all pass, including the pre-existing checks that the fee value and invoice URL both still land correctly inside `param2`. Syntax-checked and packed again (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-08-25 — `onDemandInvoicing` Custom API (Deluge, Zoho-side, not this widget's own code) made dynamic — was 100% hardcoded demo data until now

User shared the actual Deluge source of the `onDemandInvoicing` Custom API for the first time and asked: "make it dynamically so data generate based on the quote screen[;] breakup is the items and do the thing based on the project requirement."

**What was found**: the deployed script never read `recordId` at all — every field (`issueType`, `distance`, `baseCharge`) was a fixed constant, so every invoice this API created was identical regardless of which real ticket triggered it, even though the widget has been sending `payload:{recordId}` since this API's very first version.

**Fix** — tracked at [`getRescueTicket/custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge) (this repo has no way to deploy Deluge directly; paste that file's contents into the Custom API's Zoho Creator editor to ship it — full rationale is in that file's own header comment):
- Looks up the real ticket from `Agent_Ticket_Report` using the `recordId` the widget already sends, instead of ignoring it.
- Builds the invoice's line items straight from the ticket's own `Rate_Breakup` field — the exact text the agent already sees on the Quote screen (`"ISSUE: Rs. 499"`, `"ISSUE: Rs. 850 (RT_KM: 20 km)"` for TOW formula rates, or a `"Rs. -- (...)"` placeholder when no rate exists yet — same format `calcQuote()` in this widget already builds it in). Placeholder lines are skipped, not invoiced.
- Falls back to a single `Total_Service_Fee` line item if `Rate_Breakup` has no priced line at all, so invoicing never breaks outright over formatting.
- `reference_number` is now the ticket's real `Case_ID`, not a random `"demo_"+timestamp` string.

**Deliberately left alone, flagged as open questions rather than guessed at** (see the file's own header for the full reasoning):
- `customer_id`/`tax_exemption_id`/`organization_id` are still the same fixed demo values — there's no ticket-to-Books-customer mapping anywhere in this project yet, so every invoice still posts under one fixed Books contact. Building real per-customer lookup/creation is materially bigger scope, needs its own explicit decision.
- This now invoices the full itemized quote (effectively `Total_Service_Fee`), not just the `Booking_Fee` the WhatsApp payment-link message asks the customer to pay right now — reading "Rate Breakup is the items" as the answer to the exact open question raised in an earlier entry above ("whether onDemandInvoicing should invoice the Booking Fee specifically... or something else"). Flag this back if a Booking-Fee-only invoice was actually intended.

**Not testable from here**: this is Deluge running inside Zoho Creator, not JS in this repo — there's no local interpreter to run the usual Node-VM suite against it. Every syntax element reused from the original script (`try/catch`, `Map()`/`List()`, `invokeurl`, the response JSON shape) is already proven working in production; the newly-added string-parsing calls (`toList`, `subText`, `removeFirstOccurence`, `toDecimal`) are real, documented Deluge functions but weren't exercised by the original script. **Please test with one real ticket that has a real `Rate_Breakup` value before relying on this** — the widget side (`onDemandInvoicing()`/`sendWhatsAppTemplate()` in `widget.html`) needs no changes either way, since the response shape is unchanged.

### 2026-08-25 — Real bug fixed: Rescue Charge didn't update when Booking Fee was entered (user-reported live)

The Client_Charge/Rescue_Charge fields were built 2026-08-17 per the user's own confirmed spec: `Rescue_Charge = Total_Service_Fee − Client_Charge`, deliberately independent of `Booking_Fee` (a different split — who's billed vs. when it's collected). User reported live that Rescue Charge should also net out whatever was already collected as the Booking Fee; asked to confirm the exact formula rather than guessing at financial math, user picked: **`Rescue_Charge = Total_Service_Fee − Client_Charge − Booking_Fee`**.

**Fix**:
- Quote step's live recompute (`renderWizard()`, `step.key==="quote"`) now subtracts `Booking_Fee` too, alongside `Client_Charge` — `Booking_Fee` already had `recompute:true` wired on its own field, so this alone makes Rescue Charge update immediately as soon as Booking Fee is typed, no separate wiring needed.
- `saveClosure()`'s own "Client Charge + Rescue Charge = Total Service Fee" validation (added 2026-08-17, guards against `Refund_Amount` desyncing the two after Rescue Charge was last computed) updated to match the new formula: now checks `Client_Charge + Rescue_Charge + Booking_Fee = Total Service Fee`. This specific check wasn't independently re-exercised end-to-end (`saveClosure()` requires a much larger DOM/field mock to reach past its own required-fields gate) — it's a direct, symmetric algebraic consequence of the same formula change that IS tested below, not a separate guess.

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, real `renderWizard()`) — extended the existing 2026-08-17 Client_Charge/Rescue_Charge regression suite: confirmed the new formula's result (1000 − 600 − 200 = 200, previously 400); added a check reproducing the exact reported bug — same ticket, only `Booking_Fee` changed from 200 to 350, confirming `Rescue_Charge` actually moves (200 → 50); confirmed a blank `Booking_Fee` is treated as 0 (no regression for tickets that never touch it); re-confirmed the ON DEMAND null-forcing behavior is unchanged. Full suite (37 checks, includes the untouched nearestVendorBaseLocation/legDistanceKm/runClosureDistanceCalculations/Operations-Manager checks from the same file) — all pass. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`). `FIELDS.md` updated to match.

**2026-08-25 (same day, user request: "dont need to add client charge if user not entered client charge")** — the "stays null until `Client_Charge` is entered" behavior from the original 2026-08-17 spec (deliberate at the time, to avoid showing "Rescue Charge = full Total Service Fee" on an untouched ticket) has been replaced per this explicit request: a blank `Client_Charge` is now simply treated as `0` in the same formula, so with no `Client_Charge` entered, `Rescue_Charge = Total_Service_Fee − Booking_Fee`; once a real `Client_Charge` value is entered, it's `Total_Service_Fee − Client_Charge − Booking_Fee`, unchanged from the fix just above. `Client_Charge` itself is never silently written to — only `Rescue_Charge`'s own computed value changes. ON DEMAND is still the only case that forces both fields to `null`.

**Tested** — updated the same regression suite's "stays null" check to instead confirm `Rescue_Charge = Total_Service_Fee − Booking_Fee` (1000 − 200 = 800) when `Client_Charge` is blank, and that `Client_Charge` itself is left genuinely untouched (`undefined`, not silently defaulted to `0`). Full suite: 38 checks, all pass. Syntax-checked and packed again (`getRescueTicket.zip`). `FIELDS.md` updated to match.

### 2026-08-25 — `onDemandInvoicing` made fully parameter-driven; issues can now be multiple (user request)

User pasted the Custom API's Deluge source again — still the ORIGINAL hardcoded demo version, not the dynamic one from the entry above — and asked for it to send real dynamic data (issue, price, other details), explicitly calling out that issues can be multiple, and asked which parameters the widget needs to send.

**Design change from the previous dynamic attempt above**: that version had the Deluge script itself look the ticket up (`Agent_Ticket_Report[ID==...]`) and parse `Rate_Breakup`'s free text server-side — which depended on Deluge syntax (`subText`, `removeFirstOccurence`, `toDecimal`, and the report-lookup query itself) that couldn't be verified from this repo. Since the ask now is explicitly about picking the right parameters to send, the parsing has been moved into this widget's own JS instead — where it can actually be unit-tested — and the Custom API now just trusts the already-structured result. Fewer unverified assumptions overall, not more.

**New `parseRateBreakupIssues(text)`** (`widget.html`, right after `calcQuote()`) — parses `Rate_Breakup`'s own display text (`"ISSUE: Rs. 499"`, `"ISSUE: Rs. 850 (RT_KM: 20 km)"` for TOW formula rates, or a `"Rs. -- (...)"` placeholder) into a real `[{description, amount}, ...]` list — one entry per issue, so multiple issues become multiple invoice line items. Placeholder lines are skipped. Parses whatever's *currently* in `Rate_Breakup` at call time (not a fresh recompute), respecting any manual edit the agent made to it or to `Total_Service_Fee`.

**`onDemandInvoicing(record)` now sends these parameters** (`payload`):
- `recordId` — unchanged, kept for logging/traceability.
- `referenceNumber` — the ticket's real `Case_ID` (via `caseLabel()`), used as the Books invoice's `reference_number`.
- `issuesJson` — a JSON-encoded **string** of the parsed issues array (sent as a string, not a raw nested array, so the Deluge side has one unambiguous way to read it — `toJSONList()` — instead of depending on how Zoho's Custom API auto-deserializes nested request bodies).
- `totalAmount` — the widget's own computed sum, sent for logging/cross-checking only; the Custom API computes its own total by summing the parsed issues rather than trusting a client-sent number for the actual invoice math.

Falls back to a single `Total_Service_Fee` line item (labeled `"<Service_Type> Service Fee"`) when `Rate_Breakup` has no priced line at all — same safety net as the previous entry. If there's truly nothing to invoice (`Rate_Breakup` blank/all-placeholder AND `Total_Service_Fee` is 0/blank), `onDemandInvoicing()` now returns `""` immediately **without calling the Custom API at all**, rather than sending an empty invoice request.

**Custom API rewrite** — tracked at [`getRescueTicket/custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge) (paste into the Zoho Creator Custom API editor to ship it): drops the `Agent_Ticket_Report` lookup and all free-text parsing entirely; reads `input.issuesJson` via `toJSONList()`, builds one invoice line item per issue, sums its own total from the parsed list, and uses `input.referenceNumber` for the invoice's `reference_number`. Response shape is unchanged (`code`/`message`/`invoice_id`/`invoice_number`/`total_amount`/`issue_type`/`distance`/`url`) — `distance` is now always `0` since the widget no longer sends it (nothing reads it back). `customer_id`/`tax_exemption_id` are still the same fixed values, same open question as before, not touched.

**Tested** against the real shipped widget code (Node-VM harness) — 6 new checks directly on `parseRateBreakupIssues()` (multiple issues, TOW `RT_KM` suffix stripped correctly, placeholder lines skipped, blank/null input, all-placeholder input); extended `onDemandInvoicing()`'s own tests — confirms `issuesJson` carries both parsed issues as real structured data (not a lumped total), `totalAmount` is the real sum, `referenceNumber` carries the real `Case_ID`, the `Total_Service_Fee` fallback produces the right single line item, and the "nothing to invoice" case never calls the Custom API at all. Full suite: 30 checks, all pass. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

**Not testable from here** (same caveat as the previous Deluge entry): `toJSONList()` is a real, documented Deluge function but wasn't exercised by the original script. Test with one real ticket that has multiple issues in its `Rate_Breakup` before relying on this in production.

### 2026-08-25 — Real bug fixed (user-reported live): `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` threw "Variable 'recordId' is not defined"

The entry above accessed every incoming parameter via an `input.xxx` map (`input.recordId`, `input.issuesJson`, etc.) — exactly the one thing flagged as unverified in that same entry ("NOT verified against already-proven-working code"). It was wrong: this Zoho account's Custom APIs don't expose an `input` map at all. Parameters declared in a Custom API's own **Parameters tab** (Custom APIs → `onDemandInvoicing` → Parameters — name + type) are injected directly as **bare variables** of that exact name, with no prefix.

**Fix**: every `input.xxx` reference in [`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge) changed to the bare `xxx` it should have been (`recordId`, `referenceNumber`, `issuesJson`, `totalAmount`). Renamed the local `referenceNumber` fallback-resolution variable to `referenceNumberResolved` so the script never tries to reassign a parameter-bound variable, only its own local copy.

**Action needed in Zoho Creator, not just this script**: the 4 parameters (`recordId`, `referenceNumber`, `issuesJson`, `totalAmount`) must actually be added in the Custom API's own Parameters tab with these exact names before this script can read them — the reported error is exactly what happens when a script references a name that was never declared there.

Not independently re-testable from this repo the same way — this specific bug only exists in how Zoho Creator's own Custom API runtime binds declared parameters into script scope, which has no Node-side equivalent to simulate. Please re-test with one real ticket after adding the parameters in Zoho Creator's UI.

**Fix**: [`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge)'s function signature is now `string onDemandInvoicing(recordId, referenceNumber, issuesJson, totalAmount)` — the 4 parameters declared directly on the function itself, superseding the previous entry's guess about a separate Zoho Creator "Parameters" configuration screen. `referenceNumber`'s fallback-to-`recordId` logic now writes to a new local `referenceNumberResolved` variable instead of reassigning the `referenceNumber` parameter itself, so it never depends on whether reassigning a declared parameter is even allowed. Everything else in the function — parsing `issuesJson`, building one invoice line item per issue, creating the invoice, emailing it, the returned response shape — is completely unchanged.

**Widget side**: no change needed. `onDemandInvoicing()` in `widget.html` already sends `payload: { recordId, referenceNumber, issuesJson, totalAmount }` (from the entry two above) — those exact 4 key names already match this new signature, so the existing, already-tested widget code lines up with this fix with zero edits.

Not independently re-testable from this repo (same caveat as the entry above) — please re-test with one real ticket that has multiple issues in its Rate Breakup, and confirm via Zoho Creator's own execution log that `recordId`/`referenceNumber`/`issuesJson`/`totalAmount` all arrive with real values this time.

### 2026-08-25 — Real root cause found, with conclusive evidence: wrong field name for a Zoho Books line item's price all along

User got the actual logged `jsonPayload` this time (via Zoho Creator's own execution log, `info`-logged per an earlier fix) — and it proved the request body being sent to Zoho Books was **completely correct**: `"unit_price":"499"` for both line items, properly formatted, properly structured. Every one of the previous four fix attempts (`toDecimal()` normalization, `toMap()` round-tripping, using `amtRaw.toString()` instead of `amt.toString()`) had been chasing the wrong layer — none of them could have mattered, because the payload was already right before any of them.

**Actual root cause**: Zoho Books' Invoice API doesn't have a `unit_price` field on a line item at all — its real, documented field for a line item's price is **`rate`**. `unit_price` is a field name from a different Zoho product, not Books, and had been silently ignored by Books this entire time — for every invoice this system has EVER created, including the original hardcoded demo script's own "successful" one. That original "success" was only ever confirmed by this script's own echoed `total_amount` field (a local Deluge variable, not anything read back from the real created invoice) — nobody had actually looked at that invoice in Zoho Books' own UI until today, when the user started doing exactly that and immediately spotted `Rate: 0.00` on every single test invoice.

**Fix**: `lineItem.put("unit_price", ...)` → `lineItem.put("rate", ...)`. Everything else about the line item (`description`, `quantity`, `tax_exemption_id`) stays exactly as-is.

**Not independently testable from here** (same recurring caveat) — but this is now backed by hard evidence (the confirmed-correct request body) rather than a guess about internal Deluge type coercion. Please retest via the Execute dialog or a real "Send Payment Link" click and confirm the invoice finally shows real `Rate`/`Amount`/`Total`/`Balance Due` values (₹499 + ₹499 = ₹998).

**Current final state of the whole `onDemandInvoicing` chain, for reference**: widget parses `Rate_Breakup` into structured `{description, amount}` issues (JS, unit-tested) → sends `recordId`/`referenceNumber`/`issuesJson`/`totalAmount` as declared function parameters → Custom API parses `issuesJson` via `toJSONList()`, round-trips each element through `toMap()` for reliable field access, builds one Zoho Books line item per issue with `rate` (not `unit_price`) as the price field → creates the invoice, emails it, returns the payment URL → widget folds that URL into the WhatsApp `booking_fees_link` message's `param2`, joined with `" - "` (not a newline, which WhatsApp's API rejects) → sends via `sendWhatsAppTemplate()`.

### 2026-08-25 — Major change: `onDemandInvoicing` replaced with the "Ondemand Workflow for Invoicing" PDF's GST-inclusive reverse-calculation

User shared a new PDF spec ("Ondemand Workflow for Invoicing") and, after being asked to clarify since it directly conflicted with the itemized/GST-exempt invoice just finished today, explicitly decided: **replace** `Send_Payment_Link`'s invoice with this GST logic entirely (not add a second, separate invoice).

**Found first**: this exact GST reverse-calculation was **already implemented**, formula-for-formula matching both of the PDF's own worked examples — `computeGstBreakup()` and `onDemandInvoiceEligibility()` in `widget.html`, built 2026-08-20 for the ticket-**closure**-time flow (`maybeCreateOnDemandInvoice()`, writes a record into `On_Demand_Invoicing_Report`). That flow's own real-Books push (`sendToZohoBooks()`) was still an explicit no-op stub — never wired up. Today's change reuses that same proven math for the **Quote-stage** `Send_Payment_Link` flow instead of writing a Creator-only record.

**Amount-selection rule** (from the PDF, matching `onDemandInvoiceEligibility()`'s own existing rule minus its `Closure_Status=="Completed"` gate — `Send_Payment_Link` fires well before closure, so there's no `Refund_Amount`-adjusted final figure yet):
- `Client === "ON DEMAND"` (walk-in) → invoice `Total_Service_Fee`.
- Any other (B2B/contracted) Client → invoice `Rescue_Charge`, only when it's `> 0`.

**`onDemandInvoicing()` in `widget.html`** now computes `computeGstBreakup(amount)` and sends `serviceCharges`/`cgst`/`sgst`/`roundOff` plus `customerName`/`customerPhone`/`customerEmail`/`gstNumber`/`serviceLabel` to the Custom API — no more `issuesJson`. `parseRateBreakupIssues()` itself is left in place, unused by this function now, in case a future itemized-invoice need returns.

**[`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge) rewritten** to:
1. Find-or-create the customer in Zoho Books by phone (per explicit decision).
2. Build ONE `"Service Charge"` line item at the reverse-calculated `serviceCharges` rate, with CGST + SGST applied as real Books taxes (`tax_id`/`tax_id2`).
3. Apply `Round Off` as the invoice's own `adjustment` field (a native Zoho Books feature — exactly what the PDF specifies, "Apply Round Off as Adjustment").
4. Set `place_of_supply` to Karnataka.
5. Create, email, and return the payment URL — same response shape, same widget-side extraction logic, no changes needed there.

**Genuinely new, unverified Zoho Books API surface — none of today's earlier debugging touched any of this** (flagged extensively in the file's own header comment):
1. **Blocking**: `cgst_tax_id`/`sgst_tax_id` are literal placeholder strings right now — nothing will apply real GST until these are replaced with real Tax IDs from your Zoho Books org (Settings → Taxes). You said you'd provide these — please share the actual CGST 9% and SGST 9% Tax IDs (or say if your org instead uses one combined 18% GST group tax, in which case the script needs a small adjustment to use a single `tax_id` instead of `tax_id`+`tax_id2`).
2. Customer search by `?phone=` query param — a reasonable guess, not confirmed against this org.
3. Customer creation fields (`contact_name`/`phone`/`email`/`gst_no`/`gst_treatment` as top-level Contact fields) — not exercised live yet.
4. `place_of_supply` sent as `"KA"` — the standard Karnataka GST state code, but not confirmed against what this org's own invoices actually expect.
5. `adjustment`/`adjustment_description` for Round Off — real, documented Books invoice fields, but this exact usage hasn't been tested against this org yet.

**Tested** (JS side only — the Deluge side has too much genuinely new surface to responsibly claim untested code works): `computeGstBreakup()` re-confirmed against both exact PDF worked examples (₹2,500 → Round Off ₹0.00; ₹999 → Round Off ₹0.01); `onDemandInvoicing()`'s new payload confirmed for both the ON DEMAND (`Total_Service_Fee`) and non-ON-DEMAND (`Rescue_Charge`, gated on `>0`) paths, customer details passed through correctly, and the full `Send_Payment_Link` → WhatsApp flow re-confirmed unaffected end to end. Full suite: 34 checks, all pass. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).
