### 2026-08-25 — Real bug fixed (user-reported live): Zoho Books itself rejected the tax_id/tax_id2 approach — "Both CGST and SGST has to be applied"

Testing with the real Tax IDs produced a genuine Zoho Books validation error: `{"code":"ERROR","message":"Both CGST and SGST has to be applied, as this is an intra state transaction"}`. This confirmed `tax_id2` was a wrong guess — it isn't a real dual-tax field on a Books line item. Asked the user directly whether a combined "GST18"-style Tax Group already exists in Settings → Taxes (separate from the two individual CGST/SGST entries) — confirmed **no**, only the two separate entries exist.

**Root cause**: Zoho Books' real mechanism for a compound GST split (CGST+SGST) on one line item is a single **Tax Group** that bundles the two component rates into one combined reference — a line item applies just ONE `tax_id` (the group's own id), and Books internally splits it into CGST+SGST for an intra-state transaction. Applying the two component ids directly via `tax_id`/`tax_id2` isn't a supported mechanism.

**Fix**: replaced `cgst_tax_id`/`sgst_tax_id` + `tax_id`/`tax_id2` with a single `gst_group_tax_id`, applied as the line item's only `tax_id`. Currently a placeholder (`<<REPLACE_WITH_GST18_TAX_GROUP_ID_ONCE_CREATED_IN_BOOKS>>`) — **blocking** until you:
1. Create a Tax Group in Zoho Books (Settings → Taxes → New Tax Group), combining the existing CGST 9%/SGST 9% entries into one (e.g. named "GST18").
2. Provide that Tax Group's own id (a different id from the two you already gave — those were the individual component ids, not the group's).

Not independently testable from here (same recurring caveat for this file's real Zoho Books integration surface) — this is now a **known, understood** blocker rather than a guess, backed by Zoho Books' own explicit error text.

### 2026-08-25 — User declined the Tax Group; verified against Zoho's own official API docs, then implemented the explicitly-chosen alternative

User: "i dont want to use combine tax." Rather than guess a third approach, looked up Zoho Books' own official API documentation directly ([Taxes API](https://www.zoho.com/books/api/v3/taxes/), [Invoices API](https://www.zoho.com/books/api/v3/invoices/)) — confirmed definitively: a Tax Group is the **only** API mechanism to apply CGST+SGST as real tax on a single line item; there is no `tax_id2` or per-line dual-tax field at all (that was this session's own wrong guess, not a real option Zoho ever offered).

Explained clearly that creating the Tax Group is standard one-time setup (doesn't touch the existing CGST/SGST rates, just adds one reference pointing to both) and offered the real trade-off of the alternative before either was chosen. User explicitly chose: **add CGST/SGST as plain line items instead of real Zoho Books tax**.

**Fix**: `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` now builds **three** line items — Service Charge, then CGST 9% and SGST 9% each as their own already-computed amount (from `computeGstBreakup()` in `widget.html`) — with no `tax_id` anywhere in the script at all. `Round Off` still applies as the invoice's own `adjustment` field, unchanged. Fixed a string-vs-number comparison risk while at it: `cgstAmt`/`sgstAmt` are initialized as the literal number `0` (not the string `"0"`) before their `> 0` checks — comparing a string to a number was exactly the class of bug chased down earlier today (the `unit_price`/`toDecimal()` saga).

**Known, accepted trade-off — documented in the file's own header, not hidden**: since CGST/SGST aren't tagged as real tax via `tax_id`, Zoho Books' own GST return / GSTR reports will **not** recognize these amounts as tax — they'll show as ordinary line-item revenue. If GST filing directly off Books reports ever becomes a requirement, this will need revisiting (most likely via the Tax Group this same entry describes).

Not independently testable from here (same recurring caveat) — please retest with the same dummy data. This time nothing tax-related should be rejected, since no tax is being applied through Zoho's own tax engine at all.

### 2026-08-25 — Real bug fixed (user-reported live): `invokeCustomApi` failed with the generic `{code:9430}` Zoho crash message, not a graceful error

User clicked "Send Payment Link" on a real ticket (Total_Service_Fee 2000) and got `{code: 9430, message: "There is an error while executing Custom API."}` — Zoho's own generic execution-crash message, not one of this script's own `{"code":"ERROR",...}` JSON returns. That distinction matters: it means something threw *before* reaching this script's own error handling.

**Root cause**: the previous version's parameter-validation/setup code (building `referenceNumberResolved`, `serviceChargesAmt`, `roundOffAmt`, `descriptionText`) sat **outside** the `try`/`catch` block — only STEP 1 onward was wrapped. Any exception in that setup code, or in STEP 1's own still-untested `type :GET` customer-search request, would escape uncaught and surface as this opaque Zoho-level crash instead of a readable message.

**Fix**: the entire function body is now inside one `try` block, from right after the constant declarations to the very end, with the existing single `catch (mainEx)` at the bottom. No logic changed — purely a matter of which lines are inside the safety net. Any future failure (including whatever actually caused this one) will now come back as a real, readable `{"code":"ERROR","message":"..."}` instead of the generic 9430.

**Tested**: brace structure verified balanced end-to-end (one `try`, one `catch`, correctly nested with the existing inner email try/catch). Not independently testable beyond that from this repo — please retest via "Send Payment Link" or the Execute dialog; if it fails again, the message should now say exactly why.

### 2026-08-25 — Real bug fixed (user-reported live): "Unable to convert the input text to decimal"

Good news first: the previous fix worked exactly as intended — this came back as a real, specific message (`{"code":"ERROR","message":"Exception: Error at line : 35, Unable to convert the input text to decimal"}`) instead of the opaque `9430` crash. `serviceCharges.toString().toDecimal()` is the only `.toDecimal()` call in the current design, so that's almost certainly where this happened.

**Note on file sync**: the local copy of this file had again diverged from what I'd last written — the header documentation was gone and the return-value variable had been renamed to `invoiceURL` (evidently edited directly, likely while pasting between here and Zoho's own editor). Applied the fixes below on top of that current state rather than overwriting it, since the actual logic was intact and equivalent.

**Fix, two parts**:
1. Added explicit `null`/`""` guards for `serviceCharges` *before* calling `.toDecimal()` on it — a missing/blank value now returns a clear message immediately instead of reaching the parse at all.
2. Wrapped the `.toDecimal()` call itself in its own narrow `try`/`catch` that echoes the exact raw `serviceCharges` string back in the error message if parsing still fails. The outer catch already told us *that* something failed to parse; this tells us *what* — so if this happens again, the actual malformed value will be right there in the message instead of needing another guess.

Not independently testable from here — please retest via "Send Payment Link" or the Execute dialog. If this specific error recurs, the new message will include the literal `serviceCharges` value received, which should make the real cause obvious (e.g. scientific notation, a stray character, or an actually-empty value that somehow bypassed the null/blank guards).

### 2026-08-25 — Real bug fixed (user-reported live, with the restored diagnostic log): Custom API returned "No valid priced issue in issuesJson" even with confirmed-good data

The restored diagnostic log immediately paid off: it surfaced the Custom API's real error — `{"code":"ERROR","message":"No valid priced issue in issuesJson","url":""}`. User then confirmed the ticket's real `Rate_Breakup` value directly: `"BATTERY JUMPSTART: Rs. 499\nAIR FILL: Rs. 499\n"`. Manually traced this exact string through `parseRateBreakupIssues()` — it correctly produces 2 valid issues (`BATTERY JUMPSTART`/499, `AIR FILL`/499), matching the already-tested behavior. So the widget was sending good data; the bug was purely on the Deluge side.

**Root cause**: in the `for each issueMap in issuesList` loop, every single issue was being skipped by `if(amt <= 0) continue;` — meaning `issueMap.get("amount")`'s return value didn't compare the way a plain number does. `toJSONList()` most likely doesn't hand back a directly-comparable decimal for a JSON *number* value the same way it does for other types.

**Fix** (in [`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge)): the amount is now always normalized through `.toString().toDecimal()` before the `<= 0` comparison — safe regardless of whatever native type `get("amount")` actually returns, since `.toString()` accepts any type and `.toDecimal()` reliably parses the resulting text back into a comparable decimal. Also improved the "No valid priced issue" error to include how many raw issues *were* parsed (`issuesList.size()`), so a future failure like this is diagnosable from the error message alone.

**Not independently testable from here** (same recurring caveat for this file) — the widget-side data is already confirmed correct by test and by this exact real string being manually traced through it; only the Deluge-side numeric handling changed. Please paste the updated file into the Custom API's editor and retry.

**2026-08-25 (same day, user request: "also remove unwanted logs")** — stripped the original demo script's step-by-step `info` trace (`"========== INVOICE CREATION STARTED =========="`, `"📋 Building invoice..."`, `"🌐 Sending to API..."`, `"✅ Invoice created:"`, `"📄 Invoice URL:"`, `"📧 Sending email..."`, `"✅ Email sent"`, `"========== ✅ COMPLETE ✅ =========="`), plus a couple added earlier the same day for the new parsing logic — several of those were dumping the entire invoice payload (including the real invoice URL) into the execution log on every successful run, not just failures. What's left in [`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge) are only the logs that flag something actually went wrong or unusual: a missing ticket, a skipped non-priced `Rate_Breakup` line, the `Rate_Breakup`-empty fallback, a failed invoice/email call, and the main exception handler. No behavior changed — only logging.

### 2026-08-25 — Real bug found and fixed, thanks to the restored diagnostic: `.get()` returns `null` on a `toJSONList()` element even though its own `.toString()` proves the data is there

The diagnostic worked exactly as intended. User's next test returned: `[raw={'amount':499,'description':'BATTERY JUMPSTART'} description=null amountRaw=null]` — the item's own `.toString()` clearly shows both keys with correct values, yet `issueMap.get("description")` and `issueMap.get("amount")` both still returned `null`. Whatever `toJSONList()` hands back per-element isn't a plain Deluge `Map` for `.get()` purposes, even though it *prints* like one.

**Fix**: each raw element is now round-tripped through text and re-parsed with `toMap()` — a genuinely native Deluge Map — before `.get()` is called on it at all: `fixedMap = rawItem.toString().toMap(); description = fixedMap.get("description");` etc. This is the same "normalize through text first" pattern as the earlier `toDecimal()` fix, just applied to the whole item instead of only its amount.

**Note on file sync this round**: the local copy of [`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`](custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge) (and briefly this README) reverted to an earlier state between rounds, likely something IDE-side while the file was open in an editor — the deployed Zoho script still had the diagnostic version, which is how the useful error came back at all. Rewrote the file from scratch this round with both the diagnostic and this new fix included, to avoid losing anything again.

**Not independently testable from here** (same recurring caveat) — please paste this into the Custom API editor, save, and retry. If it works, the invoice should now show 2 line items (BATTERY JUMPSTART ₹499, AIR FILL ₹499, total ₹998) and the WhatsApp message should carry a real payment link. If it still fails, `debugFirstItem` will now show the FIXED map's extraction, which should make the very next step obvious.

**Separate, unrelated item spotted in the same log** (not investigated — flagging only): `sendWhatsAppTemplate: invokeCustomApi failed for location {code: 10020, message: "This Custom API can be invoked only from widgets in a portal."}`. This is a different Custom API call (the "location" WhatsApp template, not `onDemandInvoicing`) and suggests that specific call happened outside a recognized portal/widget context — possibly a preview/test environment rather than the live embedded widget. ### 2026-08-25 — Targeted fix: `unit_price` now uses the raw parsed value's own `.toString()`, not the `Decimal`'s

User confirmed via a real Zoho Books invoice screenshot: descriptions (`BATTERY JUMPSTART`, `AIR FILL`) are correct, but `Rate`/`Amount`/`Sub Total`/`Total`/`Balance Due` are all still `0.00`. Didn't yet have a fresh `"debug"` field to inspect, so rather than request another round-trip blind, reasoned from the one difference that actually matters: the **original, proven-working hardcoded script** (which really did produce `total_amount: 750` live) put a **plain Deluge number's own `.toString()`** into `unit_price` — it never went through `toDecimal()` first. This script's `amt` **is** a `toDecimal()`-converted value, and `amt.toString()` (a `Decimal`'s own re-stringification) may format differently — extra precision, grouping, etc. — in a way Zoho Books silently can't parse into a real rate, while the rest of the line item (description, quantity) still gets created fine since those don't depend on that value.

**Fix**: `amt` (via `toDecimal()`) is now used *only* for the numeric `> 0` comparison and the `computedTotal` sum — the actual `unit_price` put into the line item is `amtRaw.toString()`, the plain text straight from the parsed JSON, matching how the original working script's own plain number behaved.

**Diagnostics kept, extended**: `firstLineItemDebug` (in the `"debug"` field of the SUCCESS response) now shows `amt.toString()` and `amtRaw.toString()` side by side, so if this still isn't the whole story, the exact difference between the two will be visible immediately.

**Not independently testable from here** — please retest via the same Execute dialog (or a real "Send Payment Link" click). If the invoice now shows real amounts, this was it. If not, paste the `"debug"` field — seeing both string forms side by side should make the actual formatting difference obvious.

### 2026-08-27 — New feature (increment 1): Vendor Accounting Level 1 fee formula

User shared a new "Zoho - Vendor Accounting.pdf" spec — a two-level system (Operations Approval, then Accounts Payable/TDS/GST) for paying vendors for completed jobs, entirely separate from customer-facing invoicing. Before writing anything, audited the codebase against it: this exact feature was already scoped in detail in `Accounting_Integration_Estimate.csv`/`_Detailed.csv` (repo root, 2026-08-19) — Vendor Accounting Level 1 ≈13-20 hrs, Level 2 ≈20-28 hrs, out of a much larger combined estimate (152.5-218.5 hrs across Vendor + Client Accounting + On-Demand Invoicing + branding). Confirmed almost nothing of this specific piece exists yet — see the audit summary in chat for the full field-by-field breakdown.

**User decisions confirmed 2026-08-27** (see the code's own header comment for the same list):
- Vendor rate card (Base Fee/Base KM/KM Rate) is **per-vendor**, not per-vehicle-type — deliberately a different granularity from the existing unwired `KM_Slab_Repair`/`KM_Slab_Tow_2W_FBT`/`KM_Slab_Tow_4W_FBT`/`KM_Slab_Tow_4W_MT` fields on `Vendors_Report`, which *do* vary by vehicle type. Flagged as an open inconsistency, not silently resolved either way.
- TDS Rate hardcoded at 1% for now (matches the spec's own worked example).
- `Extras` is a genuinely new field, not a reuse of the existing `Extra_Payable`.
- The eventual Operations-Approval/Accounts-Payable screens will live inside `getRescueTicket`, gated by the existing `isOperationsManager()` check.

**Built this round** (`getRescueTicket/app/widget.html`, new isolated section right after `maybeCreateOnDemandInvoice()` — does not touch that function, `onDemandInvoicing()`, `computeGstBreakup()`, or anything else):
- `computeVendorKmFee(actualKm, baseKm, kmRate, slabKm)` — the PDF's own ROUNDUP-slab formula (`kmRate × ROUNDUP[(actualKm − baseKm)/slabKm]`, 0 if at/under baseKm), branch-agnostic by design — RSR vs TOW only differs in which distance field the *caller* passes in (`Vendor_Distance` vs `Vendor_Round_Trip_Distance`), not in this function's own logic.
- `computeVendorFee(input)` — Gross Fee/Net Fee/Balance-before-TDS, composed exactly like the PDF's own worked example (Base Fee + KM Fee = Gross; + Extras = Net; − Cash Collected = Balance).

**Deliberately NOT built yet** (next increments, once the fields below exist): the actual Operations-Approval screen, the `Settlement_Status` transition/lock action, all of Level 2 (TDS/GST calc, batch multi-case payment entry, Zoho Books Bill + Payment Made), and the Vendor Portal statement view.

**Fields that need to be created in Zoho Creator before the next increment** (I cannot create Creator fields myself):
- On `Vendors_Report`: `Base_Fee` (Number), `Base_KM` (Number), `KM_Rate` (Number) — the new per-vendor rate card.
- On the ticket form/report (`Create_Case`/`Agent_Ticket_Report`): `Cash_Collected` (Number), `Extras` (Number), `Vendor_KM_Fee`/`Vendor_Gross_Fee`/`Vendor_Net_Fee`/`Vendor_Balance_Before_TDS` (Number, computed/readonly), `Settlement_Status` (Dropdown — exact value list still to be confirmed, likely something like `Pending Approval`/`Payable`/`Paid`), `Ops_Approval_Comments` (Multi-line, per the spec's own "Comments" field).
- `Vendor_Round_Trip_Distance` (already exists) needs to become editable at this new Operations-Approval stage specifically — currently `readonly:true` everywhere.

**Still open, blocking the next increment**: which `KM_Slab_*` field a TOW job should actually use (needs to know what "FBT"/"MT" mean and how a ticket's own Vehicle maps to one of the three) — RSR has no such ambiguity (only one slab field, `KM_Slab_Repair`).

**Tested** — new suite (`test_vendor_accounting_level1.js`, 10 checks): the ROUNDUP-slab formula verified mechanically against its own textual definition (at-boundary, mid-slab, exactly-one-slab, into-a-second-slab, zero-slab guard, blank-input guard), and the full Gross/Net/Balance composition verified against the PDF's own exact worked numbers (Base 1000 + KM 500 = Gross 1500; + Extras 200 = Net 1700; − Cash Collected 300 = Balance 1400). ### 2026-08-27 (same day) — TOW slab-field resolution wired: FBT/MT confirmed as real `Vehicle_Issue` names

User confirmed `Base_Fee`/`Base_KM`/`KM_Rate` created on `Vendors_Report`, and clarified FBT/MT are real `Vehicle_Issue` values (Service_Type TOW only) — not a vehicle attribute as originally guessed. This closes the one blocker flagged in the previous entry: which of the 4 existing `KM_Slab_*` fields a given TOW ticket should use.

**Built**:
- `resolveIssueLabels(rawValue, optionsList)` — like the existing `lookupNamesFor()` but returns the raw array of resolved labels instead of one comma-joined string, so an exact match against "FBT"/"MT" doesn't rely on fragile substring-matching inside a joined string. Doesn't touch `lookupNamesFor()` or any of its existing callers.
- `vendorKmSlabFieldFor(record)` — RSR always resolves to `KM_Slab_Repair`. TOW resolves based on the vehicle's own Category (2W/4W, via `CONFIG.vehicleCategoryField` — the exact same field `calcQuote()` already uses for the customer-quote side) combined with whether "FBT" or "MT" is among the ticket's resolved issues: `FBT+2W→KM_Slab_Tow_2W_FBT`, `FBT+4W→KM_Slab_Tow_4W_FBT`, `MT+4W→KM_Slab_Tow_4W_MT`. **`MT+2W` has no matching field on `Vendors_Report` at all** (confirmed — there's no `KM_Slab_Tow_2W_MT`) — returns `null` and logs a clear diagnostic warning rather than guessing a substitute; same for any TOW ticket whose issues don't include FBT or MT at all.
- `computeVendorFeeForTicket(record, vendorRaw)` — ties the pure formula functions to real record data: picks `Vendor_Distance` (RSR) or `Vendor_Round_Trip_Distance` (TOW) as the actual-km input, resolves the slab field, pulls the assigned vendor's own rate-card fields, and returns the same `{kmFee, grossFee, netFee, balanceBeforeTds}` shape. Returns `null` (not a guessed fee) if the slab field couldn't be resolved or no vendor record was passed in.

**Tested** — extended suite (now 18 checks total): all 4 valid RSR/TOW/category/issue combinations resolve to the correct slab field; the two unmapped cases (`MT+2W`, and a TOW ticket with neither FBT nor MT) both correctly return `null` with a diagnostic warning logged, confirmed via the actual console output; full record-level integration verified for both RSR and TOW end-to-end against hand-computed expected fees; `null` vendorRaw handled without a throw. Full existing suite (37 checks) re-confirmed unaffected. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

**Still open, same as before**: the `MT+2W` gap — either this combination genuinely never occurs in practice (no 2W tow job is ever issue-typed "MT"), or a `KM_Slab_Tow_2W_MT` field needs to be added to `Vendors_Report`. Needs your confirmation, not guessed at.

### 2026-08-31 — VALIDATIONS ON AGENT CLOSURE: new Not-Converted-vs-paid-fee gate, and each Closure TSF term now gated on its own paid status

User's exact spec (4 numbered validations): (1) Closure Status = Not Null; (2) if Booking Fee OR Remaining Fee status = PAID, Closure Status cannot be Not Converted; (3) if Closure Status = Completed/Cancelled, Total Service Fee = Booking Fee(PAID) + Remaining Fee(PAID) − Refund Amount(PAID); (4) if Client Charge > 0, Client Charge + Rescue Charge = Total Service Fee.

**Audited each against the live code before touching anything** (see chat for the full table): #1 was already fully enforced (`Closure_Status` `req:true`). #4 was already built and live-tested (2026-08-25 fix), but as a 3-term formula (`Client_Charge + Rescue_Charge + Booking_Fee = TSF`), not the 2-term wording in today's spec. #2 didn't exist at all. #3 was structurally built (`computeClosureTSF()`) but never actually gated on "PAID status" per term — it just summed the raw stored numbers.

**The real blocker for #2 and #3**: both need "was Booking Fee genuinely confirmed PAID," independent of Remaining Fee's own later status. `Payment_Status` is the ONE field reused for both the Booking Fee (Step 2) and Remaining Fee (Step 8) stages (confirmed live 2026-08-03) — the moment Remaining Fee's stage completes, it overwrites `Payment_Status` with its own status, destroying Booking Fee's earlier "PAID". Didn't want to guess a workaround for a real-money gate, so asked instead of assuming:

**User decisions (2026-08-31, asked via AskUserQuestion)**:
- Booking Fee's paid signal: **infer from Status** (no new field) rather than adding a dedicated frozen field. Leans on `statusStillEarly()` — a guard already proven and applied across every stage in this file specifically so Status never gets recomputed backward into an earlier value once a ticket has genuinely moved past it. `bookingFeeConfirmedPaid(r) = Number(r.Booking_Fee)>0 && !statusStillEarly(r, ["SERVICE FEE QUOTE","BOOKING FEE PENDING"])` — i.e. the ticket has moved past the Booking Fee gate (which only happens once it's confirmed PAID, or the fee was zero — the extra `Booking_Fee>0` check excludes that second case, since "nothing was ever due" shouldn't count as "paid").
- Validation #4's formula: **keep the existing 3-term version** (`Client_Charge + Rescue_Charge + Booking_Fee = TSF`) — it's the live-tested 2026-08-25 fix; reverting to the literal 2-term wording would reintroduce the exact mismatch bug that fix resolved.

**Built**:
- `bookingFeeConfirmedPaid(r)` / `remainingFeeConfirmedPaid(r)` (new, right after `remainingFeePaidOnline()`). Remaining Fee needed no inference at all — `Payment_received` is its own dedicated field, never touched by the Booking Fee stage, and agent-editable even in Acceptance_Mode="Manual" — already a direct, reliable fact.
- `saveClosure()`: new gate, checked before any save — `Closure_Status==="Not Converted" && (bookingFeeConfirmedPaid(r) || remainingFeeConfirmedPaid(r))` blocks with a clear toast instead of silently closing.
- `computeClosureTSF()`: `Booking_Fee` now only counts if `bookingFeeConfirmedPaid(r)`, `Remaining_Fee` only if `remainingFeeConfirmedPaid(r)` — a quoted-but-never-collected fee no longer inflates the reconciled Total Service Fee. Refund needed no new gate — it's entered fresh at Closure itself (same atomic save as `Closure_Status`), so there's no earlier stage that could have overwritten it; `Refund_Due==="Yes"` already reliably means "count this refund."

**Tested** against the real shipped code (Node-VM harness, functions extracted directly from `widget.html` via brace-matching) — 18 checks: `bookingFeeConfirmedPaid` correctly resolves true/false across paid/pending/fee-free/blank-Status combinations, including the exact scenario this whole fix exists for (Booking Fee paid, ticket fully progressed, `Payment_Status` since overwritten by the Remaining Fee stage — still correctly reads as paid); `remainingFeeConfirmedPaid` correctly keys off `Payment_received` alone, any method; `computeClosureTSF` correctly excludes an unconfirmed term from the sum and still nets out a confirmed refund; `closureCountsAsFeeBearing` re-confirmed unaffected; `saveClosure()`'s new gate confirmed present and blocking (not just warning) via source inspection. Re-ran the full On-Demand-Invoicing/WhatsApp suite (38 checks) and Vendor Accounting Level 1 suite (19 checks) to confirm no regression — both still pass. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-09-01 (later still) — Real bug fixed: a brand-new ticket never appeared in a second open tab

User-reported live, with side-by-side screenshots of the same dashboard URL open in two tabs: one tab's "New & Open" count was 3, the other's stayed at 2, well past the 30s auto-refresh interval — "auto ticket is not showing."

**Root cause**: `pollDashboardForMechanicUpdates()` only ever re-rendered the dashboard lists (`renderReportLists()`) when it detected a genuine status **change** on a ticket it already knew about (`oldStatus!=null && oldStatus!==newStatus`). A brand-new ticket — `oldStatus==null`, since it wasn't in the last snapshot — was explicitly treated as "not something that changed while the agent was watching" and silently folded into the snapshot with no re-render at all. So a second tab's poll cycle would see the new ticket, quietly remember it, and never actually show it unless some *other*, unrelated status change also happened to occur in the same 30s window.

**Fix**: split the two effects apart. `hasNewTicket` (new — a ticket with no prior snapshot entry) still doesn't trigger the mechanic-update sound/toast (that notification is specifically for an *observed* status change on an already-known ticket, not a new arrival), but now correctly triggers `renderReportLists()` on its own, same as a genuine status change does. Checked `vendorTicket`'s own equivalent poll (`pollForNewCases()`) for the same class of bug — it doesn't have it, already renders on new cases.

**Tested** — new suite (`test_dashboard_auto_refresh.js`, 11 checks) extracting the real shipped function: a brand-new ticket alone now renders but doesn't notify; a genuine status change still does both; nothing changed at all still renders/notifies nothing (no wasted re-render every poll); a new ticket + a status change together fire both effects; the very first poll after a full baseline produces no false positives; the snapshot itself is always kept current regardless. Re-ran the full On-Demand-Invoicing (38), Agent Closure (18), and native-permission-check (16) suites — all unaffected. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-09-01 — New: `CreateServiceTicket` workflow — Web_Form submission auto-creates a Create_Case ticket

User has a public `Web_Form` (Name, Email, Phone Number, Vehicle, Vehicle Issue, Vehicle Registration Number, Lead Source, Time of service, Schedule_Date, Service Time) and wants a Deluge workflow (Web_Form → Created or Edited → Successful form submission → `CreateServiceTicket`) that creates a real `Create_Case` ticket on submission.

**Audited the real requirement first**: `Create_Case.Client` and `.Vehicle` are both required Lookups; `.Vehicle_Issue` is a required multi-select Lookup; `.Vehicle_Status` is a required dropdown — none of these can be safely filled from the Web Form's own plain-text fields without either a name-matching lookup step (Vehicle/Vehicle Issue) or a genuine business decision (what Client a web lead belongs to, what Vehicle_Status to assume). Asked the user to confirm these before writing anything — **user explicitly said to use only limited fields instead**, i.e., skip all of the above rather than build the matching/decision logic.

**Built** (`getRescueTicket/workflows/CreateServiceTicket.deluge`, new folder — this is a Zoho Creator **workflow** script, not a Custom API, so kept separate from `custom-apis/`): creates a `Create_Case` record from only the plain fields (`Customer_Name`, `Phone_Number1`/`Phone_Number` mirrored, `Email`, `Vehicle_Registration_Number`, `Time_of_service`, `Schedule_Date`, `Service_Time`), `Lead_Source` hardcoded to `"Website"` (vs. the agent widget's own always-`"Call"`), `Status` set to `"SERVICE FEE QUOTE"` (same initial status a manually-created ticket gets, so it lands in the agent dashboard's "New & Open" bucket), and `Case_ID` generated after insert with the identical `"RSID"+last-6-characters-of-ID` formula the agent widget itself uses. The raw customer-typed Vehicle/Vehicle Issue text is preserved in `Remarks` (not resolved to a real Lookup) so nothing is lost — an agent fills in the real `Client`/`Vehicle`/`Vehicle_Issue`/`Vehicle_Status` when they open the ticket.

**Two real risks flagged in the script itself, not yet confirmed live**:
1. Zoho may enforce `Client`/`Vehicle`/`Vehicle_Status`'s "Required" marker even for a Deluge-created record, the same way it does for the form UI — if so, this insert fails outright. If that happens, either mark those three fields "not required" on `Create_Case`, or come back and extend this script with real values.
2. `Web_Form`'s own field link names (`input.Name`, `input.Phone_Number`, etc.) are inferred from the field **labels** shown in a screenshot of `Web_Form_Report`, using Zoho's standard label→link-name rule — not independently confirmed. This exact repo has hit "the displayed label isn't the real link name" more than once already (`Vendors_Report.vendor_name`, `Technicians_Report.technician_name`). If the workflow errors on an unknown field/variable, check each `input.<name>` against `Web_Form`'s own field Link Name (form builder → field → View Properties) first.

**Not tested** — Deluge workflow scripts can't be exercised via this repo's own Node-VM harness. Please paste the script into the already-open `CreateServiceTicket` Deluge editor, publish, submit a real test Web Form entry, and report back: did the ticket get created at all (checks risk #1), and did every field land correctly (checks risk #2)?

### 2026-09-02 — New sibling widget: `customerPage`, the public no-login Customer page

User asked for a new widget on the "Customer page" handling four customer-facing requests: Booking Payment, Remaining Payment, Location sharing, Feedback. See `../customerPage/README.md` for the full build — summarized here for this file's own changes.

**A real security finding shaped the whole design**, investigated before writing anything: the standard Zoho mechanism for public-page data access (`ZOHO.CREATOR.PUBLISH.getRecordById`) requires publishing the entire report behind one shared access token that isn't scoped per-record — meaning any customer holding that token could enumerate any other customer's data on `Create_Case` (names, phones, financial data) just by guessing a different record ID. `customerPage` instead never touches `Create_Case`/`Agent_Ticket_Report` directly at all — everything routes through 3 new narrow Custom APIs.

**This file's own changes**: added `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL` (⚠ blank until the public page is actually published — everything below degrades to today's exact existing behavior until it's set) and two helpers, `buildCustomerPageUrl()`/`customerNameWithPageLink()`. Wired the new link into:
- `Send_Payment_Link` / `Send_Payment_Link1` — folds the link into the existing WhatsApp message in place of/alongside the raw Zoho Books URL.
- Both `Send_Link_For_The_Breakdown_Location` copies — folds the link into the *same single* WhatsApp template parameter that already just held the customer's name, deliberately not adding a second parameter (a param-count mismatch against a real WhatsApp Business template already caused a real outage once this session — see `Send_Payment_Link`'s own `(#132018)` history).
- **`Send_Link_For_The_Drop_Location` — real bug fixed as a side effect**: this button had no `action` at all before now; clicking it did nothing beyond flipping its own checkbox field. Wired the same safe way as its breakdown-location sibling.
- New `Customer_Feedback_Requested` checkbox on the Final Closure screen, mirroring the existing `Google_Feedback_Requested` pattern exactly — sends a new `customer_feedback_link` WhatsApp template (name not yet confirmed against a real approved template).

**Tested** — new suite (`test_customer_page_widget.js`, 28 checks) covering both this file's new helpers and the full `customerPage` widget. Full existing regression suite (102 checks across 5 other test files) re-run — no interaction. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

Opening a case shows read-only Case System fields (Vendor, Case ID, Date, Status, Service, Vehicle, Base Fee, Travel Kms for RSR) plus editable Extras/Comments/Vendor Round-trip KM (TOW only, per the PDF's own Source/Editable columns), with a live-recalculating fee preview (KM Fee/Gross/Net/Balance) via the existing `computeVendorFeeForTicket()`. ### 2026-09-04 (later still) — New: Vendor Accounting Level 2 (Accounts Payable) — real Zoho Books Bill + Vendor Payment

Second half of the Vendor Accounting PDF spec, per explicit user request to proceed with the full implementation. Four decisions confirmed before writing anything (do not silently change without asking again):
- TDS rate is **per-vendor** (`Vendors_Report.TDS_Percentage`), not a single global value.
- Builds a **real Zoho Books Bill + Vendor Payment** this round (not Creator-fields-only) — bigger scope, deliberately accepted as the riskier option.
- **GST is out of scope** this round — no `GST_Payable`/`GST_Paid` fields or UI; `Vendor_GST_Number` stays unwired.
- Batch payment model: Payment Date + UTR No entered **once**, shared across every case in the same transaction — but each case's own `Vendor_Amount_Paid` is set to that case's own computed Final Payable, not a split/shared figure.

**Zoho Books mechanics researched before writing the Deluge script** (not guessed, given the real financial-record risk of getting this wrong): the Bill is created for the **full Net Fee per case** (gross liability, one line item per case). TDS is applied via the Vendor Payment's own `bills[].tax_amount_withheld` field ("Tax Amount Withheld during Bill Payment," Global/India/Australia editions) rather than the Bill's own `is_tds_applied`/`tds_tax_id` — the latter needs a pre-configured TDS tax rate/id per distinct percentage in Books, which doesn't work cleanly with a per-vendor rate. Cash Collected (money the vendor already received directly from the customer) is handled as a **Vendor Credit** applied to the Bill instead — a different mechanism from TDS, since it isn't a tax withholding. These three (`amount_applied` + `tax_amount_withheld` + the applied vendor credit) sum to exactly the Bill's own total, since `Final Payable = Net Fee − TDS − Cash Collected` by definition — the Bill should close to zero balance.

**Built**:
- `computeVendorTdsAndPayable(netFee, tdsPercent, cashCollected)` — pure formula, tested against the PDF's own worked example (Net Fee 1700, TDS 1% → TDS 17, Final Payable 1383).
- `isReadyForVendorPayment(r)` — a case qualifies once Level 1 approved it (`Settlement_Status="Payable"`) and it isn't already paid (`Vendor_Payment_Status` blank/not `"Paid"`).
- New **Accounts Payable** screen (`accountsPayableBtn`/`accountsPayableView`), same `IS_OPS_MANAGER` gate as Level 1 for now (no separate Operations-vs-Accounts role was requested) — a vendor picker + date-range form, then a checklist of that vendor's payable cases in range with live-updating totals (Net Fee/TDS/Cash Collected/Final Payable across only the checked rows), a shared Payment Date + UTR No, and a "Record Payment" button.
- New Custom API `recordVendorPayment.deluge` (mirrors `onDemandInvoicing.deluge`'s conventions — whole-body try/catch, `zoho_books_connection`, hand-built JSON responses): find-or-create the vendor as a Books contact (caches `Zoho_Books_Vendor_ID` on `Vendors_Report` so repeat payments skip the lookup), create the Bill, create+apply a Vendor Credit for Cash Collected if any, record the Vendor Payment with TDS withheld declared inline. Returns `bill_id`/`vendor_payment_id`/`books_vendor_id`, which the widget then writes back onto every selected `Create_Case` record (`TDS_Amount`, `Final_Payable`, `Vendor_Amount_Paid`, `Vendor_Payment_Date`, `Vendor_UTR_No`, `Vendor_Payment_Status="Paid"`, `Zoho_Books_Bill_ID`).
- Two small date-boundary helpers (`startOfDay`/`endOfDay`/`ymd`) added — didn't previously exist in this file (only in `vendorPerformance`/`agentPerformance`), needed for the date-range filter here.

**New fields needed in Zoho before this works live** (none created yet — same "specify then wait for confirmation" pattern as every other new field this session): `Vendors_Report.TDS_Percentage` (Number), `Vendors_Report.Zoho_Books_Vendor_ID` (Single Line), and on `Create_Case`: `TDS_Amount`/`Final_Payable`/`Vendor_Amount_Paid` (Number ×3), `Vendor_Payment_Date` (Date), `Vendor_UTR_No` (Single Line), `Vendor_Payment_Status` (Dropdown: `Payable`, `Paid` — distinct from the existing customer-side `Payment_Status`), `Zoho_Books_Bill_ID` (Single Line).

**STILL OPEN in the Deluge script itself, needs your input before this works live** (left as empty-string placeholders that fail loudly rather than silently posting to the wrong account, same pattern as `onDemandInvoicing.deluge`'s own `gst18_tax_id` before it was confirmed): `EXPENSE_ACCOUNT_ID` (which Zoho Books Chart of Accounts entry the Bill's line items post to) and `PAID_THROUGH_ACCOUNT_ID` (which bank/cash account the Vendor Payment is recorded from).

**Not independently live-tested — expect at least one bug-fix round**, same as `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` needed when it was first built: this is researched-but-unconfirmed Zoho Books API behavior (`tax_amount_withheld`, Vendor Credit application), not something exercisable from this repo's own Node-VM test harness.

**Tested** — new suite (`test_vendor_accounting_level2.js`, 22 checks): the pure TDS/Final-Payable formula against the PDF's worked example plus edge cases (0% TDS, fractional TDS rounding); `isReadyForVendorPayment()`'s full status matrix including the "don't double-pay an already-paid case" check; the new date helpers; and wiring checks (button/screen/showView/save-payload all present and consistent). Full remaining suite re-run — same pre-existing, already-proven-unrelated failures as before, nothing new. Syntax-checked (widget) and brace-balance-checked (Deluge script, which can't run outside Zoho) and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-09-04 (later still) — Live-testing round on `recordVendorPayment.deluge`: two real bugs found and fixed

First live test attempt, exactly as expected to need a round of fixes (same as `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` did originally).

**Bug 1 — Deluge syntax, "Improper Statement" at parse time**: `for each i in 0.to(caseLabelsList.size() - 1)` is not valid Deluge — Deluge has no C-style/JS-style numeric for-loop at all (confirmed via Zoho's own documentation, not guessed). Fixed to `for each index i in caseLabelsList`, Deluge's real construct for iterating a list's indices — the loop body itself didn't need to change. (An earlier, unrelated fix in the same session also stripped all em-dashes/embedded quotes from this file's comments, suspected as a possible contributor to the same error — that turned out not to be the actual cause, but is harmless to have done and rules it out for future files.)

**Bug 2 — real Zoho Books rejection, "Invalid value passed for bill_number"**: unlike Invoices, Zoho Books' Bills have no auto-generate-number preference — a Bill is meant to carry the *vendor's own* invoice number, so the API expects the caller to supply `bill_number` explicitly; this script wasn't setting it at all. Fixed by building one from the UTR (the real bank reference the user enters) plus the first case's label — `"VP-" + firstCaseLabel + "-" + utrNo` — giving each Bill a readable, traceable identifier tied to the real payment reference, using data already available as parameters rather than inventing a new counter/timestamp scheme.

**Not yet confirmed**: whether the Bill now creates successfully end-to-end through to the Vendor Payment — this was the very next test to run after this fix.

**Bug 3 — real, live-triggered: hand-built JSON error responses broke on a real Zoho Books error message**: after fixing Bug 2, the vendor-contact-creation step hit a genuine Zoho Books rejection (a duplicate/invalid vendor name message) — but the widget then failed with `response.result wasn't valid JSON`, hiding the real error entirely. Root cause: every error return in this script was a hand-concatenated string like `"...\"" + createContactErr + "\"..."` — if the upstream Zoho Books message itself contains a `"` character (as this one did), the hand-built JSON breaks. This is the same fragile pattern `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` already uses (not touched here, no reported failure there yet, but the same latent risk exists). Fixed by rebuilding every return value (`errMap`/`okMap`) via Deluge's own `Map().put(...).toString()` JSON serialization instead of string concatenation, which escapes correctly no matter what any upstream message contains. No widget-side change needed — the JSON shape/keys are identical, only how they're built changed.

**Root business cause behind Bug 3's actual error message**: a Zoho Books vendor contact named "get rescue" already existed (created during an earlier failed test run — Step 1 of this script succeeds before the later steps that were failing on Bugs 1/2, so a contact got created but never linked back, since `Zoho_Books_Vendor_ID` only caches on full success). User manually found the existing contact and set `Vendors_Report.Zoho_Books_Vendor_ID` to its real id — the correct permanent fix (reuse the real contact), not a workaround. Held off on hardening the phone-search-then-create fallback with a name-based search too, since Zoho's own API docs didn't clearly confirm the exact filter parameter for it and three real bugs had already come from unverified guesses in this one file — revisit only if this recurs for a different vendor.

**Bug 4 — real widget-side bug, live-confirmed (404 "No Data Available")**: caching `Zoho_Books_Vendor_ID` back onto the vendor's own record used the existing `apiUpdate()` helper — but that helper is hardcoded to `CONFIG.reportName` (the ticket report, `Agent_Ticket_Report`), so it silently tried to update a ticket record using a `Vendors_Report` record's ID, which doesn't exist there. Fixed by adding `apiUpdateReport(report, id, data)`, a generic version mirroring the existing `apiGetReport()` pattern, and pointing the vendor-id cache write at `CONFIG.optionReports.vendors.report` instead.

**Bug 5 — real widget-side bug, live-confirmed ("Enter a valid date format for Vendor_Payment_Date")**: `Vendor_Payment_Date` was written using the raw `YYYY-MM-DD` string straight from the `<input type="date">` element. Every other Date-type field save in this file already converts through `fmtDate()` first (to Zoho Creator's own expected `DD-Mon-YYYY` shape, e.g. `04-Sep-2026`) — this new write path was the one place that didn't. Fixed to `Vendor_Payment_Date: fmtDate(paymentDate)`.

**Tested** — extended `test_vendor_accounting_level2.js` to 26 checks: confirms `apiUpdateReport()` exists and is what the vendor-id cache write actually calls (targeting `Vendors_Report`, not the ticket-report-only `apiUpdate()`), and that `Vendor_Payment_Date` goes through `fmtDate()` rather than the raw input value. Full remaining suite re-run — same pre-existing, already-proven-unrelated failures as before, nothing new. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-09-06 — WhatsApp: `sendWhatsAppTemplate()` now supports a real 3rd parameter; wired into the existing payment-link sends

Working through the WhatsApp Message Templates/Logic Flow PDFs' remaining gaps (see `technicianTicket`/`driverTicket`/`vendorTicket`'s own matching entries for the field-app side of this). This entry covers `getRescueTicket` specifically.
