Confirmed it already conditionally includes a `param3` in its outgoing `parameters` list if non-blank — extending the JS side was safe. Updated the payload to `{ ..., param3: params[2]||"" }` — existing 2-param call sites are completely unaffected, `param3` just defaults to `""` for them, same as `param1`/`param2` always did when omitted.

**Applied to the existing payment-link sends**: `Send_Payment_Link` and `Send_Payment_Link1` both already fold the payment/invoice link into `param2`'s own text (a 2026-08-25 fix, after discovering the real approved template doesn't render a separately-passed link argument at all — only `param2`'s own text does). Rather than touch that proven-working behavior, added the same link as a genuine 3rd array element too — purely additive: if the real template still only has 2 placeholders, WhatsApp simply ignores the extra one, and `param2`'s own already-working text is completely unchanged either way.

**Tested** — extended `test_field_apps_whatsapp.js` (shared suite, now 28 checks) confirming `param2`'s own text-building logic is byte-for-byte unchanged, and both call sites now pass the link as a 3rd array element. Re-ran `test_on_demand_invoicing.js` and `test_on_demand_invoicing_whatsapp.js` in full — both still 100% pass, since this is the exact flow those suites cover. Full remaining suite re-run — same pre-existing, already-proven-unrelated failures, nothing new. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`).

### 2026-09-07 — Location/distance fixes from the user-provided LOCATIONS-DISTANCES-DATETIME spec (round-trip calcs + office coordinates)

Gap analysis first (as requested), covering 8 location heads (A-H), 8 distance calcs (RSR-1/2/3, TOW-1/2/3/4/5), and 16 timestamps — done via 3 parallel Explore-agent investigations reading the actual shipped code, not guessing. Full writeup shared with the user; summarized here is only what actually changed in code.

**1. `OFFICE_LAT`/`OFFICE_LON` corrected** — the spec's exact office coordinate (`12.967945122837245, 77.6110507612277`) differed from the hardcoded value by ~13-20m. User confirmed: correct it. Same fix applied to all 5 files that hardcode this constant: `getRescueTicket`, `vendorTicket`, `driverTicket`, `technicianTicket`, `Ticket Kanban` (a 5th consumer found during the gap analysis, not previously tracked in this README).

**2. Round-trip distance calcs fixed to use the live/stored GPS point instead of the static ticket location** (user confirmed: fix these) — a real, recurring bug pattern: the vendor apps already capture an accurate live GPS fix at the Reach/Reach-Drop click, but the *round-trip* calc at that same moment was silently using the ticket's static breakdown/drop coordinates instead of that just-captured fix.
- **RSR-3(a)** (`markReachedRsr()`/`markReached()` in `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`): `Roundtrip_Distance` now routes through the live GPS fix taken at this exact Reach click (spec's "E"), not the static breakdown location (spec's "C"). Falls back to the static location if GPS is denied, same tolerance the rest of this app already uses.
- **TOW-4(a)** (`reachedDrop()` in `vendorTicket`/`driverTicket`): the round trip now anchors on the stored `Reach_Location_Lat/Lon` (E, from the earlier Reach click) and this click's own live GPS fix (B), instead of the static breakdown/drop coordinates (C/A per spec).
- **TOW-4(c)** (`confirmCxRejectLoading()` in `vendorTicket`/`driverTicket`): this one already had an explicit 2026-08-04 "client-requested" comment saying the intended input was "driver location @ click of Reach breakdown location" (i.e. spec's E) — it just approximated that with the static breakdown coordinate because there was no dedicated field to read the real value from at the time. `Reach_Location_Lat/Lon` exists now, so this is a more precise match to the *same original intent*, not a change of intent.

**3. TOW-3 (`vehiclePicked()`) deliberately left unchanged** — flagged to the user rather than "fixed": this function already has its own explicit prior comment ("Mock says Drop distance/ETA are 'based on current GPS location of Driver'") calling for a *fresh* GPS fix at the Pickup click, whereas the new spec's literal "E to A" definition wants the *stored* Reach-time position instead. Real conflict between two different confirmed specs about what the origin point should be here — not something to silently pick a side on. Untouched pending the user's call.

**4. `Drop_Location_Arrival_Time` field type fixed**, `t:"text"` → `t:"datetime"` — this sibling field was missed when `RSP_Completion_Time`/`Reach_Time`/`Pickup_Time` were all upgraded on 2026-08-11 to avoid Zoho's "Enter a valid date format" rejection on ambiguous free-typed date strings. Same fix applied here.

**5. `Added_Time` stale-documentation fix** — `FIELDS.md` still described the 2026-09-04 "layout gap" bug as unresolved; the user had already confirmed it fixed live (real timestamps flowing through `vendorPerformance`/`agentPerformance`). Updated the note to reflect the confirmed fix, and flagged that `getRescueTicket`'s own dashboard timer reads the same field off the same report so should be covered transparently, though not independently re-verified for this specific widget.

**Not yet built** (proposed to the user, needs new Zoho fields before wiring): Task Confirmation Time, RSP Invite Time, Remaining Fee Receipt Time, RSP Closure Time, Task Closure Time (Agent) — see this changelog's own next entry once those are created.

**Tested** — new `test_locations_distances_2026_09_07.js` (32 checks): office-coordinate correction across all 5 files, RSR-3/TOW-4(a)/TOW-4(c) fix wiring (including that `pos`/`reachPos` are captured exactly once, not duplicated, after reordering), TOW-3's deliberate non-change, and the `Drop_Location_Arrival_Time` field-type fix. Full existing `test_field_apps_whatsapp.js` suite (28 checks) re-run — no regressions from touching the same Reach/Cancel/Pickup functions. All 5 touched files syntax-checked via a real `node --check` on their extracted `<script>` blocks (not just regex) and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later) — New: 5 timestamp fields wired (#5, #6, #14, #15, #16 from the same spec), now that the user created them in Zoho

- **`Confirmation_Time`** (`Create_Case`) — "#5 Task Confirmation Time." Two independent `extra()` hooks (`create` and `locations` stages, the two places `Status` can first reach `SCHEDULED`/`READY FOR ASSIGNMENT`) each check `!r.Confirmation_Time` before stamping — mirrors each stage's own `status()` condition independently, since `extra(r)` only ever receives the raw record, not `computeStatus()`'s just-computed result. Whichever path reaches SCHEDULED/READY first wins; never overwritten after.
- **`Invited_At`** (`Invites`) — "#6 RSP Invite Time." Stamped once, at the exact `addRecords()` call that creates each Invites record — a NEW field on the `Invites` form itself (not `Create_Case`).
- **`Remaining_Fee_Receipt_Time`** / **`RSP_Closure_Time`** (`Create_Case`) — "#14"/"#15." Kept as two separate fields (matching the spec's literal numbering, per the open question raised last entry — not merged) even though they share the exact same trigger: the cash "Payment Received" click, in all three vendor-facing apps' `confirmPaymentReceived()` and a new `extra()` hook on `getRescueTicket`'s own agent-run `payment` stage (gated on the same `statusStillEarly(r, ["REMAINING FEE DUE"])` condition that stage's own status-write already uses, so it only fires on the genuine forward transition). `Remaining_Fee_Receipt_Time` only covers the cash branch — the Zoho-Payment-gateway branch remains unbuilt (no real gateway integration exists anywhere in this app).
- **`Agent_Closure_Time`** (`Create_Case`) — "#16 Task Closure Time (Agent)." Added directly to `saveClosure()`'s existing payload, always sent on every save (not stamped-once) — consistent with every other field in that same payload (`Closure_Status`/`Remarks`/etc. are likewise always resent, not guarded).

**Reminder given to the user**: `getRescueTicket` reads `Create_Case` through `Agent_Ticket_Report`, and reads are scoped to that report's own layout (the exact `Added_Time` bug fixed 2026-09-04/05) — so the 4 new `Create_Case` fields also need adding to `Agent_Ticket_Report`'s Configure Fields, and `Invited_At` to `Invites_Report`'s, or they'll write fine but read back `undefined`.

**Tested** — new `test_new_timestamps_2026_09_07.js` (15 checks): all 5 new write sites, both `Confirmation_Time` paths, and confirms none of the surrounding existing logic (`Created_By_Agent_Email`, `Location_Received_Time`, the `payment` stage's own `status()` write, `Payment_Method_Final`, `saveClosure()`'s other fields) was disturbed. Full `test_field_apps_whatsapp.js` (28 checks) and `test_locations_distances_2026_09_07.js` (32 checks) suites re-run — no regressions. All 4 touched files syntax-checked (`node --check`) and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later still) — Diagnostic added: investigating "Operations Manager works in development, not in production"

User reported `IS_OPS_MANAGER` (gates Ops Map + both Vendor Accounting screens) works when testing but not for real agents in production. This is **not a new bug** — it's the exact risk this file's own 2026-09-04 comment already predicted: `agentUserTypeFor(LOGIN_EMAIL)` was originally abandoned on 2026-08-31 because `LOGIN_EMAIL` (`ZOHO.CREATOR.UTIL.getInitParams().loginUser`) was confirmed live to resolve to the app's own account rather than the real logged-in user, under this app's Zoho Creator **Portal login model** — then re-adopted on 2026-09-04 anyway, by explicit user request, with that risk "re-accepted... needs a real live login test to confirm one way or the other." A likely explanation for the dev-vs-production split: testing as the app's own account makes `loginUser` coincidentally resolve correctly (matching your own `Agent_Report` record), while a real Portal User's production login hits the identity-resolution bug for real.

The already-built alternative (`checkOperationsManagerPermission()`, a real native Zoho Portal Permission check unaffected by this bug, left intact-but-unused since 2026-09-04 for exactly this contingency) is the likely fix — but switching to it blindly isn't safe without first confirming the native "Operations Manager" Portal Permission is actually still granted to real agents in production (it may have gone unmaintained since being deprioritized 2026-09-04). User confirmed: not sure, need to check first.

**Built instead (this entry)**: pure diagnostic logging, zero behavior change — confirmed via a new 11-check test that `agentUserTypeFor()`/`isOperationsManager()` return byte-for-byte the same values as before. `agentUserTypeFor()` now logs exactly which email it searched Agent_Report for, whether any record matched at all, and (if so) that record's own Email/User_Type values. `boot()` now also logs a `LOGIN_EMAIL = ... | IS_OPS_MANAGER = ...` summary line right after computing it. Together with the existing `getInitParams() -> ...` log, this gives a complete trail from a REAL production Portal login's own browser console — no more inferring the cause from symptoms alone.

**Next step (not yet done, waiting on this diagnostic's real output)**: have a real Operations Manager open the production widget, open DevTools Console, and send back what these new log lines show. That will confirm definitively whether it's the predicted identity-resolution bug (and point at switching to `checkOperationsManagerPermission()`, once that native permission is confirmed set up) or something else entirely (e.g. an `Agent_Report` email typo, case mismatch, or the permission genuinely not granted).

**Tested** — new `test_ops_manager_diagnostic_2026_09_07.js` (11 checks): 3 scenarios (real match / no match / empty email) confirming `agentUserTypeFor()`/`isOperationsManager()`'s actual return values are completely unchanged, plus confirms the new log lines fire with the right data in each case. Full `test_field_apps_whatsapp.js`/`test_locations_distances_2026_09_07.js`/`test_new_timestamps_2026_09_07.js` suites re-run — no regressions. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later still) — Root cause CONFIRMED live; `IS_OPS_MANAGER` switched to a separate, Portal-readable report

The diagnostic above did its job: comparing a real development console log against a real production one side by side gave a definitive answer, and it wasn't the identity-resolution theory this project had been carrying since 2026-08-31/09-04. `LOGIN_EMAIL` was resolving *correctly* in both environments. The actual cause: production's `GET .../report/Agent_Report...` request returned **403 Forbidden** for a real agent's own Portal login (works fine in development, which uses a shared/admin account with broader access) — confirmed directly by the diagnostic's own `agentDisplayName` log reporting `"OPTIONS.agents loaded: 0 record(s)"`. With zero records loaded, `agentUserTypeFor()` had nothing to search regardless of whose email was passed in — it would have returned false for a genuine Operations Manager too, not just a coincidental mismatch.

(`Technicians_Report` also returned `400 Bad Request` in the same production log, and `Vendors_Report`/`Agent_Ticket_Report` both loaded fine — so this isn't a blanket permissions failure, just these two specific reports for this Portal profile. Flagged to the user as a separate thing worth checking in the same Zoho Sharing-settings pass, not fixed here.)

**Fix, per explicit user instruction**: rather than fixing `Agent_Report`'s own Sharing permissions (a Zoho-side change, not code), `agentUserTypeFor()` now resolves `User_Type` from a **separate report, `My_Availability_Agent`** — mirroring the exact same "own login/toggle profile, Portal-readable, separate from the full master-data report" pattern `My_Availability_Vendor` already uses for vendors (see that report's own history above). New `CONFIG.optionReports.myAvailabilityAgent` entry, new `OPTIONS.myAvailabilityAgent` array populated by `loadOptions()` via the same `pull()` helper every other option list already uses.

**Deliberately NOT changed**: `agentDisplayName()`/`agentDisplayNameFor()`'s own name-lookup still reads `OPTIONS.agents`/`Agent_Report` — this fix is scoped exactly to what was asked (the Operations Manager check), not a wholesale move of every agent-identity lookup. That means a real agent's own display name (used for Remarks attribution and the ticket-locking "who's holding this?" prompt) is likely *still* broken in production until `Agent_Report`'s Sharing permissions are separately fixed — flagged, not silently ignored.

**Not independently confirmed**: whether `My_Availability_Agent`'s own `Email`/`User_Type` field names match this guess exactly (assumed identical to `Agent_Report`'s naming, since that's the most likely convention) — `agentUserTypeFor()`'s own diagnostic log (unchanged from the previous entry, just repointed at the new report) will show immediately on the next real login if either guess is wrong.

**Tested** — extended `test_ops_manager_diagnostic_2026_09_07.js` to 19 checks: the 3 original scenarios re-run against the new `myAvailabilityAgent` mock shape (same pass/fail behavior, proving the actual matching logic is unchanged, only its data source moved), plus 8 new wiring checks confirming the new config/loadOptions/OPTIONS-init entries exist, `agentUserTypeFor()` reads from the new source, and — critically — that `CONFIG.optionReports.agents`/`OPTIONS.agents`/`agentDisplayNameFor()` are all completely untouched. Full `test_field_apps_whatsapp.js` (28)/`test_locations_distances_2026_09_07.js` (32)/`test_new_timestamps_2026_09_07.js` (15) suites re-run — no regressions, 94 checks passing across the whole session's work today. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later still) — `agentUserTypeFor()` changed to a two-tier lookup: `Agent_Report` first, `My_Availability_Agent` only as a fallback

Refinement of the previous entry, per explicit user instruction: *"use My_Availability_Agent when Agent_Report doesn't have permission"* — rather than switching the User_Type source entirely (the previous entry's approach), `agentUserTypeFor()` now tries `Agent_Report` (`OPTIONS.agents`, same source/field names it always used, and the same one `agentDisplayNameFor()` still uses) FIRST, and only falls through to the separate `My_Availability_Agent` report when `Agent_Report` yields no match at all — which is exactly what a 403-in-production produces (an empty `OPTIONS.agents`), but this also transparently covers a genuinely-missing record without needing to tell the two apart. Implemented via a small shared `findIn(list, spec, sourceLabel)` closure inside `agentUserTypeFor()` so both tiers use identical matching/logging logic, not two near-duplicate copies.

**Net effect**: wherever `Agent_Report` is genuinely readable (any environment where that Sharing permission is fine), behavior is 100% identical to before this whole investigation started. Only where `Agent_Report` is blocked (today's known production case) does it transparently fall back — no environment-detection flag, no config toggle, just "try the normal source, fall back if it comes up empty."

**Tested** — rewrote `test_ops_manager_diagnostic_2026_09_07.js` (now 27 checks) around the new two-tier shape: a real `Agent_Report` match (confirms the fallback is never even consulted), an empty-`Agent_Report`-with-fallback-match scenario (the actual production fix, confirms `isOperationsManager()` now returns `true` in that exact case), a `Agent_Report`-has-other-records-but-not-this-one scenario (confirms the fallback isn't gated on emptiness specifically), a no-match-anywhere scenario, and the empty-`LOGIN_EMAIL` early return — plus wiring checks confirming `CONFIG.optionReports.agents`/`agentDisplayNameFor()` remain completely untouched. Found and fixed one test-assertion bug of my own during this pass (a log-count assumption that didn't account for `isOperationsManager()` calling `agentUserTypeFor()` a second time internally) — not a product bug, just a test needing correcting once the real behavior was traced through. Full `test_field_apps_whatsapp.js`/`test_locations_distances_2026_09_07.js`/`test_new_timestamps_2026_09_07.js` suites re-run — no regressions, 102 checks passing across the whole session's work today. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later still) — Real bug fixed: only one of many photos displaying in the live wizard step / click-to-edit field popup

Confirmed via a full audit of every WhatsApp/photo/integration item on a 24-point feature checklist. `closureFieldRowHtml()`'s own file-rendering branch was already fixed for this exact class of bug on 2026-08-19 ("uploaded multiple photos but closure shows only one image") — but `buildField()`'s file branch, used by the live per-step wizard AND the click-to-edit field-edit popup (`#fieldModalBody`), had the identical one-thumbnail-only limitation and was explicitly flagged as "not touched" in that same 2026-08-19 entry. Fixed now: mirrors `closureFieldRowHtml()`'s own proven pattern exactly — renders one `.crow-thumb` placeholder **per file** (via `fileCountFor()`, same helper), each tagged with its own `data-thumb-index`, instead of a single generic placeholder regardless of count. No changes needed to either hydration call site (`hydrateFilePreviewsIn("#fields")`/`("#fieldModalBody")`) — both already handle indexed placeholders generically, they just never received more than one to hydrate before. Also improved the "Already uploaded" hint text to name the real count (e.g. "4 photos already uploaded") when there's more than one.

**Tested** — new `test_multiphoto_display_2026_09_07.js` (15 checks), a REAL DOM-execution test (not just regex matching against source) using a minimal `document`/`state` stub to actually run `buildField()` and inspect its output HTML: confirms a 4-photo field renders exactly 4 indexed placeholders (was 1 before the fix), a single-photo field still renders exactly 1 (no behavior change for the common case), a not-yet-uploaded field renders zero and falls back to its own hint, and a freshly-picked local file (this session, no server URL yet) also correctly renders zero thumbnails. Plus wiring checks confirming `closureFieldRowHtml()`'s own already-fixed branch and both `hydrateFilePreviewsIn()` call sites are completely untouched. Full existing suite (102 checks) re-run — no regressions. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later still) — Real bug fixed: photo capture appeared frozen, inviting repeated taps (across vendorTicket/technicianTicket/driverTicket)

Same 24-point checklist audit, item "photo clicks are taking too much time / why two camera buttons." The two buttons (📷 camera, 🖼️ gallery) are confirmed intentional, separate features from a 2026-08-03 user request — not a duplicate, not touched. The real cause: `capturePhoto()`'s own `getPositionSafe()` call can take up to 8 seconds to resolve (real device GPS acquisition), and nothing disabled the shutter button or showed any progress during that wait — the camera modal just sat there looking unresponsive, inviting repeated taps. Each extra tap started a completely new, concurrent capture (a fresh GPS request plus a duplicate photo pushed onto `state.photos` and a duplicate upload) since nothing guarded against overlapping calls.

**Fixed**, identically in all three field apps (byte-identical source, byte-identical fix): a simple `capturingPhoto` in-flight flag now makes an extra tap during an active capture a no-op instead of starting a second one, and the shutter button is disabled + visibly dimmed (`opacity:0.4`) for the duration, reset in a `finally` block so it always re-enables even if something throws. The actual capture/watermark/upload logic itself is completely unchanged — only wrapped.

**Not touched, flagged as separate follow-up work**: no image resizing/downscaling exists on either path (camera captures at native resolution with only JPEG quality compression; gallery uploads go out completely raw/uncompressed) — this contributes to real upload latency too but is a larger, separate change from today's debounce fix.

**Tested** — new `test_photo_capture_debounce_2026_09_07.js` (36 checks, 12 per widget × 3 widgets), a REAL execution test with a controllable-delay GPS mock: fires `capturePhoto()` twice before the first GPS request resolves, confirms the second call is a genuine no-op (no second GPS request, no duplicate photo, `uploadPhotoNow()`/`closeCamera()` each called exactly once), confirms the shutter visibly disables/dims during capture and resets after, and confirms a later, genuinely separate capture still works normally. Full existing suite re-run — no regressions, 153 checks passing across the whole session's work today. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-07 (later still) — Real bug fixed: Booking Fee invoice was for the FULL Total Service Fee, not the Booking Fee the customer was actually asked to pay

Found while writing out the complete payment/invoicing flow end to end for the user, then confirmed by reading `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` directly: the customer's own payment page showed `amountDue = Booking_Fee` (correct), but its "Pay Now" button linked to the SAME invoice `onDemandInvoicing()` creates for the ticket's full `Total_Service_Fee` — a customer who only owed a ₹200 booking fee would see a bill for the whole (e.g. ₹2,500) amount if they opened that link. User decision: **split into two invoices** — one for the Booking Fee at Quote stage, a second, separate one for the Remaining Fee when that's collected (previously, the Remaining Fee flow created no invoice at all).

**`onDemandInvoicing(record, amountOverride, invoiceIdField, referenceSuffix)`** — 3 new optional params, all falling back to the exact original behavior when omitted (any future/other caller unaffected): `amountOverride` lets a caller invoice an exact partial amount instead of re-deriving `Total_Service_Fee`/`Rescue_Charge`; `invoiceIdField` lets the resulting invoice ID land on its own dedicated ticket field so two invoices for the same ticket don't clobber each other's ID; `referenceSuffix` appends to the Books reference number (`-BF`/`-RF`) so the two invoices are distinguishable in Zoho Books itself.

- **`Send_Payment_Link`** (Quote/Booking Fee stage): now passes `Booking_Fee` as the override amount, still writes to the original `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` field (unchanged — the customer page's own `booking_payment` mode needed no change), suffix `-BF`.
- **`Send_Payment_Link1`** (Payment/Remaining Fee stage): previously created no invoice at all — now mirrors `Send_Payment_Link`'s own structure exactly, invoicing `Remaining_Fee`, writing to a **new field, `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID_Remaining`** (needs creating in Zoho — Single Line Text on `Create_Case`, same type as `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` — degrades safely/logged-only until then, same as every other not-yet-created field in this app), suffix `-RF`. This button now `await`s the invoice creation before sending its WhatsApp message (previously fired immediately) — same async pattern the booking-fee flow already used, and its `customerLink` now falls back to the raw `invoiceUrl` the same way `Send_Payment_Link`'s already did.
- **`getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge`** (customerPage's own Custom API): `remaining_payment` mode now also returns `invoiceUrl` (from the new field), mirroring `booking_payment`'s own pattern exactly — `renderBookingOrRemainingPayment()` on the widget side is shared by both modes already, so this needed zero widget-side changes to start showing a real "Pay Now" button for the remaining fee too.

**Needs from you before this is fully live**: create `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID_Remaining` (Single Line Text) on `Create_Case`, and redeploy the updated `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` content into its Custom API in Zoho Creator (same manual copy-paste deployment every other Deluge change in this project has needed).

**Tested** — new `test_split_invoice_2026_09_07.js` (later extended to 26 checks — see this changelog's own next entry for a real Deluge type bug found and fixed right after this shipped): a real execution test of `onDemandInvoicing()` confirming the Booking Fee call invoices exactly `Booking_Fee` (not the full fee) to the original field with a `-BF` reference, the Remaining Fee call invoices exactly `Remaining_Fee` to the NEW field with a `-RF` reference, a no-override call falls back to the exact original full-amount behavior (backward compatibility), and a zero/blank override doesn't silently try to invoice ₹0. Plus wiring checks confirming both button actions and the Deluge script's own `remaining_payment` branch. Full existing suite (153 checks) re-run — no regressions. Syntax-checked and packed (`getRescueTicket.zip`, `customerPage.zip`).

### 2026-09-07 (even later) — Real Deluge bug fixed: `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` type error, "left expression is of type DECIMAL and right expression is of type TEXT and the operator != is not valid"

User hit this immediately after deploying the split-invoice fix above. Root cause: `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` reads back as **DECIMAL** in Deluge, not the Single Line Text this project's docs assumed — `invoiceId != ""` (comparing DECIMAL directly to a TEXT literal) is exactly what Deluge's type checker rejects. The SAME anti-pattern (`if(X != null && X != "")`) turned out to be pre-existing in three other spots in this file too — `Cx_Feedback_Score`, and (genuinely broken, since these fields really are Decimal) both checks in `location` mode (`Latitude`/`DropLocationLat`). Full fix + details live in `customerPage/README.md`'s own matching entry, since the actual file touched is `customerPage/custom-apis/getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` — this entry exists here just to keep the invoicing changelog thread complete.

**Tested** — `test_split_invoice_2026_09_07.js` extended to 26 checks total. No regressions in the rest of the suite.

### 2026-09-07 (yet later) — Real Deluge compile error fixed: "Missing return statement: Provide STRING expression to return"

Same file (`customerPage/custom-apis/getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge`), hit immediately after the type-error fix above. The function's shared fall-through `return` after its whole mode-dispatch chain became unprovable to Deluge's own static checker once the type fix added nested `if` blocks — fixed by giving every branch its own explicit return instead. Full details in `customerPage/README.md`'s own matching entry; noted here only to keep this changelog thread complete. `test_split_invoice_2026_09_07.js` now at 29 checks, no regressions.

### 2026-09-08 — Google Distance Matrix API scope-restricted: only this app's Final Closure calculation may call it now

User request: *"use Google api's only for final distance calculations.. round trip, vendor travel distances and vendor round trip."* Mapped every Google Distance Matrix call site across `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket`/`getRescueTicket` (via 4 parallel Explore agents) before touching anything — one function in this app, `runClosureDistanceCalculations()` (called once from `saveClosure()`, i.e. Final Closure), computes exactly the three fields the request named almost verbatim: `Roundtrip_Distance` ("round trip"), `Vendor_Distance` ("vendor travel distances"), `Vendor_Round_Trip_Distance` ("vendor round trip") — and only ever runs once per ticket ("final distance calculations"). Confirmed via AskUserQuestion: keep Google's API only there; revert every other call site to Haversine-only.

**This app's own two other call sites, fixed**:
- **`refreshVendorDistances()`** (the vendor-assignment picker's distance-based sort/display, fired as an agent browses vendor options) — no longer calls `googleDistanceMatrix()` at all; the `const rows=...` line is now a plain `const rows=null;`, so it falls straight to its existing Haversine straight-line estimate (the exact same fallback path it already took whenever Google's call failed or returned nothing usable — nothing downstream needed to change).
- **`calcAcceptanceDistanceToBreakdown()`** (the Accept-step `Distance_To_Breakdown`/`ETA` auto-fill, used only in "Agent Entering on Behalf of Vendor" mode) — same fix. Its already-existing Haversine distance-only fallback still fires; `ETA` simply stays unfilled, exactly as it already did whenever Google's call failed to return a duration (not a new failure mode).

**Deliberately left untouched**: `loadGoogleMaps()`/`googleDistanceMatrix()` themselves (unlike the other 3 widgets, which short-circuit `loadGoogleMaps()` entirely) — `legDistanceKm()` (the Final Closure calc's own helper) still needs them, and so does the unrelated Ops Manager Map View (`loadOpsMap()`/`renderOpsMapMarkers()`, confirmed via the same investigation to never call the Distance Matrix API at all — it only renders map pins, sharing just the script-loader with the billed endpoint, not the endpoint itself — so it's unaffected either way).

**Needs redeploying**: none — client-side JS only.

**Tested** — new `test_google_api_scope_2026_09_08.js` (29 checks, shared across all 4 widgets): confirms `refreshVendorDistances()`/`calcAcceptanceDistanceToBreakdown()` no longer call `googleDistanceMatrix()`, confirms `legDistanceKm()`/`runClosureDistanceCalculations()` are untouched and still write all 3 named fields, confirms `loadGoogleMaps()` itself is NOT short-circuited in this file, and confirms the other 3 widgets' short-circuit + Haversine fallback all work. Full existing suite re-run — the only failures found were confirmed pre-existing and unrelated (stale `window.addEventListener` mock gaps, an unrelated `customerPage` preview-badge count, `test_save_acceptance_flow.js`/`test_vendor_identity_diagnostic.js`/`test_wip_readonly_gate.js` failures on RSP_Start_Latitude/vendor-identity-diagnostic/Odometer_Reading — none reference distance or Google API code). Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-08 (later same day) — Ops Manager Map View rewritten off Google Maps entirely

Follow-up client instruction, more explicit than the distance-calc scoping above: *"for the Maps view also, we should not use paid Google APIs unless absolutely required."* Confirmed via AskUserQuestion (option: "Replace with free Leaflet + OpenStreetMap") that map rendering is not absolutely required to stay on Google — a free equivalent covers everything this feature needs (colored pins, click-to-info, pan, zoom, auto-fit-to-markers).

**What actually shipped, and why it differs slightly from "Leaflet"**: rather than embed the real Leaflet.js library, this is a **minimal, purpose-built, dependency-free tile renderer** using the same free data source (OpenStreetMap raster tiles, `https://{a,b,c}.tile.openstreetmap.org` — no API key, no billing). Two reasons for building it directly instead of embedding Leaflet verbatim: (1) reliably embedding a ~40KB third-party library byte-for-byte wasn't something this environment could guarantee without real risk of silently corrupting the source: (2) every other widget in this project is 100% vanilla JS with zero external dependencies — this keeps that pattern rather than introducing the codebase's first one. Functionally it's the same category of solution the approved option described (free, OSM-tile-based, no Google) — flagging the implementation-detail difference explicitly rather than letting "Leaflet" quietly mean something else.

**New code** (`opsMapPinColor()` kept as-is): `smLonToX`/`smLatToY`/`smXToLon`/`smYToLat` (standard Web Mercator projection — the same math every slippy-map tile library uses), `smFitBounds()` (picks the tightest zoom that still fits every marker, mirroring the original `google.maps.LatLngBounds`+`fitBounds()` behavior), `smMount()` (builds the tile/marker/popup/zoom-button/attribution skeleton once per container, wires drag-to-pan and the two zoom buttons — guarded so re-entering the Fleet Map view never stacks duplicate window-level drag listeners), `smRedraw()` (rebuilds the visible tile grid + marker pins for the current center/zoom), `smSetView()`/`smSetMarkers()`. `loadOpsMap()`/`renderOpsMapMarkers()` rewritten to use these instead of `google.maps.Map`/`Marker`/`InfoWindow`/`LatLngBounds`/`SymbolPath` — all their own business logic (the Engagement/Service Type filters, `activeAssignmentIds()`, the red/green/grey color rule) is completely unchanged, only the rendering layer changed. `opsMapInstance`/`opsMapMarkers` globals removed (fully replaced by `SIMPLE_MAP`'s state object) — confirmed nothing else in the file referenced them.

**OSM tile usage note, flagged not silently assumed**: `tile.openstreetmap.org`'s usage policy expects reasonable request volume and requires visible attribution — both are satisfied here (a handful of concurrent Ops Managers making standard `<img>` tile requests, plus a fixed "© OpenStreetMap contributors" credit in the corner, required by OSM's own data license). This has **not been confirmed to actually load tiles live inside the Zoho Creator sandbox yet** — `plugin-manifest.json` now whitelists the three tile subdomains under both `connect-src` and a new `img-src` key (added defensively since it's genuinely unconfirmed whether Zoho's manifest schema enforces/needs `img-src` for plain `<img>` tags at all) — if tiles don't appear, that's the first thing to check, and report back what actually renders live.

**Deliberately unaffected**: `loadGoogleMaps()`/`googleDistanceMatrix()` themselves — still real Google, still only reachable from `legDistanceKm()`/`runClosureDistanceCalculations()` (the Final Closure calc). `maps.googleapis.com` stays in `plugin-manifest.json`'s `connect-src`/`script-src` for exactly that reason.

**Needs redeploying**: none — client-side JS + manifest only.

**Tested** — new `test_ops_map_free_renderer_2026_09_08.js` (45 checks): projection math round-trips correctly, `smFitBounds()` picks sane zoom/center for single/empty/spread/tiny/huge point sets, `smMount()` builds the expected skeleton and wires zoom buttons + click delegation + a one-time drag-listener guard (verified by mounting the same container twice), `smRedraw()` produces correct tile URLs (right z/x/y, right subdomain rotation) and correct marker pins (right color/position/count), `smSetMarkers()` mirrors the original `if(plotted)` guard (an empty filtered-out marker list leaves the current view untouched), marker click shows/hides the right popup content, zoom buttons clamp at [3,18], and drag-to-pan moves the view the right direction with a live CSS-transform preview during the drag and a real re-render only on release (a plain click with zero movement correctly does nothing). Also updated `test_ops_map_service_filter.js` — its own Service Type filter checks now hook the new `smSetMarkers()` call instead of the no-longer-created `google.maps.Marker`, confirming the filter LOGIC itself (untouched by this rewrite) still works correctly. Full existing suite re-run — no new regressions beyond the same pre-existing, unrelated set noted above. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-08 (later still) — Booking Fee invoice now created WITHOUT tax; Remaining Fee invoice keeps tax as before

User-provided evidence: a live test invoice created directly in Zoho Books, pasted verbatim, showing exactly what a no-tax line item looks like (`"tax_id": ""`, `"tax_percentage": 0`, `"tax_exemption_id": "3988332000000091002"`, `"tax_exemption_code": "TAX"`, `rate`/`sub_total`/`total` all equal — no GST backed out of the rate). Instruction: the Booking Fee invoice should be created with no tax at all; the Remaining Fee invoice (created after remaining-fee collection) should keep the existing tax-inclusive behavior, unchanged.

**Root cause of why this needed a real code change, not just a config tweak**: `onDemandInvoicing()` (both the widget JS wrapper and its `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` Custom API) is shared by both the Booking Fee and Remaining Fee calls, and unconditionally applied the real GST18 Tax Group to every line item it created — there was no way to ask for a tax-exempt invoice at all before this.

**Fix**: added a 5th widget-JS param / 12th Deluge param, `applyTax`, defaulting to `true` everywhere it isn't explicitly passed (so this is fully backward compatible — nothing changes unless a call site opts in). `Send_Payment_Link` (Booking Fee) now passes `applyTax=false`; `Send_Payment_Link1` (Remaining Fee) passes `applyTax=true` explicitly (unchanged behavior, just spelled out for clarity next to the new false case). On the Deluge side, `applyTax="false"` makes the line item carry `tax_exemption_id` (the real id confirmed via the user's live test) instead of `tax_id` — **a genuine pitfall this avoids**: Zoho Books requires a line item to carry either a real tax or an explicit exemption reason; simply omitting `tax_id` isn't enough on its own for a GST-registered org.

**A second, less obvious fix bundled in**: when `applyTax=false`, the widget JS now sends the line item's `rate` as the **full amount itself**, not `computeGstBreakup()`'s reverse-calculated (GST-backed-out) service charge. A tax-exempt line item adds nothing on top of its own rate — sending the backed-out figure (as the shared tax-inclusive code path always did) would have silently under-invoiced the customer by the ~15% that calculation assumes is embedded GST. `cgst`/`sgst`/`roundOff` are all `0` in this case; `computeGstBreakup()` itself is completely untouched (still used exactly as before for the tax-inclusive path, and for the unrelated `maybeCreateOnDemandInvoice()` Creator-report bookkeeping record, which this change doesn't touch at all).

**Needs redeploying**: `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` — paste into its Custom API in Zoho Creator.

**Tested** — new `test_invoice_tax_config_2026_09_08.js` (22 checks): confirms `applyTax=false` sends the full amount as `serviceCharges` with `cgst`/`sgst`/`roundOff` all zero, confirms `applyTax=true` still reverse-calculates the GST breakup exactly as before, confirms omitting `applyTax` entirely defaults to the original tax-inclusive behavior (backward compatible), confirms both real call sites pass the right value, and confirms the Deluge file's exemption-vs-tax branching and the real exemption/tax-group ids. Existing `test_on_demand_invoicing.js`/`test_on_demand_invoicing_whatsapp.js` re-run — no regressions. Syntax-checked and packed.

### 2026-09-08 (later still) — `onDemandInvoicing.deluge`: removed all `info` log statements

User request: *"remove logs in this function."* Removed all 6 `info` calls (the routine "called with recordId=..." dump, the "jsonPayload sent to Zoho Books" dump, and 4 failure-path logs inside error branches and the two catch blocks). Purely cosmetic — Deluge's `info` only writes to Zoho's own execution log viewer, nothing else reads it; every failure path still returns its own real, structured `{"code":"ERROR","message":...}` response exactly as before (unchanged), which the widget JS caller already surfaces via `console.warn` on the client side — no debugging signal was actually lost, just the routine per-call noise in Zoho's execution log. The email-send catch block, which had only an `info` line inside it, now has an empty body with a comment explaining why it's intentionally swallowed (email failure must not block the already-created invoice) — everything else in the function (the tax branching from the entry above, the customer lookup/creation, the invoice creation call itself) is untouched.

**Needs redeploying**: `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` — paste into its Custom API in Zoho Creator (same file as the tax fix above — if you haven't redeployed since that entry, this is bundled into the same paste).

**Tested** — extended `test_invoice_tax_config_2026_09_08.js` to 22 checks: confirms no `info` statement remains anywhere in the file, and confirms every error path still returns its own real message (at least 8 distinct `{"code":"ERROR",...}` returns found, matching the original count) — so nothing was silently lost. All prior checks in the same suite still pass unchanged, confirming the tax logic itself wasn't touched by this cleanup.

### 2026-09-08 (later yet) — Real live bug: Booking Fee invoice still showed tax after redeploying; added a belt-and-braces fallback

User reported live, with a real invoice screenshot (`RSID508006-BF`, Rate `200.00`, CGST `18.00`, SGST `18.00`, Total `236.00`) that tax was still being applied to a Booking Fee invoice. The numbers backed out exactly to the *original* `computeGstBreakup(236)` formula, confirming the old unconditional-tax code path ran, not a new bug in the tax-branching logic itself — but the user reported this persisted even after redeploying.

**Most likely root cause, not fully verifiable from here**: Zoho Creator Custom APIs typically require each parameter to be explicitly (re-)registered in the Custom API's own setup screen in Zoho Creator, separate from just being named in the Deluge function's signature. `applyTax` is a brand-new parameter added this same day — if it wasn't (re-)registered there, it silently arrives as `null` inside the function regardless of what the widget sends, defaulting straight back to `taxExempt=false` (tax applied) — exactly the reported symptom, even with the correct script body live.

**Fix, without depending on that Zoho-side registration step at all**: added a second, independent trigger for `taxExempt` — `referenceNumberResolved`'s own `"-BF"` suffix (`referenceNumberResolved.toString().contains("-BF")`). This suffix is added *only* by `Send_Payment_Link` (the Booking Fee call, see `widget.html`'s own comment there) via a **pre-existing, already-provably-working parameter** — the user's own screenshot shows it landing correctly as `RSID508006-BF` on the invoice's P.O.# field. Either signal (`applyTax="false"` OR the `-BF` suffix) now independently triggers the exemption — so this works correctly whether or not `applyTax` itself is ever successfully registered/bound on Zoho's side. `-RF` (Remaining Fee's own suffix) shares no substring with `-BF`, so the two invoice types can't cross-trigger each other.

**Needs redeploying**: `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` again — same file, this fallback is bundled into it.

**Tested** — extended `test_invoice_tax_config_2026_09_08.js` to 26 checks: confirms the `-BF` suffix check exists and runs after `referenceNumberResolved` is actually resolved (not before), confirms both signals are independent OR conditions (not mutually exclusive), and confirms `-RF` can never accidentally match the `-BF` substring check.

## 2026-09-08 — Agent notified when a customer shares their location via the Customer Page

User-requested flow integration, point 4: *"notify the agent so they can open the ticket and continue with the existing ticket workflow"* — after the customer submits a breakdown or drop location on the separate `customerPage` widget (see that widget's own matching entry for its TOW auto-chain-to-drop-location piece, point 2/point in the same request). Points 1 and 2 of that request (send the WhatsApp message with the Customer Page link; walk the customer through booking payment → location) were **already built** in earlier sessions — `Send_Payment_Link` already builds `customerLink = buildCustomerPageUrl(r, "booking_payment") || invoiceUrl` and sends it via the `booking_fees_link` WhatsApp template; `customerPage`'s own `advanceAfterPayment()` already auto-advances Booking Fee success straight into the Location step. The one blocker for point 1 actually taking effect live: `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL` (line ~3956) is still blank — **needs a real published Customer Page URL entered before the WhatsApp link stops falling back to the raw Books invoice URL.** Not something I can fill in without that real URL.

**What's new here (point 4)**: no push/webhook mechanism exists anywhere in this project — `customerPage` writes location straight into the same `Create_Case` record with no direct link back to this app at all. Rather than build a new channel, this rides the **same 30s dashboard poll** that already detects and announces mechanic Status changes (`pollDashboardForMechanicUpdates()`/`notifyMechanicUpdates()`/`playNotifSound()`/the `#mechNotifStack` card UI, from the 2026-08-07 "real-time-ish" notification feature). Added a parallel snapshot (`DASHBOARD_LOCATION_SNAPSHOT`, alongside the existing `DASHBOARD_STATUS_SNAPSHOT`) tracking whether each ticket's `Latitude`/`Longitude` and `DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` pairs are populated; the same poll cycle now also detects either pair transitioning from blank to populated (a customer isn't editing these day-to-day — they're `readonly` fields on the agent side) and surfaces it via a new `showLocationSharedNotification()` card ("📍 Customer shared their breakdown/drop-off location") using the exact same sound + stacking-card + click-to-open UX as a mechanic Status change. The existing Status-change detection itself is completely untouched — this is a parallel, independent check added to the same loop, not a modification of it.

**Point 3** ("automatically update the ticket information in the Agent Portal") is inherently satisfied by the existing architecture — `customerPage` writes directly into the same `Create_Case` record the Agent Portal reads, so there's no separate "sync" step needed; the dashboard re-renders on the very next poll (now also triggered by a location-only change, not just a Status change or new ticket, so this stays current even when nothing else about the ticket changed).

**Needs redeploying**: none — client-side JS only.

**Tested** — new `test_agent_location_notification_2026_09_08.js` (23 checks): `hasCoordPair()`/`updateLocationSnapshot()` correctness, the new notification card's content (case label, customer name, breakdown-vs-drop-off wording), the same 5-card cap + overflow-toast behavior as the mechanic-update notification, and wiring checks confirming the poll loop's Status-change detection is byte-for-byte unchanged while the new location detection runs alongside it. Also fixed `test_dashboard_auto_refresh.js` (pre-existing suite covering the Status/new-ticket poll logic) — it didn't inject the new dependencies this function now references, so its own try/catch was silently swallowing a ReferenceError before ever reaching the assertions being tested; added no-op stubs for the new location-specific pieces (out of that test's own scope) — 11 checks, no regressions. Full existing suite re-run — no new regressions beyond the same pre-existing, unrelated set already tracked earlier today.

## 2026-09-08 (RESOLVED) — root cause of the entire "Customer Page link doesn't show up live" saga found: Zoho Creator Portal's own "Relaunch to update" step

After the `[DEPLOY-CHECK]` button-label test and the hardcoded-payload test both still showed stale behavior on `getrescued.zohocreatorportal.in` despite the user confirming they upload+publish the widget AND publish the app itself, a screenshot of that Portal showed a **"Relaunch to update"** button sitting in the top-right corner, unclicked. The Portal caches the loaded app per browser session and only picks up a newly published version once that button is clicked — every single "still broken after redeploying" report earlier today was this, not a code or packaging problem. Confirmed live: clicking it made the button show `[DEPLOY-CHECK]` immediately, and the WhatsApp message correctly carried `"...CustomerPage : https://creatorapp.zohopublic.in/.../Customer_Page/..."` — the exact payload shape requested two entries above.

Follow-up live report right after: "showing customer page but actual ticket data is not load" — expected, since the delivery-test link (see entry above) intentionally had no `?recordId=&mode=` query string. Now that the deployment mystery is resolved, reverted to the real logic:
- `customerPageUrl`/`customerLink` back to `buildCustomerPageUrl(r,"booking_payment") || invoiceUrl` (no longer hardcoded) — restores the `?recordId=&mode=booking_payment` query string so the Customer Page actually loads the right ticket.
- Removed the `[DEPLOY-CHECK]` button-label suffix (back to plain "Send Payment Link & Request Location") and the temporary `alert()` debug popup — both did their job.
- **Kept** (now confirmed working, not reverted): the `"CustomerPage :"` label wording and the removal of the `param3` payload key — both were part of the user's own explicit final payload spec and delivered successfully.

**Needs redeploying**: the widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked. **Also needs, every time going forward**: after publishing, click "Relaunch to update" in the Portal before testing — this is now a documented, permanent step in this project's deploy process, not just a one-off fix.

**Tested** — syntax-checked; confirmed working via the user's own live test (correct link + correct label arrived in the real WhatsApp message). The recordId/mode query string restoration itself (fixing "ticket data not load") has not yet had its own live confirmation — expected to work since this is exactly the same `buildCustomerPageUrl()` call already proven correct earlier in this project, but flagging since it hasn't been re-tested after this specific revert.

## 2026-09-09 — real root cause found: `?recordId=&mode=` on the Customer Page link never actually reached the widget

Follow-up live tests (after the revert above) kept showing the link correctly built with `?recordId=&mode=` (confirmed via the kept diagnostic `alert()`/console.log on the sending side) but the Customer Page still landing on the "DESIGN PREVIEW — sample data" fallback, meaning `RECORD_ID`/`MODE` were coming back empty on the *receiving* side (`customerPage`'s own `boot()`). User asked directly: investigate why `recordId`/`mode` (called "paymentMode" in the request — same thing, no separate field exists) aren't being attached/read correctly, end to end.

Investigated properly instead of guessing again:
- Fetched the actual Zoho Creator widget SDK (`widgetsdk-min.js`) and read its real `getQueryParams()` implementation — it does **not** parse the widget's own URL at all. It sends a `GET_QUERY_PARAMS` message to the **parent Zoho Creator page** and returns whatever that parent computes — logic this project has zero visibility into (a separate, much larger Zoho-internal bundle).
- Cross-referenced Zoho's own documentation: real page parameters are documented as going **after the page's own `#hash`** (`.../#<pageLinkName>?param=value`), not as a plain `?query` on the raw path.
- Found a direct, load-bearing contradiction in this project's own history: `buildCustomerPageUrl()`'s header comment claimed the plain-path-no-hash format was "confirmed live 2026-09-02," while `customerPage/app/widget.html`'s own header comment (written separately) already documented the opposite (hash required) — and this session's own repeated, reproduced live tests back the hash requirement, not the old comment.

**Fix — since which exact form a page-perma link needs isn't 100% certain either way, send it both ways at once** rather than gamble on a third guess-and-test round trip:
- `buildCustomerPageUrl()` now appends `recordId`/`mode` (and any `extraParams`) as a plain `?query` string **and** repeats the same params after `#Customer_Page?...` — e.g. `.../Customer_Page/<token>?recordId=X&mode=Y#Customer_Page?recordId=X&mode=Y`. Harmless if one form is simply ignored by Zoho's parent-page logic; costs nothing if only one turns out to matter.
- `customerPage`'s own `getPageParams()` (see its own README entry) now also checks `window.location.hash` directly as a fallback if the SDK call comes back without a `recordId`, in case the SDK's own parent-page resolution doesn't pick up the hash form either.

**Needs redeploying**: the widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked. `customerPage` needs redeploying too (see its own README).

**Tested** — syntax-checked. This is a live-debugging fix based on real SDK source and Zoho documentation, not a guess, but has not yet had its own live confirmation — next test should show `RECORD_ID`/`MODE` populated (no more design-preview fallback, no more debug alert firing) if either format is now being picked up correctly.

## 2026-09-09 (later) — `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL` updated a second time: republishing the Customer Page rotated its permalink again

**Important**: this rotation only affects *new* links — any WhatsApp message already sent with the old token is now permanently dead (will 404 or land on an expired page), not just outdated. There's no way to "fix" an already-sent message; the only path is sending a fresh one after this update goes live.

**Needs redeploying**: the widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked.

The hash form was always a hedge for a still-unconfirmed question (does the plain query string alone reach `customerPage`'s `getPageParams()`); dropped now that `customerPage`'s other real blocker (PublicKey auth on its 3 Custom APIs) is fixed. `customerPage`'s own `getPageParams()` keeps its independent hash-fallback regardless, so nothing there needs to change if the hash form is ever needed again.

Not yet independently confirmed live that the plain query string alone is sufficient — this was an explicit user instruction to simplify, not a change made after seeing confirmed-working evidence; the next live test (open a fresh link, check for real ticket data vs. the sample-data fallback) is what actually settles it.

## 2026-09-09 (real fix confirmed, plus a robustness addition) — recordId/mode fix confirmed live via Zoho Page Variables; booking fee amount now also sent directly in the URL

**Confirmed live, finally**: the actual root cause of the whole recordId/mode saga was that `Customer_Page` had zero Page Variables declared in Zoho Creator's own Page Builder — `ZOHO.CREATOR.UTIL.getQueryParams()` only ever exposes query params that correspond to a declared Page Variable, which is why it kept returning `{}` no matter what this project's own code did. User declared `recordId` (as `Text`, not `Number` — flagged and fixed to avoid precision loss on 18-digit Zoho record IDs), `mode`, `locationType`, and `feedback` as Page Variables, and the very next live test showed the full flow working — no more design-preview fallback, no more empty params. This is NOT a code fix in this repo at all; it's Zoho Creator page configuration.

**Separately, user request**: "can we do that when we are sending the customer page also attach in the url bookingfee amount" — since `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` had just shown one real bug already (₹0.00 instead of a real fee, see `customerPage/README.md`), sending the amount directly avoids depending on that Deluge read being correct at all. `buildCustomerPageUrl()`'s existing `extraParams` mechanism (already there, just never used until now) now carries `amount` — `Send_Payment_Link` passes `r.Booking_Fee`, `Send_Payment_Link1` passes `r.Remaining_Fee`, both the exact same values already used elsewhere in the same action for the invoice/WhatsApp text. `customerPage` prefers this URL value over its own Deluge-derived one (see that project's own README) whenever present and the ticket isn't already paid.

**Needs redeploying**: the widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked.

**Tested** — syntax-checked; `buildCustomerPageUrl()`'s `extraParams` mechanism confirmed to correctly append `&amount=...` (pre-existing code path, not new). Not yet independently live-tested with this specific new param.

## 2026-09-09 (later) — param renamed `amount` → `bookingamount`, matching the real Zoho Page Variable name

User declared a Page Variable in Zoho Creator named `bookingamount` (Text), not `amount`. Given what was just confirmed about Page Variables (`getQueryParams()` only exposes a query param that matches a declared variable name exactly), the mismatch would have silently failed the exact same way the whole earlier recordId/mode saga did. Both `Send_Payment_Link` and `Send_Payment_Link1` now send `{ bookingamount: ... }` instead of `{ amount: ... }` — `Send_Payment_Link1` reuses the same single declared variable for the Remaining Fee too (rather than asking for a second Zoho-side variable), since `mode` already disambiguates which fee it represents.

## 2026-09-09 (final) — explicit field persist on both Send Payment Link buttons

User request: "whenever click on the send payment link & request location save the booking_fee and other details too so that we can fetch easily in getcustomerticketinfo function." Real gap identified: both `Send_Payment_Link` and `Send_Payment_Link1` are buttons living directly ON the Quote step itself — if the agent just typed a new fee value and clicks one of these WITHOUT a separate "Save & Continue" first, `r` (in-memory JS state, used for the WhatsApp text and the `bookingamount` URL param) already reflects the new value, but the actual Zoho record could still hold the OLDER one — exactly the kind of gap that could explain `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` reading a stale/blank fee even when the on-screen value looks correct.

**Fixed**: both actions now start with an explicit, best-effort `updateTicket(r.ID, {...})` persisting `Customer_Name`, `Total_Service_Fee`, `Booking_Fee`, `Remaining_Fee` — the same fields `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` actually reads and returns to the Customer Page. Fire-and-forget: doesn't block the invoice/WhatsApp send below (which already reads from `r` in memory either way), a failure here is logged but not fatal.

**Needs redeploying**: the widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked.

**Tested** — syntax-checked. Not yet independently live-tested.

## 2026-09-09 (later) — real bug fixed: fee/detail persist could silently fail the whole update, plus "save first, then send" ordering

User-reported live: "still values not save when click on the send payment link value is not save as i expected." The Client_Charge/Rescue_Charge fields (added to the persist payload earlier the same day, alongside the pre-existing Customer_Name/Total_Service_Fee/Booking_Fee/Remaining_Fee) were sent as an explicit `null` whenever blank. This app's own established, tested convention (`buildPayload()`, the normal Save & Continue payload builder) is the opposite: a blank field is OMITTED from the update payload — explicit `null` is only ever sent for the 4 coordinate fields, a specific case already proven to work. An untested explicit `null` on a Number/Decimal field can make Zoho's update API reject the WHOLE payload, not just that one field — silently failing everything else in the same call.

**Fixed**: `Client_Charge`/`Rescue_Charge` are now omitted from the payload when blank instead of sent as `null`, in both `Send_Payment_Link` and `Send_Payment_Link1`. A save failure now also shows a visible toast ("Couldn't save the fee details before sending...") instead of only a console warning, so this can't go unnoticed again.

Separately, the same two buttons' field-persist call was also changed (same day, earlier) from fire-and-forget to genuinely blocking: the whole invoice/WhatsApp flow is now chained inside the persist's own `.then()`, so it runs only after the save attempt completes — was previously racing it, meaning a slow save could still lose the race against a customer's own link being opened.

**Needs redeploying**: this widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked.

**Tested**: syntax-checked (script blocks parse cleanly). Not yet independently live-tested — next click of Send Payment Link should confirm both the save and the new toast-on-failure behavior.

## 2026-09-09 (yet later) — real payment page instead of a hand-built invoice-view URL guess

User request: "After the customer clicks Pay Now, open the Zoho Books payment page directly instead of opening the invoice separately. The customer should be able to review the invoice, make the payment, and approve/complete the payment process so that the next step can proceed automatically."

Root cause: the `invoiceUrl` sent to customers (both in the WhatsApp message and on the Customer Page's own "Pay Now" button) was always a HAND-BUILT guess — `https://books.zoho.in/invoices/{id}?organization_id={id}` — assembled from just the invoice ID, never a URL Zoho Books' own API actually returned. That's very likely why it opened an invoice view rather than a real payable page. The "next step proceeds automatically" half of the request was already implemented (`customerPage`'s own `pollForPaymentAndAdvance()`, 2026-09-07) — nothing needed there.

**Fixed**: `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` now also calls Zoho Books' real (but genuinely under-documented — no confirmed field name found in Zoho's own public docs after checking multiple sources) "Generate payment link" endpoint (`GET /share/paymentlink`) right after creating the invoice, tries several plausible response field names, and logs the raw response either way. On success, this real `payment_url` is preferred as the returned `url`; on any failure or unrecognized shape, it falls back to the exact same hand-built URL as before — this can only improve the link, never break the existing working flow. `onDemandInvoicing()` (JS side) now also persists this real `payment_url` onto a NEW field (`Zoho_Books_Payment_URL` / `Zoho_Books_Payment_URL_Remaining` — see `FIELDS.md`, needs creating in Zoho) via a new `paymentUrlField` parameter, mirroring how `invoiceIdField` already works.

**Needs creating in Zoho**: `Zoho_Books_Payment_URL` and `Zoho_Books_Payment_URL_Remaining` (Single Line Text) on `Create_Case` — degrades safely (falls back to the old hand-built URL) until then, same as every other not-yet-created field in this app.

**Needs redeploying**: this widget itself (client-side JS) — freshly repacked. `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` needs re-pasting into its Custom API.

**Genuinely uncertain, flagged not hidden**: the exact JSON field name Zoho Books' payment-link endpoint returns isn't confirmed — several plausible names are tried, and the raw response is logged (`info "onDemandInvoicing — payment link raw response: ..."`) specifically so a live test can confirm or correct this list. If none of the tried names match, this silently falls back to today's exact existing behavior (no regression), but also won't yet show the improvement — check the Custom API's own execution log after the next real invoice creation to see the actual response shape.

## 2026-09-10 — Breakdown/Drop Location: map picker on the Customer Page, plus coordinate validation on both sides

User showed a screenshot of this app's own "Capture Locations" step (`Paste Breakdown Lat,Long` + readonly `Breakdown Latitude`/`Longitude`) right after `customerPage` got a map-based location picker (see that widget's own README), and asked that both the customer and agent paths for setting Breakdown/Drop Location: (1) work correctly, (2) actually save into `Create_Case`, (3) stay synchronized with this Agent Portal, and (4) validate the coordinates on both the map-selected and manually-entered paths.

Most of this was already true and needed no change: `Save & Continue` on this step already persists `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` to `Create_Case` via `buildPayload()`/`saveWithCoordFallback()`; the 30s dashboard poll (`pollDashboardForMechanicUpdates()` → `hasCoordPair()`/`updateLocationSnapshot()`/`notifyLocationShared()`) already detects a customer-submitted location (either field pair going blank → populated) and surfaces a notification card, so this Agent Portal already stays in sync with whatever the customer submits on the separate `customerPage` widget.

**The actual gap, fixed**: `wireField()`'s `parseTarget` handler (backing `LatLongPaste`/`DropLatLongPaste`) accepted any two parseable numbers with zero range checking — a typo'd paste like "500, 999" would have been written straight into `Latitude`/`Longitude` as if it were real GPS coordinates. Now range-checked (-90..90 / -180..180) the same way `customerPage`'s `submitCustomerLocation.deluge` now is (see that widget's own matching README entry, same day) — an out-of-range pair is left alone (same existing precedent this handler already had for unparsable text — doesn't wipe a still-valid previous value) with a `toast()` explaining why nothing changed.

**Needs redeploying**: this widget (`dist/getRescueTicket.zip`, re-packed).

**Tested**: syntax-checked only (script block parses cleanly) — not yet independently live-tested; pasting an out-of-range pair into either lat/long field should show the new toast and leave the readonly fields unchanged.

Uses `formatIndianPhone()` (already proven elsewhere in this file for WhatsApp sending) for the `tel:` URI.

## 2026-09-10 (later still) — real Zoho Payments payment link before sending the booking amount on WhatsApp

Explicit user request, with a real curl request + real response provided: "for the customer payment when we are sending the booking amount before that we need to generate the payment link... check the payload and pass the dynamic account and other details and then send this payment link on whatsapp for payment."

**Replaces the old guess with a confirmed real endpoint.** `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` previously tried Zoho Books' own `/share/paymentlink` for this, but that was always an unconfirmed guess — no documented response field name was ever found (see that block's own long git history: several candidate field names tried defensively, raw response logged for a live test that never fully confirmed one). The user's message supplies a real, working alternative: Zoho Payments' own **Payment Links API** (`POST https://payments.zoho.in/api/v1/paymentlinks?account_id=60078772586`), with a real example request and response. This directly replaces that old block — same fallback behavior (falls back to the old hand-built invoice-view URL if this ever fails), same output field (`payment_url`), so the widget's own consumption of it is completely unchanged.

**"Dynamic account and other details"**: the payload is built per-ticket, not hardcoded like the curl example —
- `amount` = the real per-ticket charge (`totalAmount`, already parsed as a Decimal).
- `email`/`phone` = the customer's own `Email`/`Phone_Number1` (phone cleaned to a bare 10-digit string — done in the **widget's own JS**, not Deluge: `customerPhoneDigits`, via a plain regex — this project has no confirmed-safe way to strip characters from a string in Deluge itself, no prior `.replaceAll()`/regex usage anywhere in its `.deluge` files to build on).
- `reference_id`/`description` = the same `referenceNumberResolved`/`descriptionText` already used for the Zoho Books invoice (e.g. `"RSID508006-BF"`).
- `expires_at` = today + 2 days, computed in JS (`expiresAt`) — a judgment call (prompt payment matters for an active rescue), not a confirmed business requirement, easy to change.
- `return_url` = the customer's own Customer Page link (`buildCustomerPageUrl()`, mode inferred from `referenceSuffix` the same way `taxExempt` already infers from it) — sends them back to the page that's already polling for payment success.
- `notify_customer` = `{email:false, sms:false}` deliberately — this function's own caller sends the real WhatsApp message right after, so Zoho Payments' own notification is switched off to avoid double-messaging the customer.
- `configurations.allowed_payment_methods` = `["upi","card"]`, matching the confirmed example exactly.

**Needs setting up in Zoho — this is the actual blocker, not the code**: a **new Zoho Creator Connection**, named exactly `zoho_payments_connection` (parallel to the existing `zoho_books_connection`), authorized for the Zoho Payments API. This project already uses the Connection mechanism successfully for Zoho Books (see every `invokeurl` call in this same file) — Connections handle the refresh-token → access-token exchange automatically and securely, so this reuses an already-proven pattern rather than hand-rolling token refresh logic in Deluge. **Genuinely uncertain, flagged not hidden**: the exact OAuth scope name to authorize that Connection with — check Zoho Payments' own API documentation when creating it.

**Needs re-registering in the Custom API's own setup screen** (same lesson learned repeatedly in this project — a new Deluge parameter can silently arrive `null` if not re-registered there separately from the function signature): `customerPhoneDigits`, `expiresAt`, `returnUrl`.

**Needs re-pasting**: `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` into its Custom API.

**Needs redeploying**: this widget (`dist/getRescueTicket.zip`, re-packed).

**Tested**: syntax-checked only (braces/brackets balanced, script parses cleanly) — not yet independently live-tested. Needs the Connection created first before any real call can succeed at all; until then this degrades exactly as safely as the old block did (falls back to the invoice-view URL, never blocks the invoice itself).
