## 2026-09-10 (later) — real field names wired in; explicit security note on why this stays server-side

User created the real Zoho fields (`Zoho_Payment_URL`/`Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining`) and asked to convert the Zoho Payments curl call into client-side JS, writing directly to these fields from the browser.

**Not done as asked, deliberately — real security concern, not a style choice**: the refresh-token → access-token OAuth exchange (`client_secret`/`refresh_token`) must never run in `getRescueTicket/app/widget.html` or any other browser-loaded code. Anyone with DevTools open while the widget is loaded could read those secrets straight out of the page source and use them to authenticate as this business's own Zoho Payments account, completely outside this app (generate arbitrary payment links, or worse depending on the token's real scope). That's exactly why this lives in `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` (Zoho's own servers hold the request; the browser never sees the credentials) — this is standard practice for any OAuth client secret, not specific to Zoho.

**What was actually changed**: `Send_Payment_Link`/`Send_Payment_Link1` now write to the user's real fields (`Zoho_Payment_URL`/`Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining`) instead of the earlier placeholder names (`Zoho_Books_Payment_URL`/`Zoho_Books_Payment_URL_Remaining`, which were never confirmed to exist in Zoho). The server-side generation itself (`onDemandInvoicing.deluge`'s Zoho Payments call, already built and using real credentials) is unchanged — this delivers the same end result the user asked for (a real payment URL saved to a real field, used by Customer Page's Pay Now button) without moving OAuth secrets into the browser.

**Needs redeploying**: this widget (`dist/getRescueTicket.zip`, re-packed). No Deluge re-pasting needed for this specific change (onDemandInvoicing.deluge's own field-writing already used whatever field name the widget passed in as `paymentUrlField` — only the widget's own call sites changed).

**Tested**: syntax-checked only.

## 2026-09-10 (later) — new standalone generateZohoPaymentLink Custom API, onDemandInvoicing no longer called for this

Explicit user request: "create new custom api for that and now dont use onDemandInvoicing function we will work on this later."

**New file**: `custom-apis/generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge` — the Zoho Payments "Payment Links" call (real refresh-token → access-token exchange, real request/response shape) pulled out of `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` into its own independent Custom API, with zero dependency on Zoho Books invoicing or on `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` at all. Takes `recordId`, `amount`, `email`, `phoneDigits`, `referenceId`, `description`, `expiresAt`, `paymentUrlField` — generates the link and writes it straight onto `Create_Case` itself (two-step fetch-then-write, this project's own confirmed-working update pattern), returning `{code, message, url, payment_link_id}`.

**Widget side**: new `generateZohoPaymentLink()` JS wrapper (right after `onDemandInvoicing()`'s own definition) computes `customerPhoneDigits`/`expiresAt` the same way `onDemandInvoicing()`'s own wrapper already does, and calls the new Custom API. `Send_Payment_Link`/`Send_Payment_Link1` now call **this** instead of `onDemandInvoicing()` — no Zoho Books invoice gets created by these two buttons anymore, only a real payment link. New small `serviceLabelFor()` helper extracted for these call sites (same computation `onDemandInvoicing()`'s own wrapper already does inline, which is left untouched).

**`onDemandInvoicing.deluge` and its JS wrapper are both left completely untouched** — not deleted, not modified — just no longer called from these two buttons. Per the user's own "we will work on this later" framing, this is deliberately reversible: swapping the one line back at each call site restores the old invoice+payment-link-together behavior if/when that's revisited.

**Needs creating in Zoho**: the `generateZohoPaymentLink` Custom API itself (paste this new file's contents in, POST, PublicKey or whatever auth this app's other Custom APIs use — same setup as `onDemandInvoicing` originally needed) — this is a brand new Custom API, not a re-paste of an existing one.

**Needs registering in that Custom API's own setup screen**: all 8 parameters (`recordId`, `amount`, `email`, `phoneDigits`, `referenceId`, `description`, `expiresAt`, `paymentUrlField`).

**Needs redeploying**: this widget (`dist/getRescueTicket.zip`, re-packed).

**Tested**: braces/brackets verified balanced, syntax-checked, and the file's presence on disk re-verified after writing (given the earlier discovery that this exact folder can silently revert a file) — not yet independently live-tested.

## 2026-09-10 (later) — real root cause of "'X' has no matching records" finally confirmed

Live error on the very first test of `generateZohoPaymentLink`: `{"code":"ERROR","message":"Error at line : 208, 'updateRec' has no matching records. Unable to update the value updateRec.Zoho_Payment_URL."}` — the same error family this project hit and never fully explained on `submitCustomerLocation.deluge` earlier (see `customerPage/README.md`'s own long history on that).

**Root cause, confirmed by comparing every write in this project**: fetching the SAME record TWICE with identical criteria within one Custom API execution fails — once for an existence/null-check, then again later for the actual write. Every write that's ever worked in this project (`createServiceRequest.deluge`'s own `Case_ID` update, `CreateServiceTicket.deluge`) fetches a record exactly ONCE, immediately before writing to it. `generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge` fetched `rec` for the null-check, then separately re-fetched the identical record as `updateRec` for the write — that second, redundant fetch is what failed.

**Fixed**: writes directly onto `rec` (already fetched, already confirmed non-null) instead of re-fetching a new variable — no second query at all.

**Also fixed the same day, same root cause, same fix**: `customerPage/custom-apis/submitCustomerLocation.deluge` and `submitCustomerFeedback.deluge` — both had this exact double-fetch pattern (a leftover from the earlier, unrelated "Improper Statement" investigation, which had introduced the re-fetch specifically to rule out reusing `rec` — the opposite of the real fix). See that project's own README for the matching entry.

**Needs re-pasting**: `generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge` into its Custom API.

**Tested**: braces verified balanced — not yet independently re-tested live.

## 2026-09-10 (later) — real bug fixed: trailing space crashed recordId.toLong()

Live error in production: `{"code":"ERROR","message":"Error at line : 30, Unable to cast the '448881000000543009 ' value into a 'Long' value because the input is in an invalid format"}` — note the trailing space inside the quoted value itself.

**Fixed**: `rec = Create_Case[ID == recordId.toLong()];` → `recordId.trim().toLong()`, same defensive pattern already proven elsewhere in this project (`createServiceRequest.deluge`'s own `vehicleId.trim().toLong()`) — a stray leading/trailing space (e.g. from a manual test-panel paste, or any future caller) no longer crashes the function before it even checks whether the ticket exists.

**Needs re-pasting**: `generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge` into its Custom API (again — 3rd time today; each fix has been a real, distinct bug, not repeat guessing at the same one).

**Tested**: braces/brackets verified balanced.

## 2026-09-10 (later still) — "Quick" build-priority group: visible payment/location links, Vendor Mobile Number, CLOSED tickets hidden from dashboard

Per explicit user instruction ("fix the points that require the least effort first") — the 3 items grouped as "Quick" out of the full soft-test backlog (see the Soft-Test Findings artifact). All three are additive/display-only changes; nothing existing was removed or restructured.

**Item #4/#7/#23 — payment and location links now visible on the agent's own screen** (previously only ever reached WhatsApp/the Customer Page, never shown back to the agent):
- Generalized the `navlink` field type in `buildField()` with two new, optional, backward-compatible properties — neither existing `navlink` field (`Navigation_Link`, `Navigate_To_Breakdown_Link`) sets either one, so both keep their exact original Break_Down_Location1/lat-lon behavior unchanged:
  - `f.hrefField` — reads the link straight from a named stored record field (e.g. `Zoho_Payment_URL`).
  - `f.hrefCompute(record)` — computes the link at render time via a function, for a link that's never persisted anywhere (the location-share link, rebuilt via the existing `buildCustomerPageUrl()`).
- 4 new read-only, computed-display fields added (nothing new stored, no schema change needed):
  - `__PaymentLinkDisplay_BF` (Quote/Booking-Fee stage, right after `Send_Payment_Link`) — `hrefField:"Zoho_Payment_URL"`.
  - `__PaymentLinkDisplay_RF` (Final Payment stage, right after `Send_Payment_Link1`) — `hrefField:"Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining"`.
  - `__LocationLinkDisplay_Breakdown` (Locations stage, after "Send Breakdown Location Link") — `hrefCompute` rebuilding the same Customer Page URL the WhatsApp send already builds.
  - `__LocationLinkDisplay_Drop` (Locations stage, after "Send Drop Location Link", TOW-only same as its sibling button) — same pattern, `locationType:"drop"`.
- Each shows a styled button link when the value/computation resolves, or a plain-text hint (e.g. "No payment link yet — click ... above to generate one") when it doesn't — never a blank/broken-looking field.

**Item #17 — Vendor Mobile Number now shown once a vendor's accepted** (previously only Vendor Email showed on the acceptance display, matching the client's exact complaint: "accepted vendor shows email instead of Name/Mobile"):
- New `t:"computed"` read-only field (reuses the existing, already-proven "computed display, nothing stored" mechanism — same one `navlink`/`__PaymentLinkDisplay_*` above also use) added right after `Assigned_Vendor` on both `acceptance` (RSR, field name `__VendorMobileDisplay`) and `acceptanceTow` (TOW, `__VendorMobileDisplay_TOW`).
- `compute:(r)=>vendorRawForTicket(r)[CONFIG.vendorPhoneField]` — reuses `vendorRawForTicket()` (already resolves `Assigned_Vendor` to the vendor's raw `Vendors_Report` record elsewhere in this file, e.g. `renderVendorAcctFeeBox()`) and `CONFIG.vendorPhoneField` (`"Mobile_Number_01"`, the same confirmed-real field `recordVendorPayment()` already sends to Zoho Books) — no new field lookups invented.
- Vendor *Name* was already shown (`vendorNamesFor()`/`Assigned_Vendor`'s own label) — only Mobile Number was actually missing, so only that was added.

**Item #22 — CLOSED tickets no longer clutter the default dashboard**:
- `renderReportLists()` (called by `loadDashboard()`) now skips any record whose `Status` is exactly `"CLOSED"` (the final Agent-Closure state written by `saveClosure()`) before bucketing — it simply never lands in any of the 4 dashboard buckets.
- Deliberately narrow: `"RSP CLOSED"`/`"RSP CANCELLED"`/`"CX REJECT"` (the "RSP Closed" bucket's other 3 statuses) are left untouched — those are earlier-stage "no longer active but not yet fully closed out" statuses, not what the client's complaint ("Closed tickets remain visible... should be archived and reachable only via search") was about.
- Search stays fully unaffected — `runSearch()` calls `getTickets()` with its own independent criteria (phone/Case ID/etc.), never reusing `renderReportLists()`'s filtered list, so a CLOSED ticket is still one search away, view-only, exactly matching the client's own requested behavior.
- Other `loadDashboard()` consumers of the raw (unfiltered) `recs` — `updateDashboardSnapshot()`, `updateLocationSnapshot()`, the mechanic-update poller — were deliberately left untouched; only the visible list rendering changed.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check on the extracted `<script>` block passed after each edit; `zet pack` run, `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` re-packed. Not yet live-tested by the user — needs redeploying, then a live check that (a) a real `Zoho_Payment_URL`/`_Remaining` value shows its link button, (b) an accepted vendor's mobile number shows correctly, (c) a `CLOSED` ticket no longer appears on the dashboard but is still found via phone/Case-ID search.

## 2026-09-10 (later still) — Ozonetel voice-callback integration: "Call via System" button + live status

User-provided, user-created Custom APIs — NOT built by this session, just consumed. Two confirmed-live Custom API URLs given directly:
```
https://www.zohoapis.in/creator/custom/getrescued/getVoiceCallbackDetails   (places a callback call — customerId, phoneNumber, callbackMessage; returns {code, ucid, ...})
https://www.zohoapis.in/creator/custom/getrescued/getCallbackStatus         (checks a call's status — callbackId; returns {code, status, call_duration, ...})
```
`api_name`/`workspace_name` below match these URLs exactly (`workspace_name:"getrescued"`, same as every other Custom API call in this file).

**⚠ Response-shape not independently confirmed**: during this work the user shared two draft versions of `getCallbackStatus` with different outcome field names — one returned `call_result`, the other `disconnect_reason` (the newer draft, with a "FIX: fallback return statement" comment, looked like the more finished one). The user then said "i have created new custome api for that" without confirming which shape the finished version actually returns. `checkVoiceCallbackStatus()` below reads BOTH (`data.call_result || data.disconnect_reason`) defensively rather than guessing — if the on-screen call outcome ever looks blank/wrong, check the real field name in that Custom API's own execution log first.

**What was added**:
- `placeVoiceCallback(record, phoneNumber)` / `checkVoiceCallbackStatus(callbackId)` (app/widget.html, right after `generateZohoPaymentLink()`) — same `invokeCustomApi()` round-trip convention as every other Custom API call in this file (`res.result` parsed as JSON if it comes back as a string). `http_method:"POST"` for BOTH calls, including the status *check* — confirmed live elsewhere in this file (`checkOperationsManagerPermission()`'s own 2026-09-01 comment: this Zoho account's Custom APIs only ever accept POST, never GET, regardless of what the call conceptually is).
- `customerId` sent as the ticket's `Case_ID` via `caseLabel()` — explicit user choice, same field `generateZohoPaymentLink()`'s own `referenceId` already uses.
- New `f.ozonetelCall:true` flag on `Phone_Number1` and `Alternate_Phone_Number` (the only 2 `t:"tel"` fields in this file, both on the `create` stage — confirmed via grep, no other stage independently defines these fields). `buildField()`'s generic tel branch renders a "📲 Call via System" button + status line right under the existing tel: click-to-dial link when the flag is set and a value exists; `wireField()`'s new `if(f.ozonetelCall)` block (end of the function, runs in addition to whatever the existing f.t chain already did — tel fields don't match any of its branches) does the actual click → `placeVoiceCallback()` → show "Placing call…" → on success, auto-checks status once after 4s, plus a manual "Refresh" link any time after.
- **Deliberately NOT persisted to any Zoho field** — the call's UCID and live status live only in `state.record["_ozCall_"+fieldName]`, a non-field key `buildPayload()` never reads (it only iterates `step.fields`, see its own comment) — so nothing new is ever written to `Create_Case` on Save. This is genuinely ephemeral: refreshing the page or reopening the ticket loses it, same as this widget's other in-memory-only UI state (e.g. `EXPANDED_BUCKETS`). No Zoho field was requested to persist the call ID/status permanently — if that's wanted later (e.g. a call history, or the outcome showing up on reopen), a real field (e.g. `Last_Callback_UCID`) needs adding in Zoho Studio first.
- The two draft Deluge functions themselves (`getVoiceCallbackDetails`/`getCallbackStatus`) are NOT saved into this repo's `custom-apis/` folder, unlike every other Custom API this project tracks — deliberately, since the user built/finalized them directly in Zoho and the exact final source was never re-shared/re-confirmed after the two-draft ambiguity above. Saving a possibly-stale guess as if it were the tracked source of truth would be worse than not tracking it at all.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check passed; `zet pack` run, `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` re-packed; edits confirmed persisted via a fresh grep re-read (this OneDrive-synced folder has silently reverted a `.deluge` edit once before this session — same re-read discipline applied here). Not yet live-tested — needs redeploying, then a real call placed from a real ticket to confirm (a) the call actually reaches the customer's phone, (b) a `ucid` comes back and the button doesn't just show "Call placed, but no call ID came back", (c) the auto/manual status refresh shows real data, not blank — and if outcome shows blank specifically, that's the `call_result`/`disconnect_reason` ambiguity flagged above, not a code bug.

## 2026-09-10 (later still) — least-effort-first pass: Timed Out status, Round Trip Distance on Reach, Android nav fix

Per explicit user instruction ("check and verify the tasks that require the least effort... implement the remaining quick fixes first"). 5 background investigations (image capture, Round Trip, Android nav, Timed Out feasibility, QR feasibility) ran first to get real effort estimates instead of guessing — see each item below for what they found. QR code generation (item #6) was confirmed as the largest of the five (no QR library anywhere in the repo, CSP currently blocks all CDN libraries in these widgets, plus new data plumbing needed in vendorTicket/technicianTicket) — deliberately deferred, not started.

**Item #17 (second half) — real "Timed Out" vendor invite status.** Previously: an invite either showed "Rejected", "Accepted", or "Awaiting response" forever, no matter how long it sat unanswered.
- New `INVITE_TIMEOUT_MS` constant (15 min, matching `LOCK_STALE_MS`'s own value as a reasonable default — **not an explicit client SLA**, just a sensible starting point, easy to change).
- `inviteStatusFor(inv)` now returns `{label:"Timed Out", color:"var(--muted)"}` when still-awaiting and `Invited_At` (already a real, already-written field — see `syncInvites()`) is older than the threshold. Parsed via the existing `parseDateTime24()` (already proven on `Locked_At` elsewhere in this file).
- `assignInviteStatusHtml()` now also shows elapsed time ("(22m ago)") next to Awaiting/Timed Out rows, via the existing `fmtDurationShort()`.
- Purely additive/display-only — nothing new is written to Zoho, and `rejectedVendorIds()` (which only ever matches the `"Rejected"` label) is completely unaffected.

**Item #19 (second half) — Round Trip Distance now visible on the Reach stage.** Previously only existed at WIP/Final Closure (`Roundtrip_Distance`), two steps later than the client's actual complaint ("REACH page (Repair cases) needs to display... Round Trip Distance").
- New `__RoundTripEstimate` (RSR, `reach` stage) / `__RoundTripEstimate_TOW` (TOW, `reachTow` stage) — `t:"computed"` display-only fields, reusing the existing `calcRoundTripKm()` formula as-is (already proven on the `locations` stage's own Calculate Round-Trip Distance button; already branch-agnostic — falls back to a 2-leg Office→Breakdown→Office estimate when no drop coordinates exist, which covers every RSR ticket automatically).
- **Deliberately does NOT write into `Roundtrip_Distance` itself** — that field's own Final Closure backfill (`runClosureDistanceCalculations()`) only computes a value "if empty," preferring Google Distance Matrix over Haversine. Writing an early Haversine-only estimate here would have permanently locked out that more-accurate later calculation for every ticket where this field is seen. Labeled "(Estimate)" and "(straight-line estimate via office)" to make clear this is a rougher, earlier number than the one Final Closure eventually computes.

**Item #12 — "Navigate to Breakdown" now uses a `geo:` URI on Android**, across all 4 widgets. Previously every nav link was a plain `https://maps.google.com/?q=lat,lon` URL, which Android's own map-app chooser doesn't reliably honor from inside a webview/browser tab.
- New `buildNavUrl(lat, lon)` helper, added once per widget file (no shared JS module exists across these 4 widgets — same duplication convention as `esc()`/`readVal()`/etc.): `vendorTicket` (5 call sites), `technicianTicket` (2), `driverTicket` (4), `getRescueTicket` (1, inside `buildField()`'s `navlink` branch).
- Detects Android via `navigator.userAgent`, returns a `geo:lat,lon?q=lat,lon` URI (RFC 5870) there; every other platform (iOS/desktop) gets the exact same `maps.google.com` link as before, byte-for-byte unchanged.
- Every call site's own existing null/empty-check logic (whether a stored link value exists first, whether lat/lon are present) is completely untouched — only the URL-construction sub-expression itself was swapped for a function call.

**Item #14 — image capture no longer encodes at full native camera resolution.** `capturePhoto()` (byte-identical across `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket`, confirmed via diff) previously sized its canvas at `video.videoWidth`/`videoHeight` — the camera's raw stream resolution, often 1920×1080+ — with no cap.
- New `CAPTURE_MAX_DIM = 1600` constant (matches the backlog item's own suggested figure) in all 3 files. Canvas now sizes to `nativeDimension * Math.min(1, 1600/max(nativeW,nativeH))` — scales down only, never up, so a device whose native stream is already under 1600px is completely unaffected.
- JPEG quality (0.85) deliberately left unchanged — the original complaint said "compression itself is good," only the missing size cap was the problem.
- `getRescueTicket` has no camera capture code at all (confirmed) — not part of this fix. The separate gallery-picker path (item #16, raw uploads with zero processing) was also deliberately left untouched — bigger scope, not part of this "quick fixes" pass.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check passed on all 4 files; `zet pack` re-run on all 4 (`getRescueTicket`, `vendorTicket`, `technicianTicket`, `driverTicket`); every new identifier confirmed present via a fresh grep re-read after packing (this OneDrive-synced folder has silently reverted an edit once before this session). **Not yet live-tested** — needs redeploying across all 4 widgets, then real-device testing in particular for the Android `geo:` URI change (no Android device was available to test against directly).

## 2026-09-10 (yet later) — item #24: `Decimal Points` widened live in Zoho; `roundCoord()` call sites matched to the real new limit

User widened `Latitude`'s (and, per the same change, the other 3 coordinate fields') Zoho field config to Max Digits=15/Decimal Points=6 (screenshot confirmed) — the actual fix for item #24, see `customerPage/README.md`'s own matching entry for the full root-cause writeup. Asked this widget specifically to verify nothing here breaks under the new, *stricter* decimal-places limit (old config nominally allowed 15 decimal places, even though the 1-digit whole-number cap made the field unusable regardless; new config genuinely only allows 6).

**Full audit run first** (a background Explore pass across every widget/`.deluge` file in the repo) — findings:
- `customerPage` — already safe, explicitly rounds to 6dp both client-side (`.toFixed(6)`) and server-side (`.round(6)`) before ever writing.
- `getRescueTicket` — safe in the end, but not directly: `roundCoord()` (see its own comment) defaults to **17** decimal places when called with no second argument, and all 3 of this file's own call sites (`LatLongPaste`/`DropLatLongPaste` parse, the quick-edit payload builder, `buildPayload()`'s safety net) called it exactly that way. The first save attempt would therefore send up to 17dp, relying entirely on the existing `saveWithCoordFallback()`/`COORD_PRECISION_FALLBACKS` retry loop to step back down to 6 — which itself depends on Zoho's rejection message matching `/maximum digits/i`, a regex written against the OLD "too few whole-number digits" error text and never confirmed against a "too many decimal places" rejection specifically.
- `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` — read-only for these 4 fields, no write risk.
- `Ticket Kanban` (a separate, older "operations board" widget hitting the exact same `Create_Case` fields) — **confirmed real gap, not touched today**: zero rounding, zero range validation, zero retry mechanism at all for these 4 fields; `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` aren't even `readonly` there (unlike this widget). This gap predates today's field change (it would have hit the old whole-number-digit bug too) but is now the more likely failure mode there specifically if anyone still uses it to set a location. Flagged to the user directly — deliberately not fixed without being asked, since it's a different widget outside today's authorized scope.

**Fixed here (getRescueTicket only)**: all 3 `roundCoord()` call sites (`wireField()`'s `parseTarget` branch, the quick-edit payload builder, `buildPayload()`'s safety net) now pass `roundCoord(v, 6)` explicitly instead of relying on the 17-decimal default — matches the real, confirmed field limit directly, so the *first* save attempt already fits instead of gambling on the retry loop's error-text match. `saveWithCoordFallback()`/`COORD_PRECISION_FALLBACKS` themselves are completely untouched — still there as defense-in-depth for any other unexpected digit-limit surprise, just no longer load-bearing for the common case.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check passed; `zet pack` re-run; all 3 call sites confirmed updated via fresh grep. Not yet live-tested — the next real coordinate save (customer location share, or an agent's manual lat/long paste) should now succeed on the first attempt instead of needing the retry loop to intervene.

## 2026-09-11 — Real bug fixed: "Already uploaded" showed for Pre/Post-service Photo even with zero real files

User-reported live, screenshot: both file boxes showed "No file chosen" (the native input, always blank on load) while the hint text underneath said "Already uploaded" — genuinely contradictory, and no thumbnail was shown either.

**Root cause**: `Image_Upload3`/`Image_Upload4` ("Pre-service Photo"/"Post-service Photo") are Zoho **Multiple File Upload** fields (confirmed, `FIELDS.md:518-519`) — when nothing's been uploaded, Zoho returns an **empty array `[]`** for them, not `null`/`""` like a regular single File Upload field. `[]` is truthy in JavaScript, so every place that checked `state.record[f.n]` (or a local alias of it) directly with `? :` treated "zero files" as "a file exists." `fileCountFor()` (already existing, already correctly treating `[]` as 0) was available but wasn't being used consistently everywhere — the thumbnail count used it correctly (which is why no thumbnail showed), but the *hint text* next to it didn't, producing exactly this contradiction.

**Fixed in 3 places, all in `app/widget.html`, all the same one-line shape (swap raw truthiness of the record value for `fileCountFor(value)`)**:
1. `buildField()`'s own `t==="file"` branch — the live wizard step + click-to-edit popup (same shared render path) — `noFileHintText`'s condition and the final `cur?uploadedHintText:noFileHintText` ternary both now key off `fileCount` instead of `cur`.
2. `closureFieldValueDisplay()` — the Final Closure summary view's own "Uploaded"/"Not uploaded" text — same swap, `fileCountFor(v)` instead of raw `v`.
3. `collectStep()`'s mandatory-file gate (`reqFile`) — **not actively triggered today** (no multi-file field currently sets `reqFile`; only `Image_Upload`, a regular single-file field, does), but the exact same bug shape existed here too and would have silently let an agent bypass a required-photo check on any multi-file field a `reqFile` gate is ever added to later. Fixed defensively, zero behavior change for the single-file case that works today.

All three fixes are strictly a correctness improvement for the empty-array case — for a normal single File Upload field (empty is `null`/`""`, already properly falsy), `fileCountFor()` returns the exact same result raw truthiness would have, so nothing about any other existing file field's behavior changes.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check passed; `zet pack` re-run; all 3 fixes confirmed present via fresh grep. Not yet live-tested — needs redeploying, then reopening a WIP-stage ticket with these two fields still empty to confirm the hint now correctly says "not yet uploaded" instead of "Already uploaded."

Only this button's visibility changes; `Payment_Method_Final`/`Payment_received`/the QR block/`QR_Code` field are all untouched, and `buildPayload()`'s existing `if(f.when && !f.when(r)) return;` (the same mechanism already gating every other conditional field in this file) means a hidden button is correctly skipped on save, same as every other `when`-gated field already works today.

**Tested**: syntax check passed, `zet pack` re-run, gate confirmed present via fresh grep. Not yet live-tested — needs redeploying, then confirming the button disappears the instant "Payment Gateway" is picked and reappears for any other option.

## 2026-09-11 (later still) — "Generate Invoice" button added for ON DEMAND clients on Final Closure

Explicit user request, per the "On-Demand Workflow for Invoicing" PDF ("an invoice should be created when the ticket is Closed/Complete"). New button on the Agent Closure step, calling the existing (previously disconnected) `onDemandInvoicing()` function — the real Zoho Books invoice creator (GST breakup, tax, email, invoice number), left fully intact since being unplugged from `Send_Payment_Link`/`Send_Payment_Link1` on 2026-09-10.

**What was added, both new entries at the end of `CLOSURE_FIELDS`**:
- `Generate_OnDemand_Invoice` (`t:"button"`) — visible only when `onDemandInvoiceEligibility(s, computeClosureTSF(s))` returns eligible AND `isOnDemand` is true. Both functions **reused exactly as-is** — the same ones `saveClosure()`'s own `maybeCreateOnDemandInvoice()` already uses for its own separate internal-tracking-record write — so this button's visibility rule can never drift out of sync with the existing eligibility logic, and it can never appear for a non-ON-DEMAND client or a ticket that isn't Closed/Complete.
- `__OnDemandInvoiceIdDisplay` (`t:"computed"`) — shows the resulting `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` once generated (that field was never displayed anywhere in this widget before today). Same read-only display pattern already proven this session for the payment-link/vendor-mobile displays.

**Duplicate-invoice safeguards** (a real financial/audit risk `onDemandInvoicing()` itself has no built-in protection against — it always creates a fresh invoice on every call):
1. If `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` is already set on the ticket, the action refuses outright with a toast, before calling anything.
2. A local in-flight flag (`state._generatingOnDemandInvoice`) blocks a rapid double-click while the first call is still in progress (needed because the button is `repeatable:true` — same tradeoff as every other network-calling button in this file, so a genuine failure can be retried — and `Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` itself only gets refreshed into `state.record` *after* the first call's own async write completes).
3. Amount passed is `computeClosureTSF(r)` (the confirmed-paid net total, same figure the auto-tracking record already invoices) via `onDemandInvoicing()`'s own `amountOverride` parameter — not its internal default (`Total_Service_Fee`, the original quote) — matching the spec's "final amount" wording precisely.
4. `paymentUrlField` is deliberately omitted (`null`) so this call can never touch `Zoho_Payment_URL`/`_Remaining` — those stay exclusively owned by `generateZohoPaymentLink()`, zero overlap with the payment-link flow for any client type.

**Real, pre-existing bug found and fixed along the way**: `buildPayload()`'s `t:"check"`/`t:"button"` branch unconditionally includes those fields in the save payload (`["Yes"]`/`[]`), unlike every other field type, which naturally gets omitted when blank. A brand-new button field would have required a matching brand-new Zoho field (`Generate_OnDemand_Invoice`, Checkbox type) to exist before *any* closure save for an eligible ticket could succeed — otherwise every such save would start failing, a real regression risk, not just "the feature doesn't work yet." Added a new optional `f.ephemeral` flag instead — `buildPayload()` now skips a field entirely when it's set, exactly like `t:"summary"`/`t:"file"` already are. Every existing field leaves this unset (falsy), so nothing about any current field's behavior changes; only `Generate_OnDemand_Invoice` (the one field that opts in) is affected. **No new Zoho field is needed for this button at all.**

**Confirmed unaffected**: `Send_Payment_Link`/`Send_Payment_Link1`/`generateZohoPaymentLink()` — not touched in any way, for any client type, ON DEMAND included. `grp()` (the closure summary's own row-grouping) already excludes `t:"button"`/`t:"computed"` fields entirely (confirmed via its own filter, `f.t==="button"||...||f.t==="computed"`), so neither new field can appear duplicated in the summary view.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check passed; `zet pack` re-run; both new fields and the `ephemeral` flag confirmed present via fresh grep. **Not yet live-tested** — needs redeploying, then a real ON DEMAND ticket closed as "Completed" to confirm the button appears, generates a real invoice, and the invoice ID displays correctly; also worth confirming a second click (or reopening the ticket) correctly refuses to generate a duplicate.

## 2026-09-11 — "Capture location + timestamp at every relevant step" — full lifecycle audit, field-reuse bug fixed, new capture points wired

Explicit user request: systematically capture lat/lon/timestamp at every meaningful step across the ticket lifecycle, for later calculations/reporting — with an explicit instruction not to let steps "incorrectly share or duplicate fields." Two full background audits ran first (one for RSR, one for TOW) across every field-facing widget plus this one and `customerPage`, reading every actual write site rather than inferring from field names — full findings below. This file itself needed no code changes (it only ever *reads* these fields for display/distance calcs; every write happens in `customerPage`/`vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket`) — logged here as the hub record for the whole feature.

### What the audit found

| Step | Branch | Before | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ticket Created | Both | No lat/lon expected (agent at a desk); `Added_Time` is a Zoho system field | Correct as-is, no change |
| Customer shares Breakdown Location | Both | `Latitude`/`Longitude` captured live; `Location_Received_Time` only stamped later, whenever the agent's own `locations` step next saved | **Fixed** — now stamped at the actual moment of capture |
| Customer shares Drop Location | TOW | `DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` captured live; **no timestamp field existed at all** | **Fixed** — new `Drop_Location_Received_Time` field, stamped at capture |
| Vendor/Technician/Driver Accepts | Both | `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`RSP_Start_Longitude` + `Service_Acceptance`/`Service_Acceptance_For_Tow` — already clean | No change needed |
| Reaches Breakdown Location | Both | `Reach_Location_Lat`/`Reach_Location_Lon` + `Reach_Time` — already clean | No change needed |
| Work/WIP Starts (RSR) | RSR | **Doesn't exist as a distinct action** — Reach and "work starts" are the same click today | Left as-is — see decision note below |
| Vehicle Loaded (TOW) | TOW | `Pickup_Time` captured; a live GPS fix was already being fetched for a distance calc, then discarded — no lat/lon ever saved | **Fixed** — new `Pickup_Location_Lat`/`Pickup_Location_Lon`, the already-fetched fix is now persisted |
| Work Completed (RSR) | RSR | `RSP_Completion_Time` captured; no GPS ever fetched at this step | **Fixed** — new `Work_Completion_Lat`/`Work_Completion_Lon`, new GPS fetch added |
| **Reaches Drop Location (TOW)** | TOW | **Reused the same `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`RSP_Start_Longitude` fields Accept writes** — a 2026-08-04 workaround to avoid creating a new field. Reaching the drop location silently overwrote and permanently lost the Accept-time position. | **Fixed — the real bug** — new dedicated `RSP_Drop_Latitude`/`RSP_Drop_Longitude` pair; Accept's own fields are never touched again |
| Unloading & Handover (TOW) | TOW | `RSP_Completion_Time` (shared across 4 different events by long-standing design, left as-is); **no GPS fetched at all** | **Fixed** — new `Handover_Location_Lat`/`Handover_Location_Lon`, new GPS fetch added |
| Case Cancelled | Both | `Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lon` + `Cancellation_Time` — already clean | No change needed |
| Agent Closes the case | Both | `Agent_Closure_Time` only — correct, the agent isn't physically at the scene | No change needed |

A secondary, lower-priority finding from the audit, **not fixed, flagged only**: `Roundtrip_Distance` (RSR) is written by both the Reach and Cancel actions — currently safe only because a ticket can only ever go through one or the other, never both, per today's flow. Worth a dedicated field pair if that assumption ever changes.

### New Zoho fields (all created by the user before this code was written — confirmed via screenshot)

| Field | Type | Step | Written from |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RSP_Drop_Latitude` / `RSP_Drop_Longitude` | Single Line Text (matches `RSP_Start_Latitude`'s own type) | Reached Drop Location (TOW) | `reachedDrop()` — `driverTicket`/`vendorTicket` |
| `Pickup_Location_Lat` / `Pickup_Location_Lon` | Single Line Text | Vehicle Loaded (TOW) | `vehiclePicked()` — `driverTicket`/`vendorTicket` |
| `Handover_Location_Lat` / `Handover_Location_Lon` | Single Line Text | Unloading & Handover (TOW) | `confirmDropped()` — `driverTicket`/`vendorTicket` |
| `Work_Completion_Lat` / `Work_Completion_Lon` | Single Line Text | Work Completed / Customer Rejected (RSR) | `workCompleted()`/`confirmCxReject()` — `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket` |
| `Drop_Location_Received_Time` | Date-Time | Customer shares Drop Location | `submitCustomerLocation.deluge` (see `customerPage/README.md`'s own matching entry) |

Every new lat/lon field follows the Single Line Text convention `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`Reach_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lat` already established — deliberately, to avoid re-triggering the exact Decimal digit-limit class of bug items #24/the `RSP_Start_Latitude` history already fought through this project. Every new capture reuses the existing `getPositionSafe()` helper already proven in each of these 3 widgets (Accept/Reach/Cancel all already use it) — no new GPS-fetch mechanism introduced, best-effort/graceful-degrade on denial exactly like every existing call site.

### Decision deferred, not made unilaterally

**"Work/WIP Starts" (RSR) has no distinct action in the app today** — left exactly as-is (Reach doubles as work-start) rather than adding a new button/screen, since that's a real on-road workflow change, not just a hidden-field addition, and wasn't explicitly requested. If a genuinely separate "work physically began" moment is needed later, it needs its own new action and its own new field pair — not a repurposing of `Reach_Location_Lat/Lon`/`Reach_Time`, which would recreate the exact field-sharing problem this whole pass was fixing.

**Tested**: `node -e "new Function(...)"` syntax check passed on `driverTicket`/`vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`; `zet pack` re-run on all 3; every new field name confirmed present via fresh grep after packing. See `customerPage/README.md`'s own entry for the `submitCustomerLocation.deluge` side (the `Location_Received_Time`/`Drop_Location_Received_Time` stamps). **Not yet live-tested** — needs redeploying all 3 field-facing widgets, then walking a real TOW ticket through Accept → Reach → Loading → Reached Drop → Unloading, and a real RSR ticket through Accept → Reach → Work Completed, confirming each step's own lat/lon lands in its own field without touching any other step's.

## 2026-09-11 (later) — `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` payloads now sent as strings, ahead of their planned Decimal → Single Line Text conversion

User asked whether converting these 4 fields (currently Decimal, Max Digits=15/Decimal Points=6) to Single Line Text — same type already used for `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`RSP_Drop_Latitude`/etc. for the identical "exceeded maximum digits" reason — would break anything. Audited every read/write site across all 5 apps (`driverTicket`, `vendorTicket`, `technicianTicket`, `getRescueTicket`, `Ticket Kanban` — same code as this widget — plus both `customerPage` Custom APIs):

- Every **read** site already goes through `Number(...)` or the defensive `parseCoord()` helper, both of which handle a string or a number identically — confirmed via exhaustive grep, no exceptions.
- `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge`'s `lat.toString() != ""` check exists only because Deluge won't compare a Decimal directly to `""` today — becomes an unnecessary but 100% harmless no-op once the field is Text.
- `submitCustomerLocation.deluge` already writes a Deluge **string** (`latVal.toString()`) to `rec.Latitude` — works today because Deluge auto-converts on assignment to a Decimal field, and needs no change once the field is Text.

One real gap found: this widget's own **save payload** sent these 4 fields as raw JS *numbers* (`Number(v)` then `roundCoord(v,6)`, itself a `Number`) — unlike every other Text-typed coordinate field in this app (`RSP_Start_Latitude` etc.), which were assumed (at the time) to always be written as strings. Closed that gap pre-emptively so the conversion has zero remaining unknowns:

- `buildPayload()` quick-edit single-field builder and the main step-save builder both now do `v=String(roundCoord(v,6))` for `COORD_FIELDS` instead of leaving `v` as a number.
- `saveWithCoordFallback()`'s own digit-limit retry loop (`COORD_PRECISION_FALLBACKS`) updated to match — `payload[k]=String(roundCoord(payload[k],dp))` — so a retry attempt (still possible while the fields remain Decimal in Zoho) stays consistent with the initial send.

Backward-compatible either way — a numeric string writes correctly to a Decimal field today (same mechanism `submitCustomerLocation.deluge` already relies on) and will write correctly to the Text field once converted, so this didn't need to wait on the Zoho-side conversion happening first. `saveWithCoordFallback`'s whole retry mechanism becomes dead weight for these 4 fields the moment they're Text (first attempt always succeeds, no digit-limit error left to trigger a retry) — left in place untouched, exactly as it already sits unused-but-harmless for the already-converted `RSP_Start_*`/`RSP_Drop_*` fields. Syntax-checked (`node -e "new Function(...)"` per script block) and packed via `zet pack`. **Not yet live-tested** — worth saving one ticket's Locations step both before and after the Zoho field type is actually flipped, to confirm the save still succeeds either way.

## 2026-09-11 (later still) — Zoho-side conversion confirmed live: all 8 coordinate fields are now Single Line Text

User confirmed both conversions are done in Zoho Studio: `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`RSP_Start_Longitude`/`RSP_Drop_Latitude`/`RSP_Drop_Longitude` (re-confirmed — these were already documented as Text since 2026-08-03/2026-09-11) **and** `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` (the actual fields behind the originally-reported `"Round off the Latitude field value to 6 decimal places"` error — newly converted). No further code changes needed on top of the string-send fix above; this closes out the "convert to Text" recommendation with nothing left pending — at the time.

- `FIELDS.md` updated in both places `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` are listed (Step 3 table and the Part 1 field spec) to show Single Line Text instead of Decimal.
- `zoho_schema_import/README.md`'s field-type-nuances note now lists all 3 coordinate pairs that must be Text, not just `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`Longitude`.
- The §4 open question about the unconfirmed Decimal digit limit is marked resolved for these 4 fields (see that entry, above) — **`Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lon` were still Decimal at this point**, not part of either conversion — see the next entry, same day, for the follow-up.

## 2026-09-11 (later still) — `Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lon` also converted; a comment-accuracy correction

User converted the last remaining Decimal coordinate pair, `Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lon` (the one flagged as still-outstanding in the previous entry), to Single Line Text too. **Every coordinate field in this app is now Single Line Text — no Decimal lat/lon fields remain anywhere in the schema.**

While checking whether this pair needed the same `String()` payload hardening as `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` above, found and corrected a factual error in this file and in `getRescueTicket/app/widget.html`'s own comments: the earlier claim that `RSP_Start_Latitude`/`RSP_Drop_Latitude`/`Cancel_Location_Lat` (all written straight from `getPositionSafe()`) are "always written as strings (`pos.coords.latitude.toFixed(15)`)" is **wrong** — `getPositionSafe()` actually returns `Number(pos.coords.latitude.toFixed(15))`, a JS *number*, not a string (see `getPositionSafe()` in `driverTicket`/`vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`). So `RSP_Start_Latitude` and every other `getPositionSafe()`-fed Text field, `Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Lon` included, have actually been receiving raw JS numbers all along — and that's been working live since `RSP_Start_Latitude` was converted 2026-08-03. That's *stronger* evidence sending a number to a Text field is safe (proven live over weeks), not weaker, so no code change was needed for `Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lon` — they were never actually at risk. The `String()` hardening added earlier today for `Latitude`/`Longitude`/`DropLocationLat`/`DropLocationLong` remains in place as extra insurance, not because it was ever required.

Corrected the misleading comment in `getRescueTicket/app/widget.html`'s `buildPayload()` (both call sites) to reflect this. `FIELDS.md` updated for `Cancel_Location_Lat`/`Cancel_Location_Lon`. Syntax-checked and packed via `zet pack`.

**Not yet live-tested**: worth re-running the exact flow that originally threw the error (TOW ticket → "Reached Drop Location") to directly confirm it no longer fires, and saving a fresh Locations-step lat/long paste to confirm the string payload writes cleanly into the now-Text fields.

## 2026-09-11 (later still) — Final Closure Page: "Task Tracker" PDF audit — fields added, removed, and 2 real bugs diagnosed

User provided a 33-row "Task Tracker" PDF auditing exactly what should/shouldn't show on the Final Closure page (per-row: current Closure-page state, and where it belongs). Cross-referenced every row against the actual `closureFieldGroups()`/`CLOSURE_FIELDS`/`DISTANCE_METRICS_FIELDS`/`FIELD_ACTIVITY_LOG_FIELDS` code before changing anything. 3 rows were genuinely ambiguous against the real schema — asked rather than guessed (AskUserQuestion): no confirmed "Vendor ID" field exists anywhere in this app (skip, don't invent one); "Vendor Distance" vs "RSP Start Location to Breakdown" are different data (static fleet base vs. live Accept-time GPS) — keep both; "Ozonetel Call Data" has no per-ticket linkage built yet (skip, flag as a follow-up).

### New mechanism: `closureHide:true`

Several rows say "REMOVE FROM CLOSURE PAGE" / "not needed [here]" for a field that must stay fully live on its own wizard step (e.g. Payment_received is still needed on the actual Final Payment screen, just not duplicated into the Closure summary). No existing flag did this — `when:` controls real visibility everywhere, `ephemeral:true` controls save-payload inclusion. Added `closureHide:true`, checked in 3 places: `grp()` (STAGES-based groups) and both DISTANCE_METRICS_FIELDS'/FIELD_ACTIVITY_LOG_FIELDS' own inline filters. A field flagged this way renders normally on its own live step; only the Closure summary's own row is suppressed.

## 2026-09-11 (later still) — Payment links now show their GST breakdown; confirmed the invoice side already did this correctly

User confirmed Booking Fee/Remaining Fee payment links are working, then gave an explicit spec: both are GST-inclusive amounts (`Y` and `X−Y`), the final invoice is for the full `X`, and its GST is **back-calculated** from `X` (`Customer Service Fee + 18% GST = Total Fee`) — asked for the payment link generation to "include the GST part."

**Checked first, not assumed**: fetched Zoho Payments' own "Create Payment Link" API docs directly. Confirmed it has **no tax/GST/line-item fields at all** — `amount` and `description` (both plain strings) are the only content fields; everything else is delivery/notification config. So "including the GST part" on a payment link can only mean showing the breakdown as readable text in `description` — there's no separate field for it to go in.

**Built**: new `paymentLinkDescriptionWithGst(amount, baseLabel)`, reusing `computeGstBreakup()` completely as-is (same back-calculation already proven for the final invoice) to turn e.g. `"RSR - Battery Jumpstart"` into `"RSR - Battery Jumpstart — Service Charges ₹423.73 + CGST(9%) ₹38.14 + SGST(9%) ₹38.14 = ₹500.00 (incl. GST)"`. Wired into both `generateZohoPaymentLink()` call sites (`Send_Payment_Link`/Booking Fee and `Send_Payment_Link1`/Remaining Fee) — **the amount actually charged is completely unchanged** (still the exact `Booking_Fee`/`Remaining_Fee` value), only the description text shown on the Zoho Payments checkout page now breaks it down.

**Confirmed already correct, no change needed**: the final invoice's own GST math. `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` (now live via the Final Closure "Generate Invoice" button) sends the line item's `rate` as `serviceChargesAmt` (= amount ÷ 1.18, the back-calculated base) and applies the real `GST18` Tax Group (CGST 9% + SGST 9%) on top of THAT — Zoho Books reconstructs exactly `X` as the invoice total, matching "Customer Service Fee + 18% GST = Total Fee" precisely. One thing worth knowing, not touched: that same file has an old (2026-09-08) rule forcing any reference number containing `"-BF"` to be tax-**exempt** — dead code today, since the live "Generate Invoice" button always sends a blank reference suffix, but flagging it in case `onDemandInvoicing()` is ever reconnected to the Booking Fee flow directly, where it would contradict this new "Booking Fee is GST-inclusive" spec.

**Needs redeploying**: `getRescueTicket` (client-side JS only — no `.deluge` changes, since Zoho Payments' own API has nothing new to send). **Not yet live-tested**: worth sending one real Booking Fee link and checking the Zoho Payments checkout page actually shows the new description text (no confirmed max length on that field, so also worth confirming it isn't truncated).

## 2026-09-11 (later still) — Real bug fixed: WhatsApp message showed "Rs. 200/-: 2000" with no link at all

User reported the exact live message: *"...please use the following link to pay the booking fee of Rs. 200/-: 2000. The remaining amount can be paid at service completion."* — `{{2}}` rendered as a bare `Total_Service_Fee` number with the link missing entirely, then asked for the complete flow to be verified/hardened per an 8-point spec.

**Root cause found**: both `Send_Payment_Link` (Booking Fee) and `Send_Payment_Link1` (Remaining Fee) built `customerLink` as `customerPageUrl || invoiceUrl` — if BOTH `buildCustomerPageUrl()` and that call's own `generateZohoPaymentLink()` result came back empty (e.g. a transient Zoho Payments failure on that one send), `customerLink` silently resolved to `""`, and `param2` — built as `String(Total_Service_Fee) + (customerLink ? " - CustomerPage : "+customerLink : "")` — silently dropped its whole second half, leaving just the bare number. The message still sent successfully (no error anywhere), just missing its own link, which is exactly the reported symptom.

**Fixed, matching the user's own 8-point spec**:
1. **GST-inclusive amount into the payment link** — already correct (confirmed, not changed): `generateZohoPaymentLink(r, Number(r.Booking_Fee)||0, ...)` sends `Booking_Fee` exactly as entered, which per the user's own spec IS the GST-inclusive figure already — no separate GST math needed on the amount itself, only on the description text (see the entry just above).
2. **GST included before/while calling the payment API** — same point as #1; confirmed the amount sent is the unmodified GST-inclusive `Booking_Fee`/`Remaining_Fee`, nothing recalculates or strips GST out of it before the call.
3. **URL saved to `Zoho_Payment_URL`** — already correct (confirmed, not changed): `generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge` writes `rec.Zoho_Payment_URL`/`rec.Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining` directly, synchronously, before the Custom API call even returns to the widget.
4. **WhatsApp message carries the Customer Page link, never the raw payment link** — **the actual fix**: `customerLink` is now ALWAYS `customerPageUrl` specifically; the `|| invoiceUrl` fallback is gone from both flows, so the raw Zoho Payments checkout URL can no longer reach the customer directly.
5. **Customer Page shows the correct GST-inclusive fee** — already correct (confirmed, not changed): `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` returns `amount = rec.Booking_Fee` as-is, and the widget's own `bookingamount` URL param (already sent by both Send buttons) overrides it with the exact same value at send time.
6. **Pay Now opens the saved Zoho payment link** — already correct (confirmed, not changed): `getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge` returns `paymentUrl = rec.Zoho_Payment_URL` as `invoiceUrl`, and the Customer Page's own Pay Now button (`renderBookingOrRemainingPayment()`) links straight to `info.invoiceUrl`.
7. **No more "Rs. 200/-: 2000"-style garbling** — the actual root cause (§4 above): if `customerPageUrl` can't be built for any reason, the send now **aborts entirely** with a clear toast ("...payment link was saved, but the WhatsApp message was NOT sent...") instead of silently mailing a message with the link missing. Better no message than a wrong one.
8. **recordId/paymentMode/fee/URL all correctly scoped per ticket** — already correct (confirmed, not changed): `generateZohoPaymentLink()` sends `recordId: record.ID`, and `paymentUrlField` (`"Zoho_Payment_URL"` vs `"Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining"`) is passed per-call, so Booking Fee and Remaining Fee never write over each other's link.

**Needs redeploying**: `getRescueTicket` (client-side JS only). **Not yet live-tested** — worth sending one more real Booking Fee link now to confirm the message reads cleanly end-to-end, and (harder to reproduce deliberately) confirming the new abort-with-toast path if `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL` or `record.ID` is ever actually missing.

## 2026-09-12 — Full-application audit: 5 parallel code-verified reviews across every widget, 3 real bugs fixed here

User requested a complete verification pass across the whole application — every requirement, what's done/partial/missing, a full test-case checklist, and any conflicts/gaps — explicitly not to be trusted without checking the actual code. Ran 5 parallel audits (this widget's Create→Assignment flow; this widget's Acceptance→Closure + all invoicing/GST/payment-link logic; the 3 field apps; customerPage/serviceRequest; the 4 secondary widgets), each independently verifying every README/FIELDS.md claim against the live code rather than trusting the changelog. Full consolidated report delivered to the user directly; this entry covers only the real bugs found and fixed **in this file**.

**Bug 1 — Remaining Fee receipt photo had no mandatory-photo gate at all.** `Upload_Photo_of_receipt` (`payment` stage) had no `reqFile`, unlike its Booking Fee counterpart (`Image_Upload`, `quote` stage), despite `FIELDS.md` explicitly documenting the same "mandatory unless paid via Payment Gateway" rule for both. An agent in Agent-Entering mode could reach `RSP CLOSED` with zero proof of payment collected. **Fixed**: added `reqFile:(s)=> !!s.Payment_Method_Final && s.Payment_Method_Final!=="Payment Gateway"`, mirroring `Image_Upload`'s own exact pattern.

**Bug 2 — `Send_Payment_Link1` (Remaining Fee) still sent a stale 3rd WhatsApp template parameter.** `Send_Payment_Link` (Booking Fee) was fixed on 2026-08-08 to send exactly 2 params after a live-confirmed WhatsApp rejection ("(#132018) There's an issue with the parameters in your template") proved the approved `booking_fees_link` template only has 2 placeholders — but `Send_Payment_Link1`'s own identical call was never updated to match, and had been quietly sending the same shape that's already confirmed broken for this exact template. **Fixed**: dropped the 3rd param (`customerLink`), now matches `Send_Payment_Link` exactly.

**Also found, not touched — flagged for a decision**: the `payment` stage has no `canAdvance` gate at all, so even with Bug 1 fixed, nothing stops a ticket reaching `RSP CLOSED` with `Payment_received` still "No." And `computeClosureTSF()`/`saveClosure()` have no upper-bound check on `Refund_Amount` against confirmed-paid fees — a refund larger than what was actually collected silently produces a negative `Total_Service_Fee`. Both are workflow-behavior decisions (not obvious bugs to blind-fix), left for the user to confirm before changing.

**Doc fixes** (this file's own §2 STAGES table, and `FIELDS.md`): flagged the STAGES table's `viewOnly:true` claim as stale (removed from code 2026-08-07); corrected `FIELDS.md`'s `Schedule_Date`/`Service_Time` two-field row (real schema is a single `Schedule_Date_Time` since 2026-08-05) and its `Client_Charge` "not yet wired" claim (it's fully wired, with its own 2026-08-25 bug fix).

**Needs redeploying**: `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` (re-packed). **Not yet live-tested** — worth confirming the Remaining Fee receipt-photo gate actually blocks a save when missing, and sending one Remaining Fee WhatsApp link to confirm it now delivers cleanly with 2 params.

## 2026-09-13 — Real bug fixed: Vendor Accounting showed "(no vendor)" on a ticket that genuinely has an assigned vendor

User-reported live, screenshot: `RSID590002` — `RSR · Maruti Suzuki Brezza`, a case with a real assigned vendor — showing `(no vendor)` in the Vendor Accounting (Operations Approval) list.

**Root cause**: `vaVendorDisplayName()` matched `Assigned_Vendor` against `OPTIONS.vendors` by `o.id` only (a clean Lookup object's own record id, via `unwrapLookup()`). `vendorRawForTicket()` — a few lines above this same function, added for the identical reason — already needs a second fallback match by `o.v` (the vendor's own label/name), because `Assigned_Vendor` isn't always written as a clean Lookup object; on some tickets it's a bare vendor-name string (see this file's own "Lookup write-shape revert" history), which `unwrapLookup()` then passes through unchanged. That string never matches any `o.id`, so the vendor lookup silently fell through to `"(no vendor)"` even though the ticket has a real, working vendor assignment. Fixed `vaVendorDisplayName()` to use the exact same id-first/label-fallback pattern `vendorRawForTicket()` already uses, right next to it. Tickets that were already matching by `o.id` are unaffected; this only adds a second chance to match, never removes the first.

Also fixed the same day, separately: [ACCESS.md](../ACCESS.md) was stale about `Vehicle_Master_Report`/`Vehicle_Issue_Report` access for `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` (user-reported live: a vendor's own dashboard showed a raw Zoho record id instead of the vehicle name) — corrected there, not here, since it's a cross-widget permissions doc, not this widget's own code.

**Needs redeploying**: `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` (re-packed).

This is that guard working exactly as designed — it's this file's own already-documented, recurring gotcha (see the comment right above `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL`): **republishing the Customer Page in Zoho Creator rotates its permalink token every time**, and this constant never updates itself. This is the third time it's happened (first two: 2026-09-08, 2026-09-09).

User supplied the new live permalink directly; `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL` updated to match. **Any WhatsApp payment-link message already sent before this fix still has a dead link** — per the constant's own comment, that's expected and unavoidable; the fix only takes effect on a fresh "Send Payment Link" click after this new zip is redeployed.

If this recurs a 4th time, the actual, durable fix is worth considering: a Custom API or a small Deluge lookup that reads the Customer Page's current permalink from Zoho directly at send-time, instead of a hardcoded JS constant that has now gone stale three times — flagging this as a real, recurring operational cost, not proposing it unprompted.

User provided exact expected wording for both messages and reported neither is being received. Compared each line-by-line against the actual code rather than guessing at either.

**Real bug fixed — phone number could get a doubled country code.** See the dedicated entry (search this file for `formatIndianPhone`) — this was checked specifically because the user's own list called out "customer phone number and country code," and it's a genuine, previously-unnoticed asymmetry between this function and its neighbor `isValidIndianPhoneDigits()`. Fixed identically across all 6 widgets that carry a copy of it.

**"Request Breakdown Location" — already matches the spec, as far as the code can confirm.** `Send_Link_For_The_Breakdown_Location`'s existing `sendWhatsAppTemplate(r.Phone_Number1, "location", [customerNameWithPageLink(r, "location", {locationType:"breakdown"})])` already produces exactly `"<Customer_Name> - <link with recordId, mode=location, locationType=breakdown>"` as its one parameter — which lines up with the user's own expected text ("please click this secure link: test SY - [link]..."). No code change made here; if this message genuinely isn't arriving, the most likely remaining causes are outside what this repo can see or fix directly: (1) `CUSTOMER_PAGE_BASE_URL` was stale until the fix earlier this same day (see the entry above) — not yet confirmed redeployed, which would degrade this message to name-only (no link), not stop it outright; (2) the real, approved WhatsApp template named `"location"` may not exist/may differ from what's assumed — its exact body text has never been independently confirmed until the user's own message just now, which is the first real confirmation this project has had; (3) a previously-flagged, never-resolved Custom API permission error (`code:10020, "This Custom API can be invoked only from widgets in a portal"`) logged once before against the sibling `"booking_fees_link"` template — worth checking whether the same error now shows for `"location"` too, via this action's own `.then(function(ok){ if(!ok) toast(...) })` — if that toast fires, the failure is real and needs the Custom API's own Zoho execution log to see why; if no toast fires and the message still never arrives, the failure is happening entirely downstream of this widget (Zoho → WhatsApp/Meta), invisible to anything in this repo.

**"Send Payment Link & Request Location" — the expected wording doesn't match what this specific button currently sends, but it DOES match a different button's output exactly.** The user's spec (`mode=remaining_payment`, wording "`<amount> - Pay now: <link>`") is byte-for-byte what `Send_Payment_Link1` (the Final Payment stage's own "Send payment link" button, Remaining Fee) already sends today — not what `Send_Payment_Link` (this Quote-stage button, Booking Fee) sends, which currently uses `mode=booking_payment` and "`- CustomerPage : <link>`" wording instead. These two modes are NOT interchangeable — `customerPage`'s `booking_payment` branch reads `Booking_Fee`/`Zoho_Payment_URL`/`Payment_Status`, while `remaining_payment` reads `Remaining_Fee`/`Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining`/`Payment_received` (see `customerPage/custom-apis/getCustomerTicketInfo.deluge`). Switching this button's mode to `remaining_payment` without also switching what it reads would show the customer the wrong amount/status on a link meant to collect the Booking Fee — a real regression, not a fix — so this was deliberately NOT changed without confirming which button the user actually meant. Asked the user directly rather than guessing. **User confirmed**: they were describing `Send_Payment_Link1`, which already matches — no code change needed for the mode/wording question.

## 2026-09-13 (later) — Real gap fixed: the generated payment link wasn't showing up on the agent's own screen until the ticket was reopened

Re-verifying soft-test item #4/#7 ("the generated payment link must be clearly displayed on the interface," the same requirement `__PaymentLinkDisplay_BF`/`__PaymentLinkDisplay_RF` were built for on 2026-09-10) turned up a real, previously-missed piece: those two navlinks correctly read `Zoho_Payment_URL`/`Zoho_Payment_URL_Remaining` off `state.record`, and `generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge` correctly saves that field server-side — but neither `Send_Payment_Link`'s nor `Send_Payment_Link1`'s own click handler ever wrote the freshly-generated URL back into the widget's own in-memory `state.record`, or re-rendered afterward. So immediately after a successful "Send Payment Link" click, the link was already real and valid in Zoho, but the agent's own screen kept showing "No payment link yet" until they left the ticket and reopened it (which triggers a fresh fetch).

**Fixed** in both actions: the moment `invoiceUrl` comes back truthy, it's written onto `r`/`state.record` (the same object — `f.action(state.record)`, see `buildField()`'s own call site) and `renderWizard()` is called immediately — independent of whether the WhatsApp send that follows succeeds, fails, or aborts, since the payment link itself is already real the moment it's generated. Nothing about the WhatsApp-sending logic, the Custom API call, or either navlink's own field definition was touched.

**Needs redeploying**: `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` (re-packed). **Not yet live-tested** — worth confirming the "💳 Open Payment Link" row appears immediately after clicking Send Payment Link, with no reload needed.

## 2026-09-13 (later still) — Real bug fixed: "NAVIGATE to Breakdown Location" still unresponsive on Android — the 2026-08-21 fix never actually ran

User-reported live, repeating soft-test item #12 verbatim ("the 'NAVIGATE to Breakdown Location' button is currently unresponsive on Android devices") — the exact symptom `buildNavUrl()`'s Android-aware `geo:` URI was built to fix back on 2026-08-21. Traced why the existing fix wasn't taking effect: every nav-link call site checks the stored `Break_Down_Location1` field **first**, only falling back to `buildNavUrl()` when that field is empty — but `Break_Down_Location1`'s own real shape has never been confirmed (FIELDS.md: "Single Line or Formula... not confirmed whether this is a real formula field or something else"). Whether it's a plain `maps.google.com` link (exactly the shape already found unreliable on Android from inside a webview) or something else, using it ahead of `buildNavUrl()` bypasses the Android fix completely on any ticket where it's populated — which is most real tickets, so the original fix was effectively dead code in practice.

**Fixed**: new `resolveNavHref(storedLink, lat, lon)` helper, right next to `buildNavUrl()` — on Android, it now always prefers building a `geo:` URI from live coordinates when they're available, falling back to the stored link only if coordinates aren't. **iOS/desktop behavior is completely unchanged** — the stored link is still preferred there first, exactly as before, since that platform was never reported broken. Same fix applied identically to `vendorTicket`/`technicianTicket`/`driverTicket` (every widget that has its own `buildNavUrl()`/nav-link call sites) — this file's own generic `navlink` field-type handling (used by `Navigation_Link`/`Navigate_To_Breakdown_Link`) also updated to match.

**Needs redeploying**: `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` (re-packed). **Not yet live-tested on an actual Android device** — the fix is a straightforward priority reorder, but the original 2026-08-21 `geo:` URI itself was flagged the same way and never independently confirmed live either; worth testing both.

## 2026-09-14 — Real Ozonetel calling added back to the Create step's Phone Number field, alongside the existing tel: link

User reported the "📞 Call" button "not initiating a call," with a screenshot showing DevTools open and `tel:+91...` logged as a blocked network request — classic signature of testing a plain `tel:` link from a desktop browser (which does nothing without an OS-registered phone handler). Before touching anything, asked directly whether this was actually about the plain click-to-dial link (a testing-environment limitation, not a bug) or about wanting real Ozonetel calling on this exact button — **user confirmed the latter**: a genuine feature request, not a bug fix.

**What was built**: `placeVoiceCallbackFromTicket()`/`checkVoiceCallbackStatusFromTicket()`, ported verbatim from the standalone `callCenter` widget's own proven `placeVoiceCallback()`/`checkVoiceCallbackStatus()` — same two Custom APIs (`getVoiceCallbackDetails`/`getCallbackStatus`, `workspace_name:"getrescued"`), same payload shape, same defensive response parsing (including reading both `call_result`/`disconnect_reason` for Outcome, since `callCenter`'s own README flags that field name as never independently confirmed). This is the exact same Ozonetel mechanism that was **deliberately moved out of this widget** into `callCenter` on 2026-09-10 ("Replace the inline button," not "keep both") — now restored here specifically, per this explicit new request, not a reversal made unprompted.

**Scope, deliberately kept narrow**: a "📲 Call via System" link now sits right next to the existing "📞 Call" (plain `tel:`) link on the Create step's Phone Number field — **purely additive, the existing link is completely untouched**. New optional field flag `f.ozonetelCall` (only `Phone_Number1` sets it) gates this — every other `t:"tel"` field in the app is unaffected. Clicking it places a real call via Ozonetel, toasts the outcome, and auto-checks status once ~4 seconds later (same UX timing `callCenter` already uses) — there's no persistent "Current Call" panel and no `Call_Log` write here, since that form doesn't even exist in Zoho yet (see `callCenter/README.md`) and depending on it from a second place for no real benefit wasn't worth the extra coupling.
