**Needs redeploying**: `dist/getRescueTicket.zip` (re-packed). **Not yet live-tested** — please confirm end-to-end: click "📲 Call via System" on a real ticket, confirm a real Ozonetel call actually reaches the phone number, and check the console for the exact request/response if it doesn't (the same diagnostic logging `callCenter` already relies on is in place here too).

## 2026-09-14 (later) — ONE final invoice per ticket, with both collected payments recorded against it

User's own framing: *"we are taking booking fee initially from customer and then after ticket closure we are taking remaining payment... I want one final invoice per ticket which will contain all fees."*

**Where this already stood** (worth knowing before reading the change): neither payment link creates a Books invoice any more — `onDemandInvoicing()` was disconnected from `Send_Payment_Link`/`Send_Payment_Link1` on 2026-09-10, and the old 2026-09-07 "split into two invoices" design died with it. The single Books invoice per ticket already existed, raised at closure by the **Generate Invoice** button, for the final total, with a duplicate guard (`Zoho_Books_Invoice_ID` already set → refuses). So the architecture was already "one invoice"; the gap was that **the invoice didn't know the customer had already paid** — both payments went through standalone Zoho Payments links attached to no invoice, so a fully-paid ticket's invoice still showed the whole amount outstanding.

**Approach chosen** (recommended, then user-confirmed): leave both payment links completely untouched, keep the one invoice at closure, and record the collected payments against it. Deliberately NOT the alternative designs — no invoice raised up front at quote time (the final amount isn't known yet, and refunds/extras would mean editing an invoice that already has payments applied), and no Books retainer/advance flow (correct accounting-wise, but it drags GST-on-advance receipt-voucher handling in, which is the user's CA's call, not this app's).

**Deluge** (`custom-apis/onDemandInvoicing.deluge`): 4 new parameters (`bookingFeeAmount`, `bookingFeeRef`, `remainingFeeAmount`, `remainingFeeRef`) and a new STEP 3 that records each collected fee against the just-created invoice via Books' `/customerpayments`, each in its own try/catch so a payment-recording failure can never take down the invoice itself. `bestUrl` now prefers the invoice document over a "pay now" link once payments have been recorded (a settled invoice handing back a payment link was both useless and misleading) — unchanged for the zero-payments case. Response now carries `payments_recorded` (0/1/2) so the widget/console can see what actually landed without opening Books.

**Widget**: the `onDemandInvoicing()` JS wrapper now sends those 4 values — **ON DEMAND only** (user decision: "Not for B2B"), only for fees that are genuinely confirmed paid (reusing `bookingFeeConfirmedPaid()`/`remainingFeeConfirmedPaid()`, the same two signals `computeClosureTSF()` itself is built from), and **capped so the two can never exceed the invoice total**: a refund at closure makes the final invoice smaller than the sum collected, and Books rejects over-application. Booking fee applies first, remaining takes what's left, and any genuine excess is logged for the agent to reconcile in Books rather than guessed at.

**Gap filled** (user: "Work accordingly and fill the gap"): `onDemandInvoiceEligibility()` used to require `Closure_Status==="Completed"` for every client — so a ticket cancelled *after* the customer had already paid a booking fee produced no invoice at all, despite real money having been collected. ON DEMAND now accepts any fee-bearing closure (Completed / Cancelled / Cancelled - Billable — the same set `closureCountsAsFeeBearing()` already governs), invoicing `finalTsf`, which already nets refunds out. Self-correcting at the edges: a fully-refunded cancellation nets to 0 and is still filtered out by the existing `amount>0` check, and "Not Converted" can't reach here at all since `saveClosure()` refuses that status once any fee is paid. **B2B eligibility is deliberately unchanged** — still Completed-only, still `Rescue_Charge`.

**⚠ Needed in Zoho before this works at all**: the 4 new parameters must be **registered in the `onDemandInvoicing` Custom API's own setup screen**, not just pasted into the script — a parameter that exists only in the Deluge signature silently arrives as `null` no matter what the widget sends. This exact trap already bit this file once before (see `applyTax`'s own "belt-and-braces fallback" comment). If they're not registered, payment recording simply no-ops and the invoice is created exactly as it is today — it fails safe, but it fails.

**⚠ New/unverified API surface**: Books' `/customerpayments` endpoint and its payload shape have no prior confirmed use anywhere in this project. `payment_mode` is sent as `"other"` (these arrive via UPI/card through Zoho Payments, so neither `"cash"` nor `"creditcard"` is strictly right) — if Books rejects the call, that's the first thing to adjust, and the raw response is logged via `info` in the Custom API's own execution log.

**Deluge constraint learned the hard way here (2026-09-14, confirmed live)**: a Custom API script's **line 1 must be the function signature** — a comment block placed above it is rejected outright by Zoho's own editor with `Expected 'VOID','INT','FLOAT','STRING','DATE','BOOL','MAP' or 'LIST' but found '//...'`. The header note for these new parameters was moved inside the function body as a result. Worth remembering for every other `.deluge` file in this repo: header comments go *after* the opening brace, never before the declaration.

**Needs**: re-pasting `onDemandInvoicing.deluge` into its Custom API **and registering the 4 new parameters**, then redeploying `dist/getRescueTicket.zip`. **Not yet live-tested** — test a full ON DEMAND ticket end to end (booking fee paid → remaining fee paid → close → Generate Invoice) and confirm the resulting Books invoice shows Total, both payments, and Balance ₹0.

## 2026-09-14 (later still) — New: `createTicket.deluge`, a client-facing ticket-intake Custom API

User request: *"i want to create custom api and give to the client and there params is Customer Name, Mobile, Email, Vehicle, Issue."* A B2B partner calls this to raise a Get-Rescue ticket programmatically; the ticket lands on the agent dashboard and flows through the same wizard as any agent- or website-created ticket.

**Built by modelling `serviceRequest/custom-apis/createServiceRequest.deluge`** (the public website form's own intake API) — same `insert into Create_Case` shape, same `Status:"SERVICE FEE QUOTE"` entry point, same `Vehicle_Status:"ONROAD"` default, same phone mirroring into BOTH `Phone_Number1` and the real required `Phone_Number`, same `"RSID"+last-6` Case_ID generation, same two-step fetch-then-write follow-up. No new/unverified Deluge syntax anywhere in it.

**The one real design difference**: `createServiceRequest` receives Zoho record *IDs* (its own widget picks them from live dropdowns). An external client can't know Zoho's internal IDs, so this API takes **Vehicle and Issue as plain names** and resolves them here — matched **case-insensitively** by lowercasing and comparing in a loop, rather than a direct criteria match, because Deluge criteria matching is case-SENSITIVE and silently returns nothing on a casing mismatch (already a confirmed live trap in this project — see `vendorTicket`'s `resolveVendorRecord()`). `Issue` accepts a comma-separated list, since `Vehicle_Issue` is a multi-select Lookup. `Service_Type` is **derived**, never sent by the caller — same rule everything else uses (any matched issue whose `Issue_Type` contains "TOW" ⇒ `TOW`, else `RSR`).

**`clientName` was briefly added as an optional 6th parameter, then removed at explicit user request** (2026-09-14, after a successful live test: *"do not take clientname"*). The API is back to exactly the 5 requested parameters. **Consequence, recorded deliberately**: a ticket raised through this API is created with **no `Client` set**, and an agent must fill it in before the fee/invoicing rules can apply correctly — `Client_Charge`/`Rescue_Charge` at Quote and the whole ON-DEMAND-vs-B2B split in `onDemandInvoiceEligibility()` at Closure all key off that field. It deliberately does NOT default to `"ON DEMAND"`, which would silently mark a partner's ticket as a walk-in and invoice it on entirely the wrong rules. This was flagged before removal and the trade-off accepted; if API-raised tickets ever start slipping through Closure with the wrong billing, an unset `Client` is the first thing to check.

**`Lead_Source` is written as `"App"`** — not because that's a perfect description, but because `Lead_Source` is a dropdown whose confirmed options are `Call`/`Website`/`WhatsApp`/`App` (FIELDS.md) and a value outside that list is rejected by Zoho outright. If a dedicated `"API"`/`"Client API"` option is added to that dropdown, change the single `Lead_Source = "App"` line to match; nothing else depends on it.

**⚠ Register all 6 parameters** in the Custom API's own setup screen, not just in the signature — an unregistered parameter silently arrives as `null` regardless of what the caller sends (the live incident that proved this is documented on `onDemandInvoicing.deluge`'s own `applyTax` fallback).

**Extended same day**: `vehicle` and `issue` each accept **either a Zoho record ID or a name** — added after a live test sent a Vehicle ID (`448881000000221402`) and was rejected by the name-only match. ID is tried first via try/catch (`.toLong()` genuinely throws on non-numeric text in Deluge, and this project has no confirmed-safe regex idiom to digit-test with instead), falling through to the case-insensitive name match otherwise. IDs and names can even be mixed within the comma-separated `issue` list.

**Real bug fixed same day, live**: the first working call failed with `Error on inserting value for field 'Vehicle_Issue'. Given value 'null' doesn't exist in 'Vehicle_Issue' form`. Root cause: the resolved IDs were being collected as `record.ID` references rather than plain longs, and a list of those serialises to `null` when bound to a multi-select Lookup on insert. `createServiceRequest.deluge` had the correct pattern all along (`issueIdTrim.toLong()`) — this deviated from it for no reason. All three lookups now normalise to plain longs (`.toString().toLong()` on the name-matched path, the already-long parsed value on the ID path). The single-value `Vehicle` field happened to tolerate the reference form, which is why only `Vehicle_Issue` errored — it was normalised anyway rather than left working by luck. **Confirmed working live afterwards** (`RSID585463`, `serviceType:"RSR"`).

**⚠ This file is deliberately comment-free**, unlike every other `.deluge` in this repo — explicit user request, 2026-09-14, since it's handed to an external client. All the reasoning that would normally live in its header (why names are matched case-insensitively, why ID detection uses try/catch, why `Lead_Source` is `"App"`, why `clientName` is optional-but-important) is captured in this README entry instead — don't re-add it to the file. Note the three inner `catch` blocks each carry a harmless `xFound = false;` rather than being empty: stripping their comments would otherwise have left empty catch bodies, which aren't confirmed valid in Deluge.

**Not yet live-tested** — the file is structurally validated (line 1 is the signature, braces/parens/brackets balanced, 4 try/catch pairs) but has never been run. Test each failure path too, not just the happy one: unknown vehicle, unknown issue, missing client, and a TOW issue (confirm `serviceType` comes back `TOW`).

## 2026-09-14 (later still) — New: `paymentWebhook.deluge`, Zoho Payments → ticket payment sync

User request: *"i want to set webhook when payment is done so that ticket is updated"*, scope then narrowed by the user to *"just now mark Paid when booking fee is paid."* Subscribe it to the **`payment_link.paid`** event only (not All Events — payouts/refunds would otherwise hit the same endpoint as noise).

**Scope, deliberately minimal**: the only thing it ever writes is `Payment_Status = "PAID"` on one `Create_Case` record, and only for a **Booking Fee** payment. It does not touch the Remaining Fee, stamps no timestamps, and never changes `Status`. Closing a ticket stays a human action through the vendor app — a webhook silently closing a case would bypass that screen's own mandatory receipt-photo gate, which was hardened earlier the same week. Remaining Fee handling is deliberately left out until asked for.

**Why it parses the payload the way it does** (this is the part the file no longer explains itself — see the comment-free note below): the exact JSON shape Zoho Payments sends has **not been confirmed**, and neither has whether a Creator Custom API binds a third-party webhook's nested body to declared parameters at all. Rather than guess field paths — the exact class of guess that cost round-trips three times on 2026-09-13/14 (the `update … where` statement, `/customerpayments`, `record.ID` vs long) — it joins whatever *did* arrive into one text blob and searches it for the two markers **this app itself stamps on every payment link** in `generateZohoPaymentLink.deluge`: a `meta_data` entry keyed `recordId` holding the raw `Create_Case` ID, and a `reference_id` ending `-BF`. Extraction uses only string operations with confirmed prior use in this project (`trim`/`contains`/`toList`/`length`, plus `toLong` in a try/catch) — no `.indexOf`/`.replaceAll`/`.toMap`, none of which appear anywhere in this repo's `.deluge` files.

**Safety properties, since this writes to a money-adjacent field**: it acts only when *every* check passes — a recordId was extracted, it resolves to a real record, and the payload is clearly a Booking Fee link. Anything ambiguous is logged and ignored rather than guessed. Re-delivery is harmless (setting `PAID` twice equals setting it once), and it always returns a plain success body so Zoho Payments never retries a delivery it has already deliberately ignored.

**⚠ Comment-free file**, same as `createTicket.deluge` — explicit user request; the reasoning lives here instead, don't re-add it to the script.

**Not yet live-tested.** Three outcomes to read from the Custom API's own execution log after the first real payment: ticket flips to PAID (done); all three params log `(null)` (the body doesn't bind to a Custom API at all — needs a different receiver, e.g. Zoho Flow or a Creator incoming-webhook workflow); or params populated but no recordId found (the dump shows where the metadata actually sits, small fix).

**⚠ Open security question, raised but not yet resolved**: the endpoint is publicly reachable via its publickey, so anyone who learns the URL could POST a fake `payment_link.paid` and mark tickets paid without money moving. Check whether Zoho Payments offers a webhook **signing secret**; if it does, verify it before the record write. If it doesn't, the handler should call Zoho Payments back with the payment ID to confirm the payment independently before trusting the callback.
